**Step-curve faction-asymmetric data via faction-symmetric mechanism is the right shape for late-war calibration arc fixes (2026-05-04 LANE-NIGHTSHIFT-RECONSTITUTION-POLICY-REVIEW):** When the Gap 2 verification trace identified VRS reconstitution outpacing battle attrition as the upstream growth term overriding the casualty-driven officer_quality decay, the fix shape question was: invent a new asymmetric mechanism, or tune existing parameter data? **The right answer is data-only: extend the existing `lookupStepCurve(...)` predicate (already used by RBiH + HRHB for time-bounded reinforcement multipliers) to cover VRS as well, with an asymmetric step curve.** RS gets 4-band 1.0× (early war) → 0.85× (mid war) → 0.65× (late war) → 0.45× (final phase); HRHB gets 4-band similarly; RBiH unchanged. **Mechanism is symmetric (same `lookupStepCurve` predicate); data parameters drive the asymmetric arc.** This pattern preserves: (1) faction-agnostic CODE — no `if (faction === 'RS') {...}` branches; (2) calibration explainability — the parameter values are auditable; (3) extensibility — adding 4th band, 5th band, or different boundaries is a JSON edit, not a code change. **Durable rule: late-war calibration arcs (officer_quality, personnel, morale, equipment_quality, supply_baseline) fix via faction-asymmetric step-curve data on a faction-symmetric mechanism. Hardcoded asymmetric mechanisms accrue maintenance debt; data-driven asymmetry is auditable.** Reference: `src/state/formation_constants.ts:getFactionReinforcementMult` + `data/scenarios/timelines/apr1992.json:reinforcement_mult`. Applied in `[2026-05-04] feat(calibration): VRS+HRHB reinforcement step-curve restoration`.

**Substrate-then-content sequencing prevents lane confusion + smoke-attribution ambiguity (2026-05-04 Wave 3 + Wave 4 chain):** Mission D event #11 was deferred when its `equipment_quality_modifier` effect kind didn't exist. The right sequencing: (1) substrate-first lane creates the effect kind with no-op default behavior (Wave 3 commit `658241df`); (2) follow-up content lane authors events that consume the substrate (Wave 4 commit `013bd633` event #4 `csq_patron_equipment_delivery_confirmed`). **Combining substrate + content in one lane creates two risks: (a) smoke-attribution ambiguity — if hash drifts, you can't tell whether the substrate or the event triggered it; (b) review surface inflation — substrate review is mechanism-correctness, event review is faction-symmetry/canon-fit; reviewers can't apply both lenses cleanly to a single commit.** Durable rule: when a content lane needs new substrate, ship substrate-first as its own lane with no-op default + hash-identity gate, THEN ship content lane that consumes it (also hash-identity-gated when the event won't fire in smoke window). Multiple events can consume the same substrate over many lanes. Reference: PROJECT_LEDGER 2026-05-04 Wave 4 Lane B (event #4 csq_patron_equipment_delivery_confirmed consumes Wave 3's substrate). Applied in `[2026-05-04] feat(events): divergence events Wave 4`.

**When trace evidence implicates an upstream system, fix the upstream lever — don't tune the downstream multiplier (2026-05-04 LANE-NIGHTSHIFT-FORCE-QUALITY-GAP-2-VERIFICATION-TRACE):** Gap 2 verification trace investigated whether `OFFICER_CASUALTY_MULT` needed tuning to produce the expected VRS-decline / ARBiH-rise officer-quality arc. The 188w trace at t84 showed VRS officer_quality rising slightly (+0.0204) instead of falling (expected -1). The naive fix would be to bump `OFFICER_CASUALTY_MULT` for VRS. The actual finding: the casualty-driven `applyOfficerCasualtyLoss` path is wired correctly — VRS rate-of-change `+0.000246/turn` from casualty pressure is *just barely failing* to overcome the `+0.0067/turn` growth term that comes from upstream VRS reconstitution policy outpacing battle attrition (Mission G row 1: VRS personnel +753 over 188w). **Durable rule: when a metric arc is wrong, trace the rate-of-change to determine whether the proximate mechanism is wired correctly. If yes, the fix is upstream — find the source of the offending growth/decay term and address it there. Tuning the proximate multiplier in isolation is a downstream patch that won't restore the structural arc.** Pattern applies broadly: morale (drift vs combat outcome), cohesion (drift vs casualty), exhaustion (accumulation vs recovery), supply (consumption vs production), economic dimensions (decay vs event injection). Reference: `docs/40_reports/audits/20260504_FORCE_QUALITY_GAP_2_VERIFICATION.md`. Applied in `[2026-05-04] feat(audit): force-quality Gap 2 verification trace`.

**Phase 0 STOP-AND-RECOMMEND is a valid outcome when no clear hotspot dominates (2026-05-04 LANE-NIGHTSHIFT-BOT-ORDERS-PERF-WITH-GATES):** Bot-orders pipeline optimization Wave 3 dispatch ran a Phase 0 investigation (read R2-4 baseline + bot AI source files + commander loop). Found that the 562ms/turn cost is **diffused** across commander loop + briefing builder + predictor + per-brigade evaluators — no single function dominates the way `isBridgeInSubgraphOsid` did for supply-osid (Mission C A0). Candidates within scope (sort/filter cleanup, cross-call caches) collectively look like 20-100ms/turn — real-but-modest, no clear "lift one stone, reveal the algorithm" target. Spec STOP trigger explicitly authorized this: *"Phase 0 finds no obvious hotspot → STOP and report"*. **Durable rule for hot-path optimization lanes: Phase 0 must produce a NAMED single dominating function before authorizing optimization. If Phase 0 reveals diffuse cost, dispatch an instrumentation lane FIRST** (per-call-site `hrtime.bigint()` wrappers in the pipeline step) **then re-scope an optimization lane to whichever function actually dominates.** Optimizing diffuse cost without instrumentation invites (a) optimizing the wrong call site, (b) breaking determinism via memoization keys, (c) wasting gate-discipline budget on a marginal win. The pattern is: instrument → identify → optimize-with-gates → ship. Reference: PROJECT_LEDGER 2026-05-04 Wave 3 Lane B STOP-AND-ASK entry. Applied in `[2026-05-04] (no commit; lane closed deferred with instrumentation-lane recommendation)`.

**Hot-phase optimization that previously failed hash-identity ships only with G1+G2+G3 gate discipline (2026-05-04 LANE-NIGHTSHIFT-SUPPLY-OSID-A0-TARJAN-WITH-GATES):** Three prior supply-osid optimization attempts failed (Mission C Tarjan trip-session-3 rolled back; A1 region cache STOP-AND-ASK on degenerate partition; original whole-faction WeakMap cache inadequate). The 4th attempt (A0 Tarjan retry) shipped clean only because three binding pre-merge gates were enforced: **G1** property test (10,000 randomized BiH-shape graph trials asserting bridge-set equality + per-edge classification identity between legacy and new implementation, deterministic LCG seed, includes enclave-like multi-component subgraphs); **G2** production parity wrapper behind opt-in env flag (`SUPPLY_BRIDGE_PARITY_CHECK=true` re-runs legacy and asserts identity, throws with full graph dump on mismatch, default-off no production cost); **G3** hash-identity smoke against current baseline (40w `final_state_hash` MUST be byte-identical pre/post). **Durable rule for any future hot-phase optimization that has previously failed hash-identity: the algorithm choice is secondary; gate discipline is primary. Implement BOTH legacy + new in parallel during the lane, run G1 property test BEFORE shipping (red-first if needed), wire G2 parity wrapper for ongoing regression detection, run G3 smoke as the binding ship gate. If G1 catches drift, debug-fix-retest until G1 passes; if G3 drifts, ROLL BACK before commit.** This pattern is now precedent for: bot-orders pipeline optimization, sector-reconciliation cluster, any future combat-math optimization. Reference: `tests/supply_bridge_finding_property.test.ts` + `tests/supply_bridge_finding_tarjan.test.ts` + parity wrapper at `src/state/supply_state_derivation.ts:findBridgesInSubgraphOsidWithParity`. Applied in `[2026-05-04] perf(supply): replace per-edge BFS-removal with single-pass Tarjan in deriveCorridorsOsid`.

**electron-builder treats `win.sign: null` as "use default signing" not "skip signing" — `signAndEditExecutable: false` is the canonical unsigned switch (2026-05-04 LANE-NIGHTSHIFT-DRG-REGRESSION-FIX):** The DRG regression that turned Desktop Release Guard CI red for 4+ commits had a deceptively simple cause. Mission I's platform-packaging-groundwork added `"sign": null` to `package.json` build.win config alongside the existing `"signAndEditExecutable": false`, intending redundancy. In practice, electron-builder treats `null` differently than omission: `null` triggers a default sign-hook resolution path (likely Windows SignTool lookup) that interferes with asar integrity rewriting on the packaged `.exe`. The build itself succeeded but packaged Electron started without the Warroom main window ever firing `did-finish-load` for `awwv://warroom/index.html`. **Durable rule: NEVER set `win.sign` to `null` for unsigned builds; use `signAndEditExecutable: false` alone (or omit `sign` entirely).** Failure mode is silent — packaging completes, but runtime hangs. Test contract `tests/desktop_packaging_targets.test.ts` T2 now asserts `pkg.build?.win?.sign === undefined` with rationale comment. Applied in `[2026-05-04] fix(packaging): remove redundant win.sign: null`.

**React useMemo / hooks order MUST be above all early returns; otherwise the packaged Electron probe times out (2026-05-04 LANE-NIGHTSHIFT-DRG-REGRESSION-FIX antecedent):** Mission E's `codexGhosts` useMemo was placed AFTER VerdictScreen's `if (!verdict) return <FallbackGameOver />` early return. Renders without verdict skipped the useMemo; renders with verdict called it. Different numbers of hooks across renders triggers React's Rules of Hooks error and the entire VerdictScreen render throws. The `[data-awwv-endgame-surface]` marker never appeared and the packaged endgame probe timed out at 5s. **Durable rule: when adding a new hook (useMemo, useState, useEffect, useCallback) to an existing React component with early returns, ALWAYS verify the hook is above ALL early returns.** Unit tests with mocked stores don't catch this — only the packaged probe (`tools/desktop_packaged_runtime_probe.mjs`) exercises React render with the verdict gate active. Vitest mount tests like `endgame_verdict_screen_mount.test.ts` may pass against the broken case if the mock state always has a verdict. Applied in `[2026-05-04] fix(ui): hoist VerdictScreen useMemo above early returns`.

**Replay save-sequence producer must emit as separate sidecar artifact, NEVER embed in `final_save.json` (2026-05-04 LANE-NIGHTSHIFT-REPLAY-SAVE-SEQUENCE-PRODUCER):** When implementing the replay-save-sequence producer (Mission J's consumer needs `LoadedGameState.replaySaveSequence: ReadonlyArray<GameState>`), the design call is between (a) embedding the array in `final_save.json` as an optional `replay_sequence?` field, vs (b) writing a separate sidecar `replay_save_sequence.json`. The durable answer is **(b) sidecar**, for four reasons: (1) **file size**: observed 40w produces ~320 MB sequence vs 12.7 MB final_save (50× ratio); embedding would break the "small, loadable, validatable" save contract; (2) **loader compatibility**: `parseGameState`, `deserializeState`, `validateState` would all need new branches if GameState carried a nested `GameState[]`; (3) **hash invariance**: embedding would mutate `final_save.json` byte content and fail hash-identity gates; separate artifact cannot drift the canonical save hash by construction; (4) **optionality**: replay sequence is observability, not engine state; saves remain loadable when sequence is absent. **Durable rule: any future per-turn artifact (corps temporal, sector temporal, op temporal) follows the same harness-side sidecar pattern — separate emit module, separate file, separate adapter wiring with `?? undefined` defaults, single ownership boundary per topic.** Applied in `[2026-05-04] feat(replay): replay save-sequence producer + IPC + adapter wiring`.

**Q-CANON-RUPT-4 resolved as Path (d) — engine already implements canonical silence; lane is canon-clarification, not engine relaxation (2026-05-04 LANE-NIGHTSHIFT-Q-CANON-RUPT-4-PATH-D):** When facing a §6 sign-off question with binary canon-permitted paths (heuristic recording vs explicit acceptance), the durable rule is to default to **explicit acceptance**: rupture events fire only on mechanical c2 satisfaction; ahistorical campaigns where the c2 condition is not mechanically satisfied carry no rupture flag in the verdict packet. Historical findings remain in Ring 2 (essays + codex) regardless. Path (c) calendar-window heuristic recording is rejected because it: (1) violates §0 ("atrocity is a consequence, not a lever") by converting the rupture into a calendar-scripted output; (2) violates §1.5 Ring 3 by creating a non-optimizable but still calendar-asserted outcome that overrides simulated counterfactual; (3) violates §2 criterion 3 letter (BINDING discrete deterministic game-state condition); (4) sets a precedent every late-war atrocity (Markale, Ahmići, Tuzla Gate, Bijeljina, Stupni Do, Kravica) will pressure-test. ICTY/ICJ preservation is satisfied by Ring 2, not by forcing Ring 1 in counterfactual runs. The §3-compliant counterfactual register pattern is the Mission E ghost-entry shape (`predEnclaveDefended` in `dynamic_section_builder.ts`), not rupture-evaluator relaxation. **Durable rule: any future late-war §6 sign-off chain on rupture events defaults to Path (d); only deviate when canon explicitly authorizes a heuristic and the heuristic shape can be specified mechanically.** Applied in `[2026-05-04] feat(canon): Q-CANON-RUPT-4 Path (d)`.

**BiH static OSID adjacency graph is 1 connected component; naive region-partition strategies for supply optimization degenerate (2026-05-04 LANE-NIGHTSHIFT-SUPPLY-OSID-A1-REGION-CACHE STOP-AND-ASK):** When attempting to implement region-keyed cache invalidation for `supply_reachability_osid.ts`, the spec assumed a "polygon-adjacency-only static-topology connected-component" partition would yield non-trivial regions. Direct probe over `data/derived/operational/operational_contact_graph.json` falsified this: 712 OSIDs, 1 connected component. There are no enclaves disconnected at the static graph level — Goražde, Žepa, Sarajevo, Bihać are *control*-disconnected pockets, not *graph*-disconnected regions. Connected-component partition therefore yields a degenerate single-region partition equivalent to the existing whole-faction WeakMap cache. **Durable rule: BiH-specific topology constrains naive graph-partition strategies. Any future supply-osid optimization must either (a) invent a deterministic non-trivial partition (e.g., METIS-style recursive bisection with frozen partition table checked into `data/derived/` as a generated artifact, deterministic-stability-proven) — research-+-engineering, NOT S-effort; OR (b) pivot to inner-loop optimization (Tarjan single-pass O(V+E) bridge-finding) with property-test gates (G1: 10k randomized BiH-shape graphs assert bit-identical bridge set vs legacy; G2: production parity wrapper behind env flag; G3: hash-identity smoke against current baseline) before any commit.** Mission C Tarjan attempt rolled back at first attempt because property-test gates were absent — same lesson surfaces here. Applied in `[2026-05-04] (no commit; lane closed cleanly with assumption refuted)`.

**Shadow-flag default-OFF is the right merge pattern for contested §6-adjacent mechanics (2026-05-03 LANE-NIGHTSHIFT-N4 morale-collapse override):** When a behavioral mechanic is sensitive-history-adjacent (Ring 1 but reads on enclave/atrocity-adjacent state) and the canon doesn't yet have a binding sign-off for the override path, the durable pattern is: (1) ship the **observation** (state-shape additive counter that accumulates evidence each turn, faction-agnostic, deterministic); (2) gate the **action** (env flag default-false; e.g. `process.env.MORALE_OVERRIDE_ENABLED === 'true'`); (3) prove **behavioral-zero with flag OFF** via byte-stable smoke vs predecessor (state-shape diff is acceptable; behavior diff is not). This lets the counter populate saves at scenario seeds so a future ON-run can be A/B'd against an OFF-baseline at the same seed before promotion. Mirrors LANE-2026-05-02-B1-PLANNING-INVALIDATED-COOLDOWN's §8.3 (a) honest-correction shape. Canon amendment must precede the shadow-flag (Engine Invariants §6.2.4 + Systems Manual §6.4 in this case) so the override path has named canon authority even when defaulted off. Reference: `tests/morale_collapse_override.test.ts` T6 (flag-OFF no-override) + T7 (flag-ON override). Applied in `[2026-05-03] feat(combat): morale-collapse dissolution override`.

**must_hold multiplier should be pressure-responsive variable, not flat (2026-05-03 LANE-NIGHTSHIFT-ROUND2 R2-1):** Flat 1.5× must_hold budget multiplier was insufficient against 5-9× attacker ratios at high-pressure OSIDs (Ozren-pocket evidence). Flat increase to 4.0× or 6.0× over-fortifies cosmetic must_holds and starves real fronts. The durable pattern is `max(FLOOR, min(CAP, SCALE × observed_pressure))` reading the existing `zone.commitment_ratio` already on the `ZoneAssessment` parameter (no new field). Floor 2.0× prevents under-reinforcement of nominal must_holds; cap 5.0× prevents one hyper-pressured OSID from draining the corps garrison budget; 0.75× scale matches historical reinforcement (defender doesn't need full parity — terrain/entrenchment/fortifications already in engine). Faction-agnostic; defensive guard collapses NaN/Infinity/non-positive to floor. Applied in `[2026-05-03] feat(round2): six-lane parallel ship`.

**Six-lane parallel autonomous dispatch is viable for Ring 1 / no-§6 work when file-ownership is non-overlapping (2026-05-03 LANE-NIGHTSHIFT-ROUND2):** When the user authorizes ambitious scope and lanes touch disjoint file sets, the durable orchestration pattern is: (1) Phase 0 — three parallel verdict dispatches (game-designer, narrative-designer+historian, audit-historian) up front to resolve binding decisions (multiplier value, event seed list, diagnostic scope); (2) Phase 1 — six parallel implementation agents launched in a single message, each with explicit file-ownership boundaries; (3) Phase 2 — single combined smoke window covering only engine-affecting lanes (items 1+2 in this case); (4) Phase 3 — combined commit with multi-section message body for traceability rather than 6 separate commits with `git add -p` partial staging. SendMessage to a stalled agent is the right unblock — don't re-spawn. The convention of per-lane commits is sacrificed for trip-pace velocity; commit message body provides traceability instead. Pre-merge gates run in parallel (scenario-tester for hash, war-or-game for realism). Applied in `[2026-05-03] feat(round2): six-lane parallel ship` covering must_hold, events, tutorial, perf audit, damage seed, srebrenica diagnostic.

**Perf instrumentation lives in the harness and is reverted before commit (2026-05-03 LANE-NIGHTSHIFT-ROUND2 R2-4):** When measuring per-phase per-turn timing for performance audit, the durable pattern is: (1) add synchronous `process.hrtime.bigint()` instrumentation to `scenario_runner.ts` ONLY (never engine code); (2) emit aggregated timings to a single audit file (`data/derived/perf_baseline_round2.json`); (3) **revert the instrumentation** before commit — only the audit artifacts (raw data + markdown report) ship. Determinism preservation: synchronous timing only, no async profilers, no behavior change. Findings live in audit report with **specific functions to investigate next** (with line numbers, not general "look at combat"). The audit names the gap (40w n1626 mean 3,094 ms/turn = 30.9× over <100 ms target) and flags P0 (every turn >1s); it does NOT propose fixes — that's the next lane's scope. Applied in `[2026-05-03] feat(round2): six-lane parallel ship`.

**Sensitive-history §6-blocked diagnostic work ships parametric tools + binding canon questions, never preconditions relaxation (2026-05-03 LANE-NIGHTSHIFT-ROUND2 R2-6):** The Srebrenica genocide rupture not firing in 188w runs is OPEN_P0; the FIX is §6-blocked (canon sign-off chain required) but the AUDIT is Ring 1. The durable pattern is: (1) build a **parametric** diagnostic tool (rupture-trace, not srebrenica-specific in code) that emits per-turn condition status against any rupture event JSON; (2) classify each unmet condition as engine-bug / upstream-prerequisite-missing / bot-AI-gap / combat-math-gap / canon-correct-emergent-silence; (3) ship a **ranked failure hypothesis** with evidence citations (force_ratios, brigade rosters, perimeter personnel) — not prose; (4) name **specific binding §6 sign-off questions** so a future canon-amendment lane has a concrete question, not vague "look at this". STRICT BOUNDARY — never propose fixes to `enclave_resilience.ts`, OOB JSON, rupture conditions, FORAWWV, paint anchors, or `political_controllers`; never relax preconditions; never modify run artifacts. The diagnostic tool + audit report + tests are the deliverable; the fix is the user's call once §6 sign-off lands. Reference: `tools/diagnostics/srebrenica_rupture_trace.cjs` + `docs/40_reports/audits/20260503_SREBRENICA_RUPTURE_NON_FIRING_DIAGNOSTIC.md`. Applied in `[2026-05-03] feat(round2): six-lane parallel ship`.

**Default new `recovery_reason` enum values to feeding `failed_offensive_objectives` cooldown unless canon-grounded reason to skip (2026-05-02 LANE-B1 PLANNING_INVALIDATED_COOLDOWN):** When `sector_offensive.ts:recordFailedObjectives` had an explicit `if (op.recovery_reason === 'planning_invalidated') return;` skip, six sequential commander ops at the same target_osids tuple silently re-emitted every turn. The fix was removing the skip — mirror existing failure modes (`max_failures`, `brigade_attrition`). The durable rule: a `recovery_reason` value defaults to FEEDING the cooldown. Skips must be canon-grounded (`probe_complete` is recon-by-force not a failure; `political_blocked` is truce-induced not a corps decision). Treating new enum values as default-skip produces re-emission loops because the existing `failed_offensive_objectives` mechanism is the canonical CO-memory of "this objective is unproductive — try elsewhere." Systems Manual §6.4 explicitly distinguishes the diagnostic role (separating planning-phase failure from execution-attempt counters) from the cooldown role (CO-memory). Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(combat): planning_invalidated feeds CO objective-failure cooldown`.

**When an anomaly type masks distinct root causes, split via `subtype` field rather than letting consumers heuristic-classify (2026-05-02 LANE-B3 ANOMALY_SECTOR_SUBTYPE):** `empty_contested_sector` and `undefended_front_subsegments` were emitting one warning each despite encoding two distinct routings (`pool_exhausted` → operations/formation expert for replacement pool; `misallocated` → corps-army-commander for rebalance). The durable pattern: optional `subtype?: string` on `AnomalyReport`; group flagged entities by subtype during emit; emit one report per subtype found. Consumers filter by `subtype` to route to the correct specialist. Avoid: encoding the root cause in `description` text and forcing consumers to grep, or splitting one anomaly into two TYPE values (the type is the OBSERVATION; subtype is the CAUSE). Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(scenario): anomaly check sector subtype classification`.

**When `war_termination.ts` adds flag-based triggers, wire a producer at the source decision-resolution function (2026-05-02 LANE-D1 WAR_ENDED_EARLY_PRODUCER):** `war_termination.ts:62` was reading `flags.war_ended_early` + `flags.early_peace_implemented` since the file's authorship, but no production code path ever wrote them. `resolvePeacePlan` set `meta.game_over=true` directly, bypassing the flag. The durable rule: when adding a flag-based termination trigger, identify the canonical source decision-resolution function (here `resolvePeacePlan` when allAccepted) and write the flag THERE alongside the direct `meta.game_over` write. Defense-in-depth: termination already worked via `meta.game_over` short-circuit at `war_termination:36-43`, but downstream consumers reading the flag (UI, AAR, Cost Ledger, future event pipelines) get the signal too. Phantom flag branches that consume but never fire are debt; convert them to real producers when adding the flag, not later. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(negotiation): wire war_ended_early producer`.

**Per-turn × per-formation observability emits live in the harness, never the engine (2026-05-02 LANE-A1 PER_TURN_BRIGADE_SNAPSHOT):** When a future investigation needs per-turn × per-formation evidence (location_osid, mv_state, sector assignment, op participation, etc.), the canonical pattern mirrors `weekly_report.jsonl` at `scenario_runner.ts:1593-1597 / :2118 / :2154`. New `src/scenario/<topic>_temporal_emit.ts` exports a pure read-only `buildXxxRows(state, weekIndex)` function consumed by the harness emit block. Engine NEVER imports the emitter; pipeline NEVER mutates GameState for emission; save/load schema NEVER changes. strictCompare from `src/state/turn_phases.ts` for stable iteration; `stableStringify` for JSON line serialization. Always-on (no flag) — `weekly_report.jsonl` precedent shows ~10-20 MB at 188w is in-band. The pattern is now precedent for any future temporal log (per-corps, per-sector, per-op) — separate emit, separate file, separate builder, single ownership boundary per topic. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(harness): per-turn brigade-keyed snapshot emit`.

**The structural-test pattern over `warPhases` step indices is the canonical gate against pipeline-ordering hypothesis re-positing (2026-05-02 LANE-2026-05-02-TRIGGERED-OP-TEMPORAL-TRACE):** When a closeout report names a successor blocker that depends on a specific within-turn step ordering ("X runs before Y in the prep loop"), write a structural test that asserts the relevant step indices in the live `warPhases` export. The test goes RED only if someone reorders the pipeline. This blocks future Claude/Codex from re-positing the same superseded hypothesis without first acknowledging the ordering invariant. Pure synchronous assertion over a static module export — no synthetic GameState construction, no async, no random. `tests/triggered_op_temporal_contract.test.ts` is the precedent; replicate the pattern when retiring any pipeline-ordering hypothesis. Applied in `[2026-05-02] docs(combat): retire queued-order predicate hypothesis`.

**Decision Room dossiers should be active-card projections, not a second priority store (2026-05-02 Decision Room priority dossier):** Once the Decision Room has a deterministic `PresidentialDecisionRoomCard[]` archive and grouped `sourceHandoffs`, a detailed "why does this matter?" pane should derive from those objects directly. The durable pattern is: accept an optional `selectedCardId`, fall back to the top sorted card, copy the selected card's source/evidence/action target, derive related items from the existing handoff's `cardIds`, and mark whether the card is present in `advanceReadiness.items`. Do not create `priorityDossierQueue`, `priorityDossierLedger`, a second card archive, or a Warroom-owned dossier. The dossier is an Army HQ inspection affordance; source truth remains with the review queue, opportunity dossiers, operational SITREP, command briefing, Turn Aftermath records, active cost, and Chronicle. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(ui): add Decision Room priority dossier`.

**Warroom handoffs may summarize Decision Room urgency, but clicks must leave through App-owned target handling (2026-05-02 Warroom source handoff routing):** The Warroom priority docket is allowed to show compact source handoffs, but it must not become a second router or records browser. The durable pattern is: pass the preserved `PresidentialDecisionRoomNavigationTarget` upward through a typed callback (`onReviewTarget`), close the local tray, and let `App.tsx` call `openPresidentialDecisionRoomNavigationTarget(...)` just like row-level `Review Before Advance` actions. Do not teach Warroom components how to open Army HQ tabs, focused Turn Aftermath records, corps briefings, or Chronicle directly. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(ui): route Warroom source handoffs through App target handling`.
**Combat-power context lookups (supply, home-distance) read brigade `location_osid` directly — predictor calls for committed-in-transit participants need a destination-context override or they evaluate power against the wrong OSID (2026-05-02 in-transit combat-power context lane):** `computeAttackerPower` in `combat_math.ts` evaluates `getSupplyMult(formation, state, mode, supplyByOsid)` (which reads `formation.location_osid` for the supply-state-by-osid lookup) and `getHomeDistanceMultFromCache(state, formation)` (which reads a per-formation cached hop-count derived from current location). For an operation participant in `brigade_movement_state.status === 'in_transit'` toward an axis-relevant OSID, `location_osid` is the INTERMEDIATE transit OSID — so the brigade's predicted attacker power is computed against unfavorable transit-territory supply state instead of the staging/destination OSID where it has been committed to operate from. The fix: extend `computeAttackerPower(... contextLocationOverride?: string)` and `getSupplyMult(... contextLocationOverride?: string)` with optional override parameters; caller (`estimateForceRatio` only) builds per-op relevance set (staging + axis stagings + approach OSIDs for current launch objectives), deterministically selects override OSID (`op.staging_osid` first, else `strictCompare`-sorted first relevance OSID), and gates on `isCommittedInTransitTo` from the predecessor lane. **Skip home-distance from override** — the cache is per-formation/opaque from the call site; recomputing in this hot path is a layer violation; effect is marginal (0.85–1.0× band) vs the supply-state binary cliff (1.0 → 0.45 attack-mode at critical). The durable rule is: **any `combat_math.ts` helper that evaluates a formation against `location_osid`-derived context must accept an optional override parameter when called from the predictor layer; default-undefined preserves byte-stable behavior for the resolver/predictor/sector-rating callers (which evaluate against current physical location, correctly).** Caller restriction is intentional: only `estimateForceRatio` knows the operation-relevance set and can honestly redirect context to where the brigade is committed; resolver, predictor, and rating callers must continue to evaluate against current physical location for live combat resolution / adjacency-from-here / sector ratings. Faction-agnostic; deterministic via `strictCompare` + `Set.has()`. Reference: `tests/operation_preparation_in_transit_context.test.ts` T1 + T7; predecessor handoff #7 from In-Transit Predictor lane `87062cc4`. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(combat): predictor / combat-power context honest for committed-in-transit operation participants`.

**Command-loop questions belong as lanes over the Decision Room archive, not as new queues (2026-05-02 Decision Room command-loop lanes):** Once Strategic Priorities has a deterministic card archive, the player-facing questions "what is urgent?", "what decisions are pending?", "what fronts/corps explain the urgency?", "what should I inspect next?", and "what will be forgotten if I advance?" should be answered by projections over that archive. Do not rebuild source logic for each question. The durable pattern is: sort cards once in `buildPresidentialDecisionRoomView(...)`, then derive local lanes (`Urgent`, `Decisions`, `Fronts`, `Inspect`, `Advance`) that carry card ids, counts, headlines, and the existing source navigation target. Pre-advance reminders should be category-diverse before duplicates are allowed, so one noisy source cannot hide a different class of risk. The lanes are navigation/scan affordances only: source truth remains with the review queue, opportunity dossiers, operational SITREP, command briefing, Turn Aftermath records, active cost archive, and Chronicle. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(ui): add Decision Room command-loop lanes`.

**Predictor / readiness gates that filter on brigade `location_osid` must also consider committed-in-transit destinations, otherwise pre-stage helpers and other deliberate movement systems are silently undone (2026-05-02 in-transit predictor lane):** `areParticipantsReadyForExecution` and `axisHasExecutableOpeningAttack` in `sector_offensive_launch_helpers.ts` historically read brigade.location_osid for adjacency / readiness checks and treated `brigade_movement_state.status === 'in_transit'` as an unconditional skip. After commit `98446604` introduced `prestageBrigadesForTriggeredOp` (which writes column-march orders so participants reach axis staging during planning_duration grace), every triggered op's participants entered `in_transit` next turn — and were silently dropped from the readiness gate, so the op `planning_invalidated`'d even with committed brigades en-route. The fix is the smallest deterministic predicate: `isCommittedInTransitTo(state, brigadeId, relevantOsids)` returns true iff `brigade_movement_state[id].status === 'in_transit'` AND `destination_sids.some(d => relevantOsids.has(d))`. Two callsites: readiness uses each axis's `axisApproachOsids` / `objectiveApproachOsids`; the opening-attack gate uses front-edge-adjacency-to-objective. **QA semantic split** (T6b): the gate count includes in-transit-toward-relevant participants but the concentrated-outcome stack uses staged-only — en-route brigades raise the gate but cannot inflate predicted concentrated combat power until they physically arrive. Determinism-auditor rule: scan `destination_sids.some(...)`, NOT `[0]`-only — `[0]` semantics depend on whether `[0]` is "next hop" or "final destination" and could brittle under future route planners. The durable rule is: **any readiness/predictor predicate that filters by current `location_osid` is incomplete; engine helpers that legitimately put brigades in_transit (Phase B distribution, commander correction, triggered-op pre-stage, army-reserve recall, emergency defense) commit those brigades to a destination — predicates that ignore the destination undo the helper's commitment.** Apply to other readiness/launch sites: brigade-attack eligibility, sector-assignment eligibility, reactive-defense triggers — anywhere `location_osid` drives an availability check. Reference: `tests/sector_offensive_in_transit_predictor.test.ts` T1–T6 + D1 + G; predecessor handoff #7 from Krivaja PARTIAL `98446604`. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(combat): predictor / readiness gates count operation participants in_transit toward axis-relevant OSIDs`.

**Subagents that summarize ICTY/legal paragraphs can fabricate paragraph-specific specificity — verify direct text before citing in canon-grade comments or tests (2026-05-02 Krivaja-95 ICTY citation correction):** When `/historian` was dispatched to research Krivaja-95 and asked to cite specific ICTY paragraphs supporting brigade attack-axis assignments, the agent returned a confident table with claims like "1st Milici LIB | Supporting (W axis, Milići → Jadar) | Krstić §123" and "1st Zvornik LIB | Did NOT participate in opening assault". Codex review flagged these citations as unsupported. Direct fetch of Krstić IT-98-33-T §§122–123 from `icty.org` proved §§122–123 discuss only Krivaja-95's STRATEGIC OBJECTIVES (split enclaves, reduce to urban cores) — they name no brigades and assign no attack axes. Direct extraction of Popović IT-05-88-T from the official PDF showed §244 actually addresses the preparatory order to "the Zvornik, Birac, Romanija, Vlasenica, Podrinje, Bratunac, Milici and Skelani brigades", §245 fn 757 specifically tasks "the Battalion of the Zvornik Brigade ... along the axis of three wooded hills (500 metres north of Zeleni Jadar) – Pusmulići village – Bojna – Srebrenica", and §247 places Zvornik Brigade Commander Pandurević in command of TG-1 at the south of the enclave on 5–6 July (opening assault). The historian agent's "Zvornik joined only post-fall for column interdiction" claim was directly contradicted by §247. The durable rule is: **for any canon-grade comment or test that cites a specific ICTY/legal paragraph as authority, verify the citation against the direct paragraph text before committing — do not rely on a subagent summary alone.** Use `WebFetch` on the official ICTY HTML/PDF, then for PDFs use local `pdftotext -layout` to extract verbatim. Anchor regex tests in the catalog comment to required citation patterns (`Popović §244`, `§245 fn 757`) AND to forbidden fabrication patterns (e.g., the prior "Zvornik post-fall only" framing). This pattern applies to any historical-source citation: ICTY, ICJ, UN reports, primary documents. Subagent confidence is not evidence; direct paragraph text is. Reference: PROJECT_LEDGER `[2026-05-02] fix(operations): Krivaja-95 catalog ICTY-citation correction`. Applied as the corrective layer over `68b56d1f`.

**Trigger-turn pre-stage helper must declare an explicit overwrite contract — silently writing brigade_movement_orders breaks engine owners with priority and resets in-transit progress (2026-05-02 Krivaja-95 helper contract revision):** The first version of `prestageBrigadesForTriggeredOp` (commit `68b56d1f`) wrote `brigade_movement_orders[id]` unconditionally for any participant whose `location_osid !== axis.staging_osid` — a "fill in the gap if not at staging" model. Codex review flagged that this silently overwrites: (a) brigades already in_transit toward the staging OSID (re-issuing the order resets `turns_remaining`/`path` per architecture lesson 2026-04-01); (b) brigades in_transit toward a different OSID (originating owner had reason — emergency defense, commander correction, army-reserve recall); (c) brigades with an existing `brigade_movement_orders[id]` entry from another owner. The corrected helper now SKIPS in all three cases — triggered-op pre-stage has NO priority over other movement-order owners. Net effect: pre-stage fills the GAP for participants without any movement plan; participants with plans keep theirs. The durable rule is: **any helper that writes to `brigade_movement_orders` (or any other shared mutator surface) MUST declare an overwrite contract: who has priority over whom, what to do when a record already exists, what to do when transit state is active. Silent overwrite is never the right default.** Movement-order owners include at least: Phase B distribution (`brigade_front_distribution.ts`), commander correction (`commander_march_correction.ts`), army-reserve recall (`army_reserve_system.ts`), emergency-defense march, retreat (`attack_retreat_displacement.ts`), and (now) triggered-op pre-stage. Each owner should document its precedence rules in the helper JSDoc. Reference: `prestageBrigadesForTriggeredOp` JSDoc rules 1–4 + tests T7–T10 in `tests/krivaja_roster_and_prestage.test.ts`. Applied in `[2026-05-02] fix(operations): Krivaja-95 catalog ICTY-citation correction + pre-stage helper overwrite contract`.

**Triggered ops have no built-in pre-stage mechanism — `planning_duration` grace was the only window for participants to drift to staging, and Phase B distribution is op-blind (2026-05-02 Krivaja-95 roster + pre-stage):** `checkTriggeredOperations` (`src/sim/combat/triggered_operations.ts`) injects a triggered op when its predicate fires and its primary corps has no active op, but it never emits movement orders for participating brigades. The design relied on bot AI to drift brigades to the axis `staging_osid` during the `planning_duration: 3` grace turns, but `brigade_front_distribution.ts:660` (Phase B) makes movement decisions blind to op participation — a brigade in a Krivaja-95 catalog can be parked at `op:olovo:slivnje` 5+ hops from the Bratunac axis at trigger turn t168, and the op silently `planning_invalidated` because `hasExecutableOpeningAttack` (`sector_offensive_launch_helpers.ts:280-325`) requires `adjacentParticipants > 0` measured from `brigade.location_osid`. The fix is a generic `prestageBrigadesForTriggeredOp(state, def)` helper that emits column-march `brigade_movement_orders` for non-staged eligible participants at injection. Phase B's in-transit guard at `brigade_assignment.ts:1809/1876/1992` already excludes brigades with active movement orders, so the helper composes cleanly with the existing pipeline. The durable rule is: **any "triggered" subsystem that depends on participants being at a specific position by trigger turn must EITHER own its own movement-order emission OR explicitly document that it relies on emergent pre-positioning. Silent reliance on emergent placement is a hidden gate that fires invisibly when bot AI makes different choices.** Apply to other "triggered" surfaces: opportunity decisions, reactive defense triggers, autonomy proposals — if any of these depends on a brigade being adjacent to an objective at activation, audit whether the triggering path also emits the movement order. Determinism: any pre-stage helper must `strictCompare`-sort both axis iteration and per-axis brigade iteration so writes land in deterministic order. Caveat (next-lane handoff): pre-stage helper interacts adversely with `estimateForceRatio` numerator computation, which excludes `in_transit` brigades from `adjacentParticipants` count even when destined for the op's staging — pre-stage moves brigades into `in_transit`, dropping them from numerator before they arrive, so the predictor reports a LOWER force_ratio after pre-stage than before. Successor lane: `estimateForceRatio` should count `in_transit`-destined-for-staging brigades as numerator participants. Reference: 188w n1614 force_ratio drop 0.084 → 0.052 on Krivaja-95 + 0.282 → 0.209 on Stupčanica-95 with byte-stable audit-layer counts. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(operations): Krivaja-95 catalog historical correction + trigger-turn pre-stage helper for triggered ops`.

**Sensitive-history successor lanes need a controller+rupture proof surface, not prose-only verdicts (2026-05-02 sensitive-history status diagnostic):** For Srebrenica/Žepa work, a lane is not resolved just because predictor ratios improve or narrative events fire. The repeatable proof must show canonical enclave OSID controllers, capital controllers, rupture-consequence presence, watched late-war AARs, and Drina brigade status in one deterministic artifact. Use `node tools/diagnostics/sensitive_history_status.cjs <run_dir>` after every Krivaja roster, Stupčanica defender-stack, brigade co-location, or Žepa surrender successor lane. Applied in `[2026-05-02] tools(diagnostics): add sensitive-history enclave status verifier`.

**Chronicle review should be a filtered projection of one archive, not per-lens regeneration (2026-05-02 Chronicle review filters):** Chronicle entries already encode campaign memory from `turnSummaries`; review tools should keep one canonical `allEntries` list and derive `filteredEntries` plus counts from it. This avoids drift where Cost, Headlines, Combat, or Diplomatic lenses disagree about what happened. Dossier panels, dots, event totals, and empty states should all read the same filtered projection. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(ui): add Chronicle review filters`.

**Chronicle memory should record costly turns as events, not wait for final judgment (2026-05-02 Chronicle cost memory):** Final Cost Ledger owns endgame reckoning, but Chronicle owns campaign memory. If a turn crosses severe player-scoped cost thresholds, the timeline should file a `cost` entry from `turnSummaries` so the player can later see which weeks were expensive, even if the final verdict has not happened. Keep this as a read model: no scoring, no new state writer, no duplicate ledger. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(ui): add Chronicle cost memory cards`.

**Active campaign cost is a read model between Turn Aftermath and final Cost Ledger, not a second reckoning owner (2026-05-02 active campaign cost spine):** The player needs cumulative cost while the war is still live, but the final Cost Ledger remains the endgame judgment owner. The durable pattern is: derive active cost-so-far from persisted `turnSummaries` / `latestTurnSummary`, surface it in Army HQ Records and War Summary, and keep it explanatory. Do not create a second simulation writer, second cost ledger, or hidden campaign-score state. Filtered archive windows and campaign-level cost are separate products: `Campaign pulse` may recompute over visible records, while `Campaign cost so far` should read the full archive unless the UI explicitly labels it as a filtered view. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(ui): surface active campaign cost before endgame reckoning`.

**Presidential review count must be adapter-owned, not patched per surface (2026-05-02 review queue unification):** Operation-opportunity dossiers are presidential decisions, so they belong in `presidentialReviewQueue.pendingCount` at the `GameStateAdapter` boundary. Do not let Army HQ, toolbar, Warroom, and Inbox each add their own local count math. Surfaces may still render category-specific shortcuts, but the root "reviews pending" signal must come from one adapter-level summary. Army reserve requests stay distinct because they are army-reserve management pressure, not the same presidential review queue. Applied in `[2026-05-02] fix(ui): include operation dossiers in the presidential review queue`.

**Post-turn product truth needs a dedicated aftermath packet, not a loose return to the map (2026-05-02 Turn Aftermath spine):** Once the player advances a turn, the product owes a compact answer to four questions before asking for more orders: what changed, what did it cost, which records matter, and what needs attention next. The durable pattern is a pure read model over already-persisted turn truth (`latestTurnSummary`, `turnSummaries`, inbox/review state), an immediate modal after advance-turn, and a persistent Army HQ records archive. Do not create a second simulation writer, second cost ledger, or second history store for this surface. Browser proof also showed the footer actions are mission-critical on mobile, so keep modal action bars in stable grid tracks rather than wrapping flex rows inside fixed-height overlays. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(ui): land Turn Aftermath product spine with browser proof`.

**Deep objectives after a breakthrough belong in live-corridor follow-on opportunities, not in the initial operation footprint (2026-05-02 Sana follow-on proof):** The original Sana 95 opportunity bundled the Sanski Most / Kljuc interior axis into the initial breakout. n1605 proved that axis had no live approach corridor, so the launch-readiness engine correctly skipped it and later exposed it as `unreachable_at_launch`. The catalog fix split Sana into initial reachable axes (`sana_krupa`, `sana_bihac_petrovac`) and `sana_95_follow_on` for the interior push, gated by live RBiH corridor anchors (`jasenica_2`, `vrtoce`, or `dobro_selo_2`) plus the normal pocket / Storm / readiness / logistics predicates. Rebased 188w proof `b2426eb412f4422e` shows the desired behavior: `sana_95` launches as reachable UNDERDELIV:2 (4 attacks, 0/18 captures), `sana_95_follow_on` stays blocked in-window by `staging_access` + `logistics`, and the campaign proof reports 0 reachability warnings. The durable rule is: **for any historical operation whose later objective set depends on an achieved corridor, author a separate follow-on opportunity gated by live control of approach anchors. Do not stuff polygon-interior objectives into the initial operation, and do not add a hardcoded `<initial_op>_completed -> follow_on` chain. Let any live path that opens the corridor surface the follow-on.**

**Parallel lanes must be integrated against latest main and re-proven as a combined system (2026-05-02 Codex architecture integration):** A branch can be locally green and still need a combined proof pass after another mega-lane lands. The opportunity proof platform and Storm theater gate split were each valid in their worktrees, but only the current-main integration could prove the full chain: combat-math AAR carryover + Storm readiness/rupture split + 5th Corps opportunity predicates + campaign proof diagnostics. Durable rule: after parallel autonomous lanes, merge/rebase onto current `main`, run the focused cross-surface tests, run at least one relevant scenario smoke, and classify hash drift with direct artifact diffs before calling the architecture stable. In this case n1607->n1609 hash drift was additive-only and explained by 15 `operation_history[*].force_ratio_estimate` fields, while n1610 proved Storm readiness at t85 and actual theater rupture at t174. This prevents false regressions and prevents "works in my lane" claims from becoming repo truth.

**Gate names must encode event timing, not precondition timing (2026-05-02 Storm theater split):** `operation_storm_triggered` had been used as an abstract readiness flag, but opportunity logic needed the stricter meaning "the Operation Storm event opened the western theater." Splitting readiness (`operation_storm_preconditions_met` / `operation_storm_precondition_turn`) from rupture (`operation_storm_triggered` / `operation_storm_turn`, backed by `event_last_fired_turn.operation_storm_1995`) unblocked pre-Storm crisis opportunities without letting post-Storm exploit operations fire early. Durable rule: if one historical condition has both buildup and event consequences, model them as separate state surfaces and make catalog predicates name which one they consume.

**Staleness contract for AAR snapshots: any field copied from live op state into AAR via additive carryover is launch-tick or last-tick value, NOT post-mortem honest value (2026-05-02 Combat-Math estimateForceRatio Defender-Modifier Integration):** When `OperationAAR.<field>` is populated by a 1-line carryover from `op.<field>` at finalize (the LANE B `recovery_reason` pattern, the Phase 5a `force_ratio_estimate` pattern), the AAR record snapshots whatever value the live op last held — which for assignment-during-assessment-only fields is the LAUNCH TICK value, frozen for the entire op lifetime. Three production examples proved this in n1607/n1608: Prsten 14.82 (vrs_sarajevo_romanija, n1607: launched ~t1-t2 with 6 JNA phantom brigades + 5 SRK vs ARBiH at entrenchment_turns=0; honest at launch tick 5-15×; hand-computed end-of-t40 honest ratio = 0.21). Pravda 47.47 (arbih_4th_corps, n1608, t86: 3 light-mountain bdes vs 1 disrupted HRHB defender at Stolac; honest at launch with small denominator + stacked mods; outcome confirms 5-star Brilliant Victory). Krivaja-95 0.0838 (vrs_drina, n1608, t168: planning_invalidated; predictor correctly returned "infeasible" at decision-time before any combat). The durable rule is: **any field copied from live op state into AAR via additive carryover is launch-tick or last-tick value, NOT post-mortem honest current-state. Future readers and tools must NOT interpret these stale values as predictor-state-at-completion. The lane invariant is "honest at decision time," NOT "honest at finalize tick." If a post-mortem honest snapshot is needed, that is a NEW design surface (recompute at finalize, separate field name to distinguish), not a re-interpretation of the existing field. This pattern generalizes beyond `force_ratio_estimate` to any `*_at_launch`, `*_at_assessment`, or `*_at_decision_time` semantic — name the field for what it captures, not what a downstream reader might wish it captured.** Reference: Phase 5 sector-expert empirical resolution of Prsten +672% anomaly; Phase 5b sector-expert resolution of Pravda 47.47 + Krivaja-95 0.0838. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(combat): integrate defender modifiers into estimateForceRatio (MEGA-LANE close)`.

**Spec-definition mismatch in war-or-game's named-event ranges: spec ranges may be GOD-MODE (full information, post-hoc historical truth) while predictor is COMMANDER-MODE (intel-fogged, decision-time) (2026-05-02 Combat-Math estimateForceRatio Defender-Modifier Integration):** Phase 5b 188w validation surfaced 5 surface "stop-gate breaches" (GREEN cases below the 2.5 floor). Empirical investigation by `/sector-expert` + `/war-or-game` proved 4 of 5 are NOT predictor bugs but spec-definition mismatches between war-or-game's named-event ranges and the sim's commander-mode predictor. Eastern Bosnia 92 sentinel-1 cluster is the canonical example: war-or-game expected 8.0–20.0 (god-mode armor-vs-rifles dominance reading from ICTY post-war forensic record) but predictor returns 1.0 sentinel because pre-planned-op intel confidence is structurally hard-zero (`op.sector_id` is undefined for pre-planned ops; `getOperationIntelConfidence` returns 0 when `!op.sector_id`; sentinel branch fires `enemyStrength === 0 → confidence < 0.5 ? 1.0 : 3.0` which becomes 1.0). The sentinel-1 reading is HONEST in commander-mode terms — real VRS at t0 had chaotic municipal Crisis Staff intel, not formed military intel (per ICTY *Krajišnik*/*Stanišić-Župljanin*). Three more examples from Phase 5b: Jackal 1992 spec range was authored for the 1992 historical event but sim's `Operation Jackal` is `available_from: 8` ≈ April 1993 (different historical event); Krivaja-95 spec 3.0–5.0 is OUTCOME ratio (achieved through CHETNIK paramilitary tail + Bratunac Brigade + UNPROFOR collapse, none in predictor scope) but predictor emits PROSPECTIVE ratio at decision time; Sana axes A/B vs corps-level granularity (spec emits per-axis, sim emits per-corps-op single ratio). The durable rule is: **war-or-game spec ranges for named historical events MUST declare which mode the range describes — god-mode (full information, post-hoc forensic truth) vs commander-mode (intel-fogged, decision-time predictor reading) vs outcome (final battle ratio achieved through factors outside predictor scope) vs prospective (predictor's eligibility-time forecast). Without that declaration, surface "stop-gate breaches" cannot be empirically classified as predictor-bug-vs-spec-mismatch. Next time war-or-game authors a spec range for a calibration-target event, the spec MUST clarify mode + granularity (per-op vs per-axis vs per-faction-side) BEFORE the implementer runs Phase 5 evidence collection.** Reference: Phase 5b `/sector-expert` + `/war-or-game` Tier 1+2 panel on n1608 evidence; Phase 5b `/gap-finder` Q6 4-mismatch enumeration. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(combat): integrate defender modifiers into estimateForceRatio (MEGA-LANE close)`.

**Layer-1.5 predictor cannot reach honest-spec ratios below ~1.5 without Layer-2 attrition modeling — posture asymmetry + supply default skew impose a structural floor (2026-05-02 Combat-Math estimateForceRatio Defender-Modifier Integration):** Phase 4 implementer flagged that QA-engineer's Phase 1 spec for Grmeč ratio `<1.5` and parity `0.85–1.15` was unreachable in pure Layer-1.5 predictor — the codebase's `POSTURE_ATTACK[attack]=1.0×` vs `POSTURE_DEFENSE[defend]=1.4×` plus supply defaults (atk=0.4 vs def=0.5) impose a structural floor on attacker-vs-defender power ratios. War-or-game's even-more-aggressive 0.30–0.60 honest spec for Grmeč 94 (light-inf vs entrenched ridge) is unreachable in Layer 1.5; production 188w n1608 achieved 1.10 (5.2× honest correction from synthetic pre-fix 5.748 — the win), but residual ~2× gap to spec ceiling 0.60 cannot be closed by adding more defender-side modifiers to the predictor. The remaining gap requires Layer-2 attrition forecasting in the predictor (e.g. running a synthetic 1-3 attack rounds of `predictCombatOutcome` against the visible defender stack and reading back the projected attacker losses + sustain rate). The durable rule is: **a Layer-1.5 force-ratio predictor that integrates static defender modifiers (terrain, urban/forest, entrenchment, supply, equipment, posture, morale, officer, fatigue, corps stance) cannot reach attrition-driven honest ratios (1:6 repulse with 5th Corps light_infantry on Grmeč ridge; 1:4 repulse with VRS at Vlašić). To reach those, the predictor must integrate Layer-2 attrition forecasting — synthetic combat rounds against the visible defender stack — which is a separate next-lane scope. Calling that "harder combat" is wrong (it's still predictor-only); calling it "Layer-2 in the predictor" is correct. Test thresholds for Layer-1.5 predictor work should be calibrated to what Layer 1.5 can structurally reach, not to what Layer-2-augmented predictor would reach. Bug-proof = honest correction multiplier (X→Y where Y is the new honest ratio achievable in the predictor's tier), not "ratio ≤ historical outcome ratio."** Reference: Phase 4 implementer deviation note (orchestrator-accepted with Phase 5 evidence verification); Phase 5b `/war-or-game` Q3 GOOD_ENOUGH classification of Grmeč 94 1.10. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(combat): integrate defender modifiers into estimateForceRatio (MEGA-LANE close)`.

**Multi-axis ops can silently launch with structurally unlaunchable axes — persist diagnostic at launch readiness check, not at AAR finalize (2026-05-01 Late-War Operation Combat Delivery):** When `areParticipantsReadyForExecution` evaluates a multi-axis op (`sector_offensive_launch_helpers.ts:215-243`), each axis is checked independently. If any axis has at least one ready brigade at a friendly approach OSID, the op launches. Axes whose first objective has zero approach OSIDs (front-unreachable from any friendly position — typically because the catalog declared a polygon-interior first objective with no front edges) hit `if (axisApproachOsids.size === 0) continue;` and are silently skipped. The OUTER op still launches; the silently-skipped axis stays in `'executing'`, accumulates idle failures, advances via skip-ahead, never attacks. The failure mode is invisible to AAR-layer post-mortem because no diagnostic is written. The durable rule is: **any pipeline skip-path that drops information about why an axis/objective/proposal failed eligibility should write a sibling write-only diagnostic on the in-state object AND carry it through to the AAR layer**. Phase C of the Late-War Operation Combat Delivery mega-lane does exactly that: `OperationAxis.unreachable_at_launch?: boolean` is set at the silent-skip path; `AxisAAR.unreachable_at_launch?: boolean` carries through at finalize. Execution flow is preserved (silent skip remains intentional behavior — the late-war ladder rests on it); the diagnostic alone changes. Apply this pattern to other silent-skip seams: brigade-attack eligibility, sector-assignment failures, opportunity evaluator skips (LANE E already), reactive-defense triggers. Reference: `sector_offensive_launch_helpers.ts:227-233`, `tests/operation_axis_unreachable_diagnostic.test.ts`. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat+evidence(operations): Late-War Operation Combat Delivery Mega-Lane`.

**`estimateForceRatio` is a personnel-only count predictor, blind to defender artillery/tanks/entrenchment/terrain/urban/supply — produces fantasy ratios like 7.19 for ARBiH light_infantry vs entrenched VRS (2026-05-01 Late-War Operation Combat Delivery investigation):** The launch-readiness force-ratio predictor at `src/sim/combat/operation_preparation.ts:192-249` counts attacker and defender personnel only. It does not consume `getDefensiveFireMult`, `getForestMult`, `getUrbanMult`, entrenchment turns, supply state, or equipment composition. The downstream consequence is documented in n1605: Sana 95 axes A/B repulse 1:6 vs predicted 7.19; Grmeč 94 catastrophic-stall 1:6 vs predicted high ratio; the corps commander's "go" decision is made against a value that has no relationship to actual combat outcome. The durable rule is: **a launch-readiness predictor that counts only one side of the kill equation is structurally a railroad — it always says go, and the loss happens at engagement time. Any combat-prediction surface (predictor, briefing, commander assessment, opportunity force_quality band) that the bot/player relies on must integrate every defender-side modifier the actual combat resolver uses**. This is COMBAT-P14 / BRIEF-GAP-1 from the 2026-04-02 Engine Health Audit and is the recommended next mega-lane after Phase C. Reference: MEMORY.md "Engine Health Audit"; n1605 evidence + 6-investigator synthesis. Applied as next-lane recommendation in `[2026-05-01] feat+evidence(operations): Late-War Operation Combat Delivery Mega-Lane`.

**Pre-commit hook + cross-lane WIP: use the LANE E set-aside pattern (`mv` + commit + restore) instead of `--no-verify` (2026-05-01 Late-War Operation Combat Delivery, repeated from LANE E):** When a parallel-lane (Codex) WIP file exists in the working tree as `??` and would cause `npx tsc --noEmit` (run by `.husky/pre-commit`) to fail because it references a yet-to-be-landed module, the canonical resolution is NOT `--no-verify`. The set-aside pattern: `mv tests/ui/turn_aftermath.test.ts tests/ui/turn_aftermath.test.ts.set_aside` (plain `mv` since untracked); commit; `mv tests/ui/turn_aftermath.test.ts.set_aside tests/ui/turn_aftermath.test.ts` (restore); confirm `git status --porcelain` shows the file back as `??`. This pattern preserves the full type-check guardrail, does not edit Codex's WIP content (only the filename, temporarily), and restores to original state after each commit. Use this for every cross-lane WIP that triggers the hook. The Late-War Operation Combat Delivery mega-lane used it for all four phase commits (A/B/C/E). Reference: LANE E ledger entry + this lane's UNBLOCK section in working-on.md. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat+evidence(operations): Late-War Operation Combat Delivery Mega-Lane`.

**Precondition readiness is not event truth; use named helpers for theater gates (2026-05-01):** LANE E found Una/Breza/Pauk blocked because `operation_storm_triggered` was being set when abstract Storm preconditions aligned, long before the `operation_storm_1995` event fired. That made a pressure/readiness flag masquerade as actual western-theater rupture. The durable rule is: **never reuse a precondition boolean as the event's world-state truth.** Persist readiness separately (`operation_storm_preconditions_met`, `operation_storm_precondition_turn`) and expose event truth through named helpers (`isWesternTheaterRuptured`, `isPreStormWesternTheater`, `getOperationStormEventTurn`). Opportunity catalogs, AAR/report surfaces, and future late-war gates should ask the semantic question they need instead of reading raw meta fields. Applied in `[2026-05-01] fix(operations): split Operation Storm readiness from theater rupture`.

**T3 defensive-crisis opportunities should be gated on live enemy threat-pressure, not on attacker-side logistics (2026-05-01 LANE E):** LANE D found T3 Una/Breza/Pauk locked away by `logistics: required` failing against saturated RBiH supply pressure — but that gate is conceptually wrong. A defensive crisis is an opportunity to authorize "commit reserves to defend an active threat to the pocket." It is gated on whether the enemy is actually pressing (live threat-pressure), not on whether the defender's supply pipeline is in good shape. You defend with what you have, not with what you have stockpiled. LANE E refactors the topology: `logistics: required → optional` (severity/risk signal — high pressure means the defensive commit hurts, but does not block), and `enemy_weakness: n_a → required` with a new `threatPressureT3` predicate reading a curated `T3_BIHAC_THREAT_RING` of 6 historically RS/SVK-controlled OSIDs around the Bihać pocket. The defensive crisis fires only when at least one ring OSID is hostile-controlled (the historical pressure is live); if 5th Corps somehow clears the entire ring, the defensive-crisis surface correctly disappears. The durable rule is: **for any defensive-crisis opportunity (T3 substrate), the required-axis topology should reflect "is there an enemy crisis to defend against?" via a live threat-state predicate, NOT "are we in shape to defend?" via a defender-state gate. Defender-state belongs on optional severity/risk axes that surface in the dossier but do not block authorization. This applies to any future T3 family (e.g. ARBiH eastern enclaves, HVO Mostar 1993): author a threat-ring OSID list per family + a shared threat-pressure predicate, demote defender-supply to optional severity.** Reference: LANE E commit + `operation_opportunity_catalog_5th_corps.ts` `T3_BIHAC_THREAT_RING` + `threatPressureT3`. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(operations): LANE E 5th Corps opportunity predicate topology + 188w validation`.

**T1 offensive opportunities need a SECOND genuine optional axis distinct from `corps_readiness` so a single saturating substrate signal cannot lock the family away (2026-05-01 LANE E):** LANE D found T1 Tigar/APWB/Grmec each had `logistics` as the SOLE optional axis with `min_optional_axes:1`. Under saturated RBiH supply pressure, this collapses to a hardcoded gate. The fix is NOT to relax logistics ceilings — supply IS strained historically and the predicate should reflect that. The fix is to add a SECOND OPTIONAL axis backed by a different live signal so eligibility can route through `min_optional_axes:1` via either path. LANE E added `'force_quality'` as a 10th `PrereqAxis` enum value designed for exactly this role: it reads specific traits from `computeCorpsOperationReadiness` (`staging_reliability` for deception thrusts, `failure_recovery` for sustained drives, `axis_coordination` for multi-brigade deep advances) — NOT `operation_readiness`, which `corps_readiness` already gates on. Each T1 entry uses a distinct trait so force_quality is multi-dimensional, not a single scalar. The durable rule is: **any T1 entry with `min_optional_axes:1` must have at least two genuine optional axes, each reading a different live substrate signal. If `logistics` is one optional, the second must NOT depend on supply substrate state. Force-quality traits from `computeCorpsOperationReadiness` are the canonical substrate for "the corps can capitalize on this opportunity even when other things are bad." Use distinct traits across entries to keep the optional set diversified.** Reference: LANE E `force_quality` enum value + `forceQualityTigar/Apwb/Grmec` predicates. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(operations): LANE E 5th Corps opportunity predicate topology + 188w validation`.

**The opportunity evaluator skip path is a write-only diagnostic seam — silent skips make in-window misses invisible to future audits (2026-05-01 LANE E):** LANE D's hardest investigation was "why didn't 6 of 7 catalog entries surface across 188 weeks?" The answer required source-side predicate inspection because the evaluator's `if (!eligible) continue;` left no audit trail. LANE E adds a per-turn diagnostic emit at exactly that skip path, writing a record to `state.military.operation_opportunity_diagnostics` capturing `(turn, opportunity_id, failed_required_axes, failed_optional_axes, optional_green_count, min_optional_axes)`. Bounded by len(catalog × in-window-turns) — out-of-season entries are explicitly excluded to avoid log noise. One-shot-guard-blocked entries are also excluded (a terminal-status proposal isn't an interesting "miss"). 188w n1605 emitted 20 records; LANE E found the T3 trio's actual gate (alliance_context Storm-flag) cleanly via these records, no source inspection needed. The durable rule is: **any pipeline-step skip path that drops information about why eligibility failed should write a sibling diagnostic record. The cost is one append per turn per in-window miss; the value is that next-lane investigations can read the breadcrumb instead of re-deriving from source. Apply the same pattern to other catalogs (triggered ops, events, reactive defense, autonomy proposals): silent skips are observability debt that blocks audits.** Reference: LANE E `OperationOpportunityIneligibilityDiagnostic` + `evaluateOperationOpportunities` skip-path emit. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(operations): LANE E 5th Corps opportunity predicate topology + 188w validation`.

**Catalog predicate topology can be a railroad-by-omission even when each individual predicate is sound (2026-05-01 LANE D):** LANE D 188w stress proved every architectural axis of the opportunity substrate green (one-shot guard, T1 → buildCorpsOperation, T3 early-return code path, AAR linker, force-quality snapshot, health audit), but only 1 of 7 catalog entries surfaced as a proposal. Six LANE C entries failed the eligibility check at every visited turn for a single shared reason: the `logistics` axis predicate (ceiling 90-95) trips against RBiH `war_supply_pressure` pinned at 100 from turn 1 onward. T3 entries (`logistics: required`) failed unconditionally; T1 Tigar/APWB/Grmeč with `logistics` as the SOLE optional axis under `min_optional_axes:1` also failed unconditionally. Each predicate was internally sound; the topology — "make logistics required OR the only optional" — combined with a saturated supply scale produced a hardcoded outcome. Author comment in the catalog (`operation_opportunity_catalog_5th_corps.ts:472`) was already explicit: "the prompt's prereq wishlist had no genuine OPTIONAL axes" — author knew there was no real optionality and demoted logistics syntactically to satisfy the schema. The durable rule is: **opportunity catalogs need a topology audit, not just a per-predicate audit. Any T1 entry with only one optional axis is a single-point-of-failure railroad if that axis depends on a saturating substrate signal. Any T3 entry that makes logistics `required` against a saturating scale is unconditionally inert. The fix is not to tune predicate ceilings; it is to author a second genuine optional axis (e.g. force-quality trait, commander_confidence, or a substrate signal that does NOT saturate to its cap mid-war) so eligibility can emerge from the live state instead of always being blocked by one signal.** Reference: LANE D run `dca64282334ae735` (188w), Operations Expert + Gap-Finder findings. Applied in `[2026-05-01] evidence(operations): LANE D 5th Corps opportunity family 188w stress + health audit`.

**Engine-wide AAR aggregator misreports `total_attacks=0` for nearly half of all completed ops because it reads `weekly_log[*].attacks_this_turn` instead of canonical `op.attack_attempt_count` (2026-05-01 LANE D):** Cross-validation during LANE D revealed 21 of 43 AARs in the 188w n1604 run misreport `total_attacks=0` despite executing real combat. Operation Prijedor with `outcome=success` + 10/10 captures shows `total_attacks=0`; Operation Sana with `op.attack_attempt_count=7` across 3 axes and `recovery_reason='max_failures'` (real combat) is logged with `did_not_launch` exit_class because the opportunity AAR linker derives exit_class from the AAR's `total_attacks` field. Sector_offensive write-side (which increments `op.attack_attempt_count` and per-axis `attack_attempt_count` at `sector_offensive.ts:1041,1076,1116`) and AAR read-side (which sums `weekly_log[*].attacks_this_turn` at `operation_aar.ts:519-528`) disagree on which counter is canonical. The `weekly_log[*].attacks_this_turn` field is not consistently written for many op types. The durable rule is: **when a state transition is owned by one module and reported by another, the canonical counter must be referenced by both ends of the chain. Where the AAR aggregator and the operation lifecycle disagree on which counter is truth, the AAR is the consumer and must read the lifecycle's authoritative counter (`op.attack_attempt_count` / `axis.attack_attempt_count`), not a parallel weekly_log field that may not be written. This affects every consumer that derives `exit_class`, `total_attacks`, or success-vs-failure-vs-did_not_launch from AAR rows — including the opportunity exit_class derivation (`exitClassFromOperationAAR` in `operation_opportunities.ts:332`). Touching this is engine-wide; it must be a dedicated lane with QA regression on every AAR row in 40w + 188w runs, not a bounded single-owner fix.** Reference: LANE D run `dca64282334ae735`, OE finding #4 + orchestrator cross-validation. Applied as next-lane recommendation in `[2026-05-01] evidence(operations): LANE D 5th Corps opportunity family 188w stress + health audit`.

**Opportunity footprint/redirect DTOs are proposal snapshots, not UI catalog imports (2026-05-01):** Operation opportunity review needs objective/staging map truth and redirect choices, but the UI must not import sim catalog files or recreate operation definitions. The durable pattern is: persist authored footprint truth on `OperationOpportunityState` (`last_footprint`, `redirect_variants`) when the evaluator surfaces or refreshes a proposal; adapt those snapshots into player-safe labels in `operationOpportunityDossiers.ts`; render Army HQ controls from that DTO only. Redirect buttons must send `redirectVariantId` through the existing `resolveOperationOpportunityDecision` IPC bridge, while `applyResolvedOpportunityDecisions(...)` remains the sole mutation owner. Map highlighting should reuse the existing `operationTargetOsids` layer, not add a new map target system. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(ops-ui): add opportunity footprint and redirect DTOs`.

**Opportunity dossiers should show force-quality bands, not raw trait numerics or hidden booleans (2026-05-01):** The opportunity layer's job is to make institutional capability legible: operation_readiness, staging_reliability, axis_coordination, support_delivery, failure_recovery, reserve_response, and collapse_susceptibility. Persist the pure `computeCorpsOperationReadiness(...)` snapshot on the proposal (`last_force_quality_traits`), then have UI adapters convert it into player-safe bands (`strong`, `adequate`, `strained`, `poor`). For `collapse_susceptibility`, invert the raw value so the displayed band reads as health: high susceptibility = poor. Do not expose exact formulas/numerics in normal play, and do not use the UI banding as a second gameplay gate. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(ops-ui): surface force-quality bands in opportunity dossiers`.

**Rich opportunity decisions are review-row intent, not desktop-side opportunity mutation (2026-05-01):** The operation opportunity UI now exposes more than binary approve/decline, but the canonical mutation path is still the war pipeline. Desktop IPC must validate the player's payload, write `opportunity_decision` plus `opportunity_decision_options` onto the matching `PendingProposalReview`, and stop there. `applyResolvedOpportunityDecisions(...)` is the sole owner that turns that row into `applyOpportunityDecision(...)` calls, resolution rows, delayed reevaluation, or normal CorpsOperation creation. This preserves replay order, prevents UI shortcuts from becoming a second operation lifecycle, and keeps legacy `accepted=true/false` proposal rows as a compatibility fallback. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(ops-ui): add rich operation opportunity decision bridge`.

**APWB / SVK auxiliaries belong on `targets_friendly_overrides`, not on a fourth faction (2026-05-01):** The Bihać-pocket arc requires opportunity targeting against APWB-controlled OSIDs (Cazin southern flank, Velika Kladuša approaches) that are RBiH-painted in the jan1993 baseline (`data/source/calibration/painted_control_jan1993.json:632-635, 169-172`) yet during the historical 1994 arc represent enemy targets to a 5th Corps offensive against APWB armed formations. Modeling APWB (or SVK) as a fourth faction would require a substrate-scale lift (controller universe, painted-target rebaselines, combat-math doctrine tables, scenario family churn, OOB). The canonical pattern instead is: add `targets_friendly_overrides?: string[]` to `OperationOpportunityDef`; honor it inside `spawnCorpsOperationFromOpportunity` via override-bypass scope-restricted to `tier === 'T1'` AND `family === 'fifth_corps'`; document the override in the catalog entry's prose with a citation. Tests must enforce that the override only fires under the scope-restricted gate. The durable rule is: when a historically-enemy-but-painted-friendly auxiliary needs targeting on a tightly-bounded historical arc, prefer the substrate flag with scope-restricted apply over inventing a faction. Reference: LANE C Phase 1 substrate at commit `14dc48e1`; consumed by Phase 2 (Tigar-Sloboda 94) + Phase 3 (APWB Pressure 94). Applied in `[2026-05-01] milestone(operations): LANE C 5th Corps Opportunity Family Expansion CLOSED`.

**T3 defensive-crisis opportunities short-circuit the buildCorpsOperation path (2026-05-01):** Some historical opportunity content models a defensive crisis (e.g. VRS / SVK / APWB pressure on the Bihać pocket — Una 94, Breza 94, Pauk 94/95) where the player/bot's authorized response is "commit reserves to defend," not "launch a corps offensive." Inventing a parallel defensive lifecycle would duplicate behavior the existing reactive defense + sector morale chain already owns. The canonical pattern is: add `tier: 'T3'` as a literal on `OperationOpportunityDef`; in `applyOpportunityDecision`, when `def.tier === 'T3'` and decision is approve, write a resolution row with `executed_op_aar_id: undefined` and `exit_class: 't3_authorized_no_offensive'`; do NOT call `buildCorpsOperation`. The decline path is identical to T1 decline. Reactive defense / sector morale chain handles outcome. Tests must enforce that approve on a T3 entry produces zero new entries on the corps's `active_operations`. The durable rule is: T3 = "authorized defensive commit, no offensive lifecycle"; it is a terminal exit_class, not a transition into an op. Reference: LANE C Phase 1 substrate at commit `14dc48e1` (early-return at lines 692-703); consumed by Phase 4 (Una/Breza/Pauk T3 triad) at commit `f22c743e`. Applied in `[2026-05-01] milestone(operations): LANE C 5th Corps Opportunity Family Expansion CLOSED`.

**Emergent inter-opportunity dependencies must be expressed through overlapping windows + shared brigade pools + live-state predicates, NEVER through hardcoded `<x>_completed → <y>_eligible` chains (2026-05-01):** Real military arcs have dynamic dependencies (operation A succeeds → brigades committed forward → operation B's defensive crisis lands on a depleted reserve pool → operation C's window opens with a different readiness state). The wrong way to model these is a hardcoded predicate referencing another catalog entry's id (`if state.opportunities['grmec_94'].status === 'approved' then make pauk_94_95 harder`). That is railroad behavior: it locks the dependency at the design layer, removes player/bot agency over the timing, and makes the engine immune to counterfactual states. The canonical pattern is: (a) author windows that overlap when the historical events overlap; (b) author brigade rosters that share members when the historical formations overlap; (c) write predicates against live state (`corps_readiness`, `staging_access`, `pocket_survival`, `logistics`) that read whatever the world looks like at evaluation time. Examples from LANE C: Tigar-Sloboda 94 (w113-w122) + APWB Pressure 94 (w113-w125) overlap on `op:cazin:sturlic_2` as a shared `targets_friendly_overrides` OSID and on multiple 50x-series brigades — if Tigar-Sloboda just consumed brigades, APWB Pressure's readiness predicate naturally computes lower for 1-3 turns. Grmeč 94 (w133-w138) commits 6 of 9 brigades (504th/506th/517th excluded by design); Pauk 94/95 (w135-w145) opens 4 turns before Grmeč closes and reads the depleted pool through `corps_readiness` — the defensive crisis is harder to weather precisely because the offensive succeeded.
