No predicate references "grmec_94 was approved"; the dependency is a structural property of overlapping windows + a shared 9-brigade pool + live-state readiness/pocket-survival predicates. The durable rule is: if you find yourself reaching for a hardcoded chain between two opportunity_ids, you have a railroad — refactor to overlapping windows, shared rosters, and live predicates instead. Reference: LANE C Phase 5 commit `2a790255` (Grmeč 94 architectural hook for emergent Pauk overextension). Applied in `[2026-05-01] milestone(operations): LANE C 5th Corps Opportunity Family Expansion CLOSED`.

**Late-war operation opportunities have a single owner-path through the opportunity catalog; calendar-only triggered ops are deprecated for non-sensitive historical content (2026-05-01):** Lane B (Operation Opportunity MVP) introduced `OPERATION_OPPORTUNITY_CATALOG` in `src/sim/combat/operation_opportunities.ts` and migrated Operation Sana from `triggered_operations.ts:_TRIGGERED_OPS` to `SANA_95_OPPORTUNITY` in `src/sim/combat/operation_opportunity_catalog_5th_corps.ts`. The durable rule is: when a late-war historical operation's eligibility actually depends on live state (pocket survival, theater opening, corps readiness, staging access, enemy posture) — which is essentially every named historical operation — its single owner is the opportunity catalog, NOT the calendar-triggered scripted-op array. The opportunity layer's `applyOpportunityDecision` always routes approval through `buildCorpsOperation` (the canonical CorpsOperation factory), so the resulting op is indistinguishable from any other corps op downstream in `sector_offensive.ts`. The two paths must NEVER both own the same named operation. The exception is sensitive-history T4 candidates (Krivaja-95, Stupčanica-95, Goražde) which remain calendar-triggered until the SENSITIVE_HISTORY_DESIGN_GATE.md §6 sign-off chain (`/historian` + `/game-designer` + `/war-or-game` + user approval) authorizes T4 opportunity entries. Tests must enforce single ownership: assert the legacy `_TRIGGERED_OPS` does not contain the migrated op name AND the catalog contains exactly one entry for it. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(operations): 5th Corps / Sana 95 opportunity (LANE B Phase 3)`.

**Adding a per-turn pipeline step that writes an empty array to a new optional MilitaryState field WILL drift scenario hashes — the drift is purely additive, not behavior-altering, and is the correct shape change to record (2026-05-01):** Lane B Phase 1 added `state.military.operation_opportunities = []` on every war turn even with an empty catalog (substrate-only ship). The 40w final_save.json gained 7,394 bytes and the final_state_hash moved from `cbd7d61db0bfbe97` to `18994397e5b3b8ae` despite zero combat / control / formation / battle / operation lifecycle change (verified by `/scenario-creator-runner-tester`: weekly_report.jsonl, control_delta.json, formation_delta.json, activity_summary.json, end_report.md, operation_aars.json all byte-identical). The durable rule is: when introducing a new optional MilitaryState field that is initialized on every turn, expect the final_state_hash to drift, document the expected drift in the phase commit AND in the implemented report's verification section, and require an analyst panel (or scenario-comparison tool) to confirm the drift is purely additive. Do NOT panic at hash drift on additive shape changes; do not rebaseline silently either. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(operations): operation_opportunities substrate (LANE B Phase 1)` + Phase 4 verification.
**Equipment floors must be applied at every live consumer axis, not only at the scalar display axis (2026-05-01):** VRS had a correct `formation.equipment_decay` floor (0.60 after week 26), but routine `degradeEquipment` continued to push `composition.tank_condition.operational` and `composition.artillery_condition.operational` toward zero. Combat math and corps operation readiness consume condition fractions, so the scalar floor was true but gameplay still saw VRS as almost equipment-empty (`tank_op=1`, `art_op=14` at 188w n1599). Durable rule: when a canon floor exists for an effectiveness system, audit all parallel representations that consumers read (scalar, composition condition, raw counts, UI summaries, diagnostics) and apply the floor at the routine-decay owner, while leaving combat-loss owners free to destroy equipment. Applied in `[2026-05-01] fix(equipment): apply VRS decay floor to routine heavy-equipment condition`.

**Vitest protection suites must not depend on Unix-only shell tools (2026-05-01):** `tests/officer_config_consumers.test.ts` originally counted source read sites with `grep ... | wc -l`; that passed in Unix-like shells but failed on the repo's Windows/PowerShell environment, making a healthy force-quality milestone look broken. Durable rule: keep static source guards in-process with Node `fs`/`path` scanning (stable-sorted, cached source text) or through a repo-owned cross-platform helper. Use `rg` freely during human investigation, but do not make protection tests require Unix `grep`, `wc`, pipes, or shell-specific quoting. Applied in `[2026-05-01] test(force-quality): make officer_config consumer guard Windows-safe`.

**Work units should be autonomous milestone lanes, not 10-minute packets (2026-05-01):** Once a subsystem has enough evidence and plans, do not keep handing Claude single-seam tasks that require the user to ask for the next prompt every few minutes. Define a large lane with internal phases, phase commits, tests, run evidence, and explicit stop gates. Stop only for canon, sensitive-history, determinism failure, active file-ownership conflict, severe invariant break, or unresolved player-facing design meaning. Expected hash movement, test updates, diagnostics, reports, and phase commits are not stop gates. Codex should work non-overlapping architecture/review/product lanes in parallel and prepare the next milestone prompt before Claude returns. Applied in `[2026-05-01] docs(process): establish autonomous parallel workstreams`.

**Force-quality consumer trace pattern: officer_quality is live, faction_officer_maturity and capability_profile are decorative in war phase, war_exhaustion is uniform across factions in combat (2026-05-01):** When auditing whether a force-quality system is wired into late-war operations, separate the schema/writer side from the consumer side per signal. As of the 2026-05-01 evidence audit: (1) `formation.officer_quality` is read live by `combat_math.ts:458,544`, decremented by `attack_post_battle_effects.ts:62-66` and `brigade_reconstitution.ts:357`. (2) `state.military.faction_officer_maturity` is written by `officer_experience.ts:181-184` and never read in `src/sim/`. (3) `faction.capability_profile` is consumed only by `early_war/control_flip.ts:561,564` and `early_war/washington_agreement.ts:209-211` — zero war-phase combat, operation, commander, or readiness consumers. (4) `state.political.war_exhaustion` feeds a single global tempo multiplier (`combat_math.ts:1126-1132`, attacker-only [0.85, 1.0]) without faction asymmetry. Future force-quality audits must run a writer-vs-reader grep across `src/sim/combat/`, `src/sim/turn_phases/`, `src/sim/combat/commander/`, and `src/state/` before proposing tuning, otherwise the proposed lever may be decorative. Applied in `[2026-05-01] docs(audit): Force Quality Trajectory Evidence Audit — no behavior changes`.

**Engine convention: `*_BASE` constants like `COMBAT_GROWTH_BASE = 0.01` are sized as multipliers, not absolute rates (2026-05-01):** `src/sim/combat/officer_quality_update.ts` defines `COMBAT_GROWTH_BASE = 0.01` and `FRONTLINE_GROWTH_BASE = 0.005` and uses them in `growth = COMBAT_GROWTH_BASE * learningRate * (1.0 - quality * 0.5)`. The hardcoded fallback values (`RBiH=1.5`, `RS=0.7`, `HRHB=1.0`) are multiplier-shaped. Any timeline JSON override (`apr1992.json:386-411`: `RBiH=0.015`, `RS=0.007`, `HRHB=0.010`) is read into the same `learningRate` slot, so absolute-rate-shaped values produce a `1/COMBAT_GROWTH_BASE = 100×` suppression. The durable rule is: **timeline overrides for any growth-rate field must be authored as multipliers OR the schema must explicitly split into `*_abs` vs `*_mult` fields**. The same trap can recur for `FRONTLINE_GROWTH_BASE`, brain-drain rates, decay rates, or any future `state.military.war_timeline.*_config` block. Schema validation should reject out-of-range values when the units are ambiguous. Applied in `[2026-05-01] docs(audit): Force Quality Trajectory Evidence Audit — no behavior changes`.

**Calendar-week-driven curves are railroads — VRS brain drain is the canonical example (2026-05-01):** `officer_quality_update.ts:39-42` defines `VRS_BRAIN_DRAIN_START_WEEK = 40` and `VRS_BRAIN_DRAIN_RATE = 0.001`, applied at `:134-136` unconditionally to every RS active brigade after week 40. This is a calendar-driven decay that does not read pool depth, casualties, supply state, exhaustion, isolation, or any signal that would make the loss endogenous to mechanics. It violates the design rule already canonized in `CALIBRATION_MASTER.md` "Faction Doctrinal Arcs": faction arcs must emerge from mechanics, not hardcoded calendar curves. Use this as the worked example when classifying a behavior as railroad: if removing the calendar gate makes the behavior disappear entirely (rather than weaken or shift in time), it is a railroad and should be replaced with mechanic-coupled erosion. The fallback assumption that calendar floors are "safe" because they only affect one faction is wrong — they make late-war faction arcs immune to player/bot agency. Applied in `[2026-05-01] docs(audit): Force Quality Trajectory Evidence Audit — no behavior changes`.

**Two parallel "exhaustion" axes exist in AWWV — unify carefully or stay explicit (2026-05-01):** `state.political.war_exhaustion` is an integer score consumed by diplomacy / ceasefire / Washington Agreement / single combat tempo multiplier (`combat_math.ts:1126`, `command_friction.ts:36`, `early_war/bilateral_ceasefire.ts:75,79`, `early_war/washington_agreement.ts:142-143`). The "exhaustion" axis used for cohesion/morale penalties in militia mechanics is `militia_pool.committed/(committed+available)` and lives elsewhere. Future authors who reach for "exhaustion" in a combat or commander system must pick the right axis: combat fatigue and pool depletion belong on the militia-pool ratio; political will and diplomatic willingness belong on `political.war_exhaustion`. Do not unify the two without `/game-designer` sign-off; the diplomatic axis is intentionally deterministic-by-event and the pool axis is intentionally emergent. Applied in `[2026-05-01] docs(audit): Force Quality Trajectory Evidence Audit — no behavior changes`.

**Audit reproducibility: date-window comparisons must come from a single long-run scenario or accept that sibling scenarios diverge on `war_timeline` (2026-05-01):** The `apr1992_definitive_*.json` family currently disagrees on whether to bind `war_timeline: "apr1992"` and `init_officers: "apr1992"`. `apr1992_definitive_40w.json` and `apr1992_definitive_188w.json` bind both; `apr1992_definitive_104w.json` binds neither and silently falls back to the hardcoded multipliers in `officer_quality_update.ts:46-49`. As long as that drift exists, comparing a 40w run to a 104w run to a 188w run for force-quality trajectory is comparing three different engine configurations. The durable rule is: **for force-quality date-window audits, walk a single long-run scenario (188w) at multiple weeks** and capture saves at the audit checkpoints, OR explicitly close the scenario-config drift first. The 5-row diagnostic table in `tools/diagnostics/_force_quality_run_output.md` documents the corpus this audit used. Applied in `[2026-05-01] docs(audit): Force Quality Trajectory Evidence Audit — no behavior changes`.

**5th Corps is an isolated-pocket family, not a normal late-war ARBiH corps with a buff (2026-05-01):** The Bihac / 5th Corps arc must be modeled as pocket survival and hardening first, then post-Storm/Oluja exploitation. Until the wider Croatian-theater rupture, 5th Corps is surrounded, APWB-politically pressured, and constrained by pocket logistics; its improvement comes from local leadership, recon-sabotage, deception, captured equipment, and survival under Una/Breza/Pauk pressure. Sana 95 should require pocket survival plus the theater-opening event and live support/staging readiness; it must not be a date-only launch or a generic ARBiH 1995 attack multiplier. Grmec and Sana should preserve overextension/counterpressure risk even when successful. Applied in `[2026-05-01] docs(plan): late-war 5th Corps opportunity family design`.

**Operation opportunity review belongs to Army HQ, not the map-local operations snapshot (2026-05-01):** Historical operation opportunities should surface as command dossiers in Army HQ, with the tactical map used for footprint visualization and the existing operation lifecycle used for execution. Do not put primary Approve / Delay / Redirect / Under-resource / Decline controls in the map-local `OperationsPanel`; that panel is a field snapshot and HQ handoff. The dossier should show player-safe prerequisite chips, force-quality trait bands, staff recommendation, and sensitive-history guardrails without raw OSIDs or hidden enemy rosters. Applied in `[2026-05-01] docs(ui): design operation opportunity review surface`.

**Force-quality packets must expose trait evidence and preserve counterfactual agency (2026-05-01):** When changing the ARBiH/VRS full-war arc, do not hide the effect inside a global combat multiplier or date-forced historical outcome. Every force-quality change should name the trait it affects (`operation_readiness`, `staging_reliability`, `axis_coordination`, `support_delivery`, `failure_recovery`, `reserve_response`, or `collapse_susceptibility`), expose that trait in diagnostics/AAR/reporting, and prove it across date windows. ARBiH should become better at launching and delivering operations when institutional prerequisites are present; VRS should lose sustained coherence while remaining locally dangerous. If a run preserves or destroys the prerequisites differently from history, the outcome should diverge rather than be dragged back to painted truth. Applied in `[2026-05-01] docs(architecture): define force-quality operation contract`.

**Professionalization belongs in operation delivery, not raw combat inflation (2026-05-01):** The late-war force-quality research shows that the desired ARBiH 1995 arc is not "more tanks = map flips." ARBiH/Federation improvement should surface through corps-level readiness, staging reliability, multi-axis coordination, support thresholds, failure recovery, and capture delivery. VRS decline should erode sustained offensive coherence, reserve response, replacement quality, and morale stability under simultaneous pressure while preserving prepared-position defense and local counterattacks. Before tuning, audit whether `officer_config.learning_rate` units, `capability_profile`, and `faction_officer_maturity` are actually wired into war-phase operations; do not hide a decorative or mis-scaled trajectory behind scripted historical operations. Applied in `[2026-05-01] docs(research): propose force-quality trajectory levers`.

**A static late-war map is force-trajectory evidence, not automatically an operation-content gap (2026-05-01):** If April 1994 / April 1995 / October 1995 runs lack organic territorial movement unless historical operations are injected as naked calendar scripts, do not classify that only as "missing ops." The deeper calibration question is whether the engine is producing the full-war force-quality arcs already stated in CALIBRATION_MASTER: VRS professional -> degraded-but-dangerous, ARBiH rabble -> professional, HRHB capable -> overstretched. Treat late-war opportunity content as the test harness for capability, not the substitute for capability. The first packet should audit officer learning/brain drain, cohesion/morale, equipment maintenance, exhaustion, operation-readiness gates, corps coordination, and commander doctrine across 40w/104w/156w/183w before tuning. Applied in `[2026-05-01] docs(calibration): open force-quality trajectory issue`.

**Late-war operations should be opportunity proposals, not calendar-forced scripts (2026-05-01):** Date-specific painted targets (`apr1994`, `apr1995`, `oct1995`) are health references, not destiny. The late-war op research catalog showed that trying to add Krivaja/Stupcanica/Mistral/Sana as naked turn-gated scripts exposes useful engine seams, but it is not the final product model. AWWV's player is an army/corps/political decision-maker, not a brigade driver, so post-Washington Agreement operations should surface as historically grounded opportunities with prerequisites: political authorization, corps readiness, staging access, logistics, commander confidence, weather/season, alliance context, and enemy weakness. The player/bot can approve, delay, redirect, under-resource, or decline; normal command, staging, supply, combat, morale, commander, and AAR systems then decide the result. Failed operations such as Breza 94, Pauk/Shield pressure, Orasje, Zvezda 94, and the early Vlasic attempt are first-class design content because pressure without guaranteed success is how the engine proves health. Applied in `[2026-05-01] docs(research): late-war operation opportunity catalog after Washington Agreement`.

**Multi-corps operations require state-wide brigade-op lookup, not corps-local (2026-05-01):** When a triggered operation has axes from more than one corps, the engine pushes the op onto `primary_corps.active_operations` only (`triggered_operations.ts:checkTriggeredOperations`). A brigade belonging to a secondary axis-corps then calls `findBrigadeOperation(brigade.own_corps_cmd, brigade_id)` (corps-local lookup) and gets `null` — the op physically lives in the primary corps's array, not theirs. The brigade-AI hot path in `bot_brigade_ai_osid.ts` therefore sees `isActiveSectorOperationParticipant=false` for cross-corps brigades, and they fall through every operation-aware evaluation in `bot_brigade_eval_attack.ts` (planning column-march, execution attack-launch, recovery posture). All four legacy TRIGGERED_OPS plus newer single-corps additions don't expose the bug because they are single-corps; the first multi-corps triggered op (`Operation Mistral 2`, `hvo_main_staff` primary + `hvo_tomislavgrad` axis 2) surfaced it. **The durable rule is: any code path that resolves "what op is this brigade in?" must search state-wide when the brigade may participate in a multi-corps op. Use `findBrigadeOperationAnywhere(state, brigadeId)` (which iterates `Object.keys(corps_command).sort(strictCompare)` for determinism) as a fallback after the corps-local fast path. Op injection and op cleanup remain primary-corps-local because the op physically lives in one place; only brigade-side resolution needs the state-wide fallback.** Applied in `[2026-05-01] fix(operations): multi-corps operation visibility in brigade-AI hot path`.

**Painted targets must use the simulation controller universe, not every exposed geometry feature (2026-05-01):** The painted-control UI loads `operational_settlements.geojson`, which currently has 744 features, while scenario `final_save.json` / `political_controllers` and the Jan 1993 painted baseline use a 712-OSID evaluation universe. If the user paints geometry-only OSIDs, target totals drift (713/714) and `compare_painted_vs_sim` counts false sim-missing mismatches. The durable rule is: built-in painted targets must share the Jan 1993/current-sim key universe until the engine's political-controller substrate is intentionally expanded; add tests that compare built-in target key sets, and treat geometry-only exposed features as a separate map/substrate backlog, not scenario-date variation. Applied in `[2026-05-01] data(calibration): complete date-specific painted-control target set`.

**Painter UIs that persist calibration targets must bind visible selection to loaded state, not only to save path (2026-04-30):** The first painted-control target tool listed built-in targets alphabetically (`apr1994` first) while `init()` auto-loaded `jan1993` without setting the dropdown back to Jan 1993. The visible target and `state.target` diverged, so a user could think they were saving April 1994 while the POST still went to `/api/target/jan1993`. The durable rule is: when a local authoring tool writes deterministic calibration data, `loadTarget(id)` must be the only owner that changes both the loaded control map and save target; dropdown changes should load the selected target (with an unsaved-change guard), and the dropdown value must be forced to the loaded id. Applied in `[2026-04-30] fix(tooling): sync painted-target dropdown with save target`.

**Late-war scenarios need date-specific painted truth before calibration conclusions (2026-04-30):** Comparing an April 1994, April 1995, or October 1995 run against `painted_control_jan1993.json` is only a smoke check; it is not a fair historical yardstick. The correct workflow is: create/select the painted target for the scenario date (`jan1993`, `apr1994`, `apr1995`, `oct1995`) using `npm run paint:control`, then run `tools/compare_painted_vs_sim.cjs <run_dir> --target <date>`. Engine health should be judged by invariants, causality, determinism, and date-correct target fit, not by chasing one stale calibration map across the whole war. Applied in `[2026-04-30] tooling(calibration): add date-specific painted-control target painter`.

**Once an artifactual amplifier is closed, the residual is usually structural multi-owner — stop at plan rather than reach for a second bounded fix (2026-04-30):** After `[2026-04-30] fix(combat): block cross-corps reconstitution drift` closed the cross-corps drift cascade (Goražde detector amplifier), 188w n1587 evidence showed the residual DRINA/HERZEGOVINA RBiH-overgain was the convolution of (1) OOB structural under-garrisoning of vrs_herzegovina (8 brigades / 92 OSIDs), (2) 6/8 vrs_herzegovina brigades scripted forward via Op Visegrad / Op Foča / Op Herzegovina Consolidation, (3) bot brigade-distribution AI concentrating brigades at the highest-pressure forward OSID (kalinovik:obalj t21–t67 vs arbih_442nd_mountain) with no rear-garrison reservation rule, and (4) defender-attrition pattern bleeding cohesion across "decisive_victory" outcomes. Each of those is a separate owner with a separate sign-off. **The durable rule is: when investigating a long-run believability gap, distinguish artifactual amplifiers (single-owner, closable bounded) from structural residuals (multi-owner, requiring an implementation-plan packet rather than another bounded fix). The temptation after closing an amplifier is to keep drilling for "one more bounded fix"; resist it when the evidence shows ≥3 separate ownership lanes. The right product of such a packet is a four-packet roadmap with named experts and required sign-offs, not a guarded code change at one of the four owners.** Applied in `[2026-04-30] investigation(combat): DRINA / HERZEGOVINA RBiH-overgain root-cause trace — stop-at-plan`.

**Brigade reconstitution must respect corps-territory ownership, not just displacement-volume (2026-04-30):** The displaced-population scan in `findRefugeeMunicipality` correctly captures where the population went, but the displaced population's largest receiving municipality is rarely in the brigade's own corps territory. The 188w n1582→n1585 evidence: vrs_drina/vrs_herzegovina brigades whose homes (Foča/Čajniče/Višegrad/Kalinovik) fell to RBiH got reconstituted at `op:banja_luka:banja_luka_2` because Banja Luka was the largest RS displacement recipient — but Banja Luka is `vrs_1st_krajina` territory, not vrs_herzegovina/vrs_drina territory. The reconstituted brigades kept their original `corps_id` and physically sat inside the wrong corps's sectors, breaking sector ownership and producing emergent westward drift that depleted the Goražde siege presence and amplified RS late-war morale collapse. **The durable rule is: any brigade lifecycle owner that places brigades back on the map (reconstitution, retreat fallback, displacement) must constrain placement to OSIDs owned by the brigade's own corps via `corps_front_sectors[*].territory_osids`. If no qualifying OSID exists, the brigade stays destroyed (corps disband when their territory is fully overrun — historically accurate). Cross-corps absorption with `corps_id` reassignment is a Path C feature requiring `/historian` + `/game-designer` sign-off; do NOT add it as a default fallback.** Applied in `[2026-04-30] fix(combat): block cross-corps reconstitution drift in findRefugeeMunicipality`.

**Cohesion-floored brigades fall out of the main_effort tier even when motorized — fitness_offense math is the second gate after N1297 (2026-04-26):** The post-Option-J 188w empirical result showed N1297 unblocked but HRHB still silent, because the tier-classification step (`force_eval.ts:128`) requires both `equip_priority ≥ 2` AND `fitness_offense ≥ 0.4`. With HRHB cohesion floored at 28-30 by siege attrition, motorized Vitezovi (pers 2200, coh 28) computed fitness_offense ≈ 0.296 → garrison tier → corps reads tier_counts.main_effort=0 → N1297 caps at defensive → `plan.ts:705` blocks plan creation before ever reading `campaign_offensive_targets`. Option K Fix A inserts an elite override (`is_elite + motorized/mechanized → main_effort regardless of fitness_offense`) so organizational cadre buffers against cohesion erosion in the runtime model. The durable rule: **the offensive-emergence chain is stance(N1297 + faction overrides) → tier classification(equipment + fitness) → plan.ts(stance gate) → emit.ts(target read) → CorpsOperation**. A fix at one layer can be defeated by the next layer's filter. Empirical verification against `state.military.corps_command[corpsId].commander_state.decision_trace` (which already persists per turn) is the cheapest way to find which layer is binding before designing a fix. Applied in `[2026-04-26] fix(commander): elite + motorized/mechanized count main_effort regardless of fitness threshold (issue #20, Option K Fix A — partial)`.

**The N1297 organizational readiness gate is one of several stance→action gates, not THE HRHB offensive gate (2026-04-24):** Issue #13 framed N1297 as the binding constraint on HRHB offensive emergence. Option J (#19) unblocked it via 6 citation-backed OOB tier promotions (HRHB: Vitezovi + 4th Guard Sinovi Posavine; RS: 1st Semberija; RBiH: 9th/7th Vitezka/4th Muslim Liberation — all light_infantry→motorized at priority 2). 188w empirical result: HRHB=0 attacks / 1 op across 188 weeks, despite 86/188 weeks in offensive stance. The binding constraint moved to campaign-plan target generation: `hvo_tomislavgrad` and `hvo_northwest_bosnia` face RS sectors but `FACTION_STRATEGIES.HRHB.offensive_objectives` lists only central-Bosnia ARBiH targets they can't reach across their fronts, while `hvo_central_bosnia` (RBiH-HRHB-war gate) and `hvo_southeast_herzegovina` (Herzegovina blanket lock) are stance-locked defensive. RBiH unblocked cleanly (142 attacks, 25 ops) because `FACTION_STRATEGIES.RBiH.offensive_objectives` includes Sarajevo-breakout targets arbih_1st_corps can see + the late-war counteroffensive clause (`bot_corps_stance.ts:188-199`) fires at ≥25% ownership. The durable rule: OOB tier is necessary but not sufficient for corps offensive emergence; the full chain is stance (N1297 + faction rules) → campaign plan target seeding (army HQ gathering → `briefing.campaign_offensive_targets`) → plan.ts `getPriorityTargetSet()` non-empty → `emit.ts buildOffensiveTargets()` non-empty → sector launch. Applied in `[2026-04-24] oob(equipment_class): promote 6 elite brigades for N1297 readiness gate (issue #13, Option J — partial)`; Option K (#20) tracks the campaign-plan wiring fix.

**Retire a side lane once only polish remains, or the roadmap will keep reopening closed ownership work (2026-04-16):** Warroom shell recovery stayed on the active side-lane board even after the host path, test story, and loaded-game room owner were already brought into alignment. The durable rule is: once a lane's remaining work is modal polish or presentation parity rather than truth/ownership debt, retire the side lane explicitly and say what backlog remains so future packets do not waste time re-auditing a closed seam. Applied in `[2026-04-16] docs(plan): retire Warroom shell recovery as an active pre-0.9 lane`.

**Host-side shell entry should converge on the same loaded-game room owner as post-load updates (2026-04-16):** Warroom already promoted loaded saves into the React `?view=warroom` shell through `applyGameStateFromJson(...)`, but the separate `showScreen('none')` branch still dropped the user through `showWarroomScene()` and revived the legacy desk as if it were the primary loaded-game owner. The durable rule is: when a shell has a canonical loaded-game room owner, menu re-entry and post-load entry must converge on the same host helper instead of letting one branch resurrect a demoted fallback surface. Applied in `[2026-04-16] fix(ui): keep loaded Warroom entry on the React shell path`.

**If a negotiation consequence constant already exists, the resolution owner must actually spend it (2026-04-16):** `PEACE_PLAN_REJECTION_SUPPORT_COST` already lived in `patron_pressure.ts`, but peace-plan rejection was only changing override authority and credibility, leaving patron `support_level` untouched. The durable rule is: when a consequence constant names a live relationship penalty, wire it at the canonical resolution owner instead of letting it sit as decorative design residue. Applied in `[2026-04-16] fix(sim): make peace-plan rejection reduce patron support`.

**CI tests that pass on Windows and fail on Linux have five recurring root causes (2026-04-16):** (1) Path separator hardcoding — `\\` in expected strings or `F:/repo/` absolute paths; fix with `path.resolve()` + `.replace(/\\/g, '/')`. (2) Baked artifact drift — snapshots built on Windows produce different bytes on Linux; fix by rebuilding per-platform in CI. (3) Missing local data — tests that load `./runs/` scenario outputs not in git; fix with `it.skipIf(!exists)`. (4) Scenario tests in the fast job — full scenario runs timeout at vitest's default 5s; fix by splitting CI into fast → scenario → baseline pipeline. (5) Platform-specific assertions — Windows path tests meaningless on POSIX; fix with `process.platform` guards. Pattern: when CI runs on a different OS than dev, every test that touches file paths, baked artifacts, or long-running scenarios needs cross-platform awareness from day one. Applied in `[2026-04-16] fix(ci): make Baseline Regression workflow cross-platform green`.

**Proof files should not preserve dead blocker stories after later packets close them (2026-04-16):** The endgame proof ladder already had direct `VerdictScreen` mount and interaction proof, but older proof-file headers still described dual-React as if full mount were still blocked. The durable rule is: once a later packet closes an infrastructure blocker, refresh the older proof comments and add a small guard test so the suite keeps describing its current proof layer instead of preserving obsolete failure context. Applied in `[2026-04-16] docs(test): retire stale dual-react blocker language from endgame proof files`.

**When one campaign-start artifact is canonical, browser/dev fallback should consume that artifact before inventing a second birth state (2026-04-16):** Desktop `apr_1992` startup had already been hardened onto the baked `data/derived/startup/apr_1992_initial_save.json` snapshot, but Warroom/browser mode still built a turn-30 mock state as its normal fallback. The durable rule is: if a committed startup save already owns the product start state, alternate shells should deserialize that same artifact first and only keep hand-built mock construction as an explicit emergency guard for missing assets. Applied in `[2026-04-16] fix(ui+docs): unify Warroom fallback startup with the baked apr_1992 snapshot`.

**When the product has only one live campaign-start key, the shell should not preserve a fake scenario-selection owner for nostalgia (2026-04-16):** AWWV's desktop entry flow had already converged on one sanctioned live start, `apr_1992`, but Warroom still made the player step through a second overlay to "select" that same lone option. The durable rule is: if the only meaningful user choice is faction, launch the campaign directly from that choice and keep scenario selection out of the shell until there are multiple live starts again. Applied in `[2026-04-16] fix(ui): collapse Warroom new-campaign flow onto the sole live scenario`.

**Roadmap and plan docs must distinguish live product truth from planned or historical design intent (2026-04-16):** AWWV now has enough pulled-forward work that old milestone plans and GUI design docs can become misleading even when they were reasonable once. The durable rule is: when a design/plan document is still useful but partly aspirational, update the current-truth baseline and authority docs around it instead of letting old "not implemented" or "next lane" language survive past the code. In practice, desktop startup docs must say the live shell consumes the baked `apr_1992` artifact, roadmap status must name the narrower live replay gap (`playback/consumer absent`) instead of saying replay is wholly absent, and milestone plans must acknowledge partially landed slices like `historicalComparison`, `dynamic_sections`, and `ghost_when`. Applied in `[2026-04-16] docs(test+roadmap): align desktop startup and v0.9 plan truth with landed repo state`.

**Replay truth should distinguish harness artifacts from live product playback, not collapse them into "exists" or "doesn't exist" (2026-04-16):** The save/load hardening docs had drifted into two opposite falsehoods at once: the desktop IPC contract still advertised replay loader endpoints that do not exist, while other authority docs had swung too far and said no replay code existed anywhere in the repo. The durable rule is to name the seam precisely: harness-side replay artifacts and replay-determinism code can exist without implying a live replay consumer/playback owner in the product. Applied in `[2026-04-16] docs(test+eng): make replay surface truth explicit and guard it`.

**Birth-time system-state initialization must normalize the substrate it depends on before seeding persistent fields (2026-04-16):** Recruitment-time heavy-equipment seeding looked like a tiny backfill helper, but it silently over-granted heavy access because `getEffectiveHeavyEquipmentAccess(undefined)` falls back to `1` when embargo profiles have not yet been normalized. The durable rule is: if a birth-time initializer persists system-owned state derived from another substrate, initialize or normalize that substrate first, otherwise the birth seam can drift away from the later canonical turn-phase result. Applied in `[2026-04-16] fix(state+test): close recruited-state gap and retire final node-test residue`.

**When golden baselines fail, compare against a pristine `HEAD` worktree before blaming the in-flight packet (2026-04-16):** The migrated baseline test first failed on `apr1992_52w`, but a detached `HEAD` worktree reproduced the same mismatch byte-for-byte. That proved the manifest was already stale and separated true packet drift from pre-existing artifact debt. The durable rule is: before refreshing committed baseline hashes after a large cleanup packet, rerun the same baseline on pristine `HEAD`; if both sides match, document the baseline refresh as stale-manifest repair rather than attributing it to the packet’s behavior change. Applied in `[2026-04-16] fix(state+test): close recruited-state gap and retire final node-test residue`.

**Process-backed audit and artifact contracts can still live on Vitest once their time budget is made explicit (2026-04-16):** `audit_state_of_game_determinism`, `scenario_golden_baselines_h2_3`, and `scenario_harness_smoke_h1_4` all touch the filesystem or child processes, but none of them needed a separate runner once Vitest timeouts were made explicit and discovery already had honest fast vs scenario slices. The durable rule is: do not treat child-process spawn, report generation, or baseline compare mode as automatic `node:test` sanctity. First see whether the contract is still just deterministic in-repo proof with a longer runtime budget. Applied in `[2026-04-16] fix(state+test): close recruited-state gap and retire final node-test residue`.

**When a migration uncovers a real owner gap, fix the owner gap and then move the original harness back onto the canonical lane (2026-04-16):** `systems_2_3_4_7_9_10_acceptance_gates.test.ts` was right to stay behind for one packet, because the first migration attempt exposed three week-1 recruited formations that were landing without `equipment_state` and `doctrine_state`. But once recruited formations were seeded with those states at birth, the honest next step was to migrate the acceptance gate into Vitest instead of preserving it as a permanent museum piece. The durable rule is: use migration attempts to surface substrate truth gaps, repair the truth at the owner seam, and then let the original test rejoin the canonical runner. Applied in `[2026-04-16] fix(state+test): close recruited-state gap and retire final node-test residue`.

**Scenario-heavy harness helpers belong in the Vitest scenario slice once discovery names them explicitly (2026-04-16):** `runSensitivityHarness(...)`, `runScenario(...)`, and similar real-run helpers all carry runtime cost, but they do not automatically deserve a second runner once discovery already has a scenario lane. The durable rule is: if a file is a real scenario proof but still lives fully in-process, teach discovery to classify the helper and move the suite into the scenario slice rather than keeping `node:test` around by habit. Applied in `[2026-04-16] fix(state+test): close recruited-state gap and retire final node-test residue`.

**Scenario-run contracts belong in the Vitest scenario slice once discovery already owns that lane (2026-04-15):** `scenario_activity_diagnostics_h1_7`, `scenario_baseline_ops_h1_9`, `scenario_bots_determinism_h2_4`, `scenario_determinism_h1_1`, and `scenario_probe_compare_h1_8` all run real scenarios, but that no longer makes them `node:test` architecture by itself. The durable rule is: when discovery already has an explicit scenario slice keyed off real runner calls, move ordinary scenario-run contracts into Vitest and let the slice absorb the runtime cost instead of preserving a second runner by inertia. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate scenario residue onto vitest`.

**Umbrella acceptance-gate files should stay behind when they surface a live owner gap instead of a runner-only issue (2026-04-15):** `systems_2_3_4_7_9_10_acceptance_gates.test.ts` looked like another migration candidate, but the first Vitest run exposed a real contract problem: three active formations in the `apr1992_phase_ii_4w` save lack `equipment_state` and `doctrine_state`. The durable rule is: if a migration attempt uncovers a live owner-truth gap, do not quietly relax the assertion just to finish the runner cleanup. Keep the file out of the packet and treat it as a separate substrate audit or bug lane. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate scenario residue onto vitest`.

**Core determinism scans should exclude external provider wrappers that only measure wall-clock latency (2026-04-15):** `determinism_static_scan_r1_5.test.ts` is meant to guard the deterministic simulation pipeline, not every integration wrapper under `src/`. The Anthropic SDK adapter legitimately uses `Date.now()` to measure request latency, which is telemetry on a non-deterministic external boundary rather than simulation truth. The durable rule is: keep the static scan strict for engine/state/scenario code, but carve out narrow provider-wrapper exclusions when the time usage is observational and never serialized into deterministic artifacts. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate bootstrap and determinism residue onto vitest`.

**Scenario-summary fixtures should assert current deterministic shape, not old monotonic-pressure folklore (2026-04-15):** `sim_scenario.test.ts` still assumed the seeded pressure metric had to increase turn-over-turn, but the current owner truth is simply that the summary is deterministic, timestamp-free, and includes the expected pressure edges. The durable rule is: when a helper/test proves deterministic scenario summaries, lock the stable output shape and canonical edge membership instead of preserving older trend claims that no longer belong to the owner contract. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate bootstrap and determinism residue onto vitest`.

**Bounded filesystem-backed contracts still belong on Vitest when they only prove deterministic temp-path persistence (2026-04-15):** `campaign_unlock.test.ts` and `data_prereq_check_h1_2.test.ts` both touch the filesystem, but neither owns a process harness, scenario run, or artifact-comparison gate. The durable rule is: if a test only writes deterministic throwaway files under a temp base and proves one in-process owner contract, move it to Vitest instead of treating any `fs` call as automatic `node:test` residue. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate knowledge and bounded fs residue contracts onto vitest`.

**Small helper bands should move together once the true keepers are clearly CLI/data/scenario gates (2026-04-15):** The next residue sweep worked because the clean helper group was obvious: knowledge-indexing/validation, decoration evaluation, enclave integrity, and two bounded persistence/prereq checks. The durable rule is: once the remaining `node:test` list is dominated by calibration, scenario, artifact, and external-data harnesses, pull the adjacent helper band across in one packet so the leftover runner debt reads like a real keeper inventory instead of a mixed bag of tiny stragglers. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate knowledge and bounded fs residue contracts onto vitest`.

**Content-truth refresh should come before deciding a suite is a harness keeper (2026-04-15):** `jna_phantom_brigades.test.ts` looked like “hard residue,” but the real problem was not runner semantics; it was stale content truth. The durable rule is: when a suite’s failures are driven by changed catalog data (counts, withdrawal turns, locations, handoff flags), refresh the test to current owner truth first. Only after that should you decide whether it still needs `node:test` semantics. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): refresh JNA phantom truth and migrate the suite onto vitest`.

**Phantom withdrawal tests should split no-handoff and handoff behavior explicitly (2026-04-15):** The JNA phantom substrate now contains both equipment-handoff and no-handoff phantoms, plus an HV phantom family. The durable rule is: do not write one blended withdrawal story that assumes every phantom distributes equipment. Prove one representative no-handoff case, one representative handoff case, and the shared operation-cleanup behavior separately. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): refresh JNA phantom truth and migrate the suite onto vitest`.

**Mixed runner residue should be split at the scenario boundary instead of dragging pure contracts into the slow lane (2026-04-15):** `phase10_ops_fatigue.test.ts` contained two different owners: pure in-process fatigue/commitment rules and one scenario determinism proof. The durable rule is: when one file mixes fast deterministic contracts with a true scenario runner, split it into a fast vitest file plus a scenario-slice proof file so discovery and review both reflect the actual seam. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): split fatigue scenario proof and migrate siege contracts onto vitest`.

**Scenario fixtures must use real faction strategy owners once they touch commander/corridor logic (2026-04-15):** The old fatigue scenario proof used fake factions `A` / `B`, which worked only until the scenario runner touched corridor-opportunity logic and looked up `FACTION_STRATEGIES[faction]`. The durable rule is: if a test runs enough real scenario code to cross into faction strategy, use canonical faction ids (`RBiH`, `RS`, `HRHB`) or explicitly isolate the path, because fake placeholder factions become fixture bugs rather than useful abstraction. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): split fatigue scenario proof and migrate siege contracts onto vitest`.

**Siege multiplier tests should assert the live floor-after-multiplier contract, not stale exact multiples of a pre-floored baseline (2026-04-15):** `runPoolPopulation(...)` computes `Math.floor(raw_strength * ... * siegeMult)`, so full-siege and partial-siege outputs can differ by a small bounded rounding step from `baseAvailable * 3` or `Math.floor(baseAvailable * 1.5)`. The durable rule is: when a multiplier is applied before final flooring, write the test against the bounded rounding window the owner really guarantees rather than against lucky exact integers from an older fixture. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): split fatigue scenario proof and migrate siege contracts onto vitest`.

**Test discovery must classify test entrypoints, not helper modules under `tests/` (2026-04-15):** The discovery owner in `tools/test/discover_test_files.mjs` used to treat every `.ts` file under `tests/` as a runnable contract, which quietly inflated the `node:test` residue list with helpers like `tests/test_factories.ts`. The durable rule is: discovery should only classify actual test entrypoints (`*.test.ts`), then decide vitest vs `node:test` from the imports inside those files. That keeps residue accounting honest and prevents helper modules from masquerading as runner debt. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate pure state and narrative residue contracts onto vitest`.

**Pure state/narrative residue should move together once it only proves deterministic in-process owners (2026-04-15):** `consolidation_scoring`, `hv_integration`, `sustainability`, `sarajevo_exception`, `production_facilities_a3`, and `war_stories` all looked like leftover `node:test` debt, but each file only exercised deterministic helper logic or in-process state mutation with no scenario harness, artifact management, or process-isolation need. The durable rule is: when an adjacent band is just pure state/narrative owner truth, migrate it together to vitest and update discovery representatives so the remaining `node:test` list reflects the genuinely hard keepers instead of historical runner inertia. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate pure state and narrative residue contracts onto vitest`.

**End-state cleanup should repair owner-path and schema drift before migrating the shell, then keep only validator/pipeline truth that is still unique (2026-04-15):** `end_state.test.ts` looked like a foundational treaty shell, but part of it was mutating a dead top-level `end_state` field while `validateEndState(...)` now reads `state.political.end_state`, and another part still fabricated a schema version the canonical serializer no longer accepts. The durable rule is: when refreshing an old end-state shell, first repair it to current owner paths and canonical serialization shape, then keep only the validator and turn-pipeline short-circuit truth that still belongs there; do not drag duplicated treaty-apply and snapshot assertions forward when newer treaty application suites already own them. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): refresh end-state shell contracts onto vitest`.

**Treaty-acceptance cleanup should preserve hard gate truth and trim duplicate Brcko smoke instead of migrating every historical clause as-is (2026-04-15):** `acceptance_constraints` and `acceptance_brcko_completeness` still owned real treaty-acceptance contracts - competence bundles, forbidden holders/factions, Brcko unresolved rejection, and deterministic rejection ordering - but they also carried generic “accepted when Brcko is present” and military-only smoke that newer treaty/Brcko application suites already proved more directly. The durable rule is: when cleaning old treaty-acceptance files, keep the unique constraint-owner logic and trim the low-value duplicate smoke instead of dragging the whole historical file boundary into the new harness unchanged. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate treaty acceptance gating contracts onto vitest`.

**Scenario reporting suites should split into report-shape Vitest owners and rare harness keepers, not migrate blindly as a block (2026-04-15):** The scenario reporting band contained both pure/control-shape contracts (`control_change_attribution`, `scenario_end_report_army_strengths`, `scenario_reporting_contracts`) and lighter scenario-run report-artifact contracts (`scenario_control_change_attribution_contract`, `scenario_end_report_h1_5`, `scenario_failure_reporting_h1_5_1`) that still fit comfortably in Vitest once given explicit timeouts. But `scenario_vrs_operation_proof.test.ts` remains a different creature: a multi-run artifact/determinism proof whose value is precisely that it behaves like a harness gate. The durable rule is: migrate reporting/report-shape suites to the canonical Vitest lane, then name any remaining harness-shaped keeper explicitly instead of leaving it behind by accident. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate scenario reporting contracts onto vitest`.

**Army-strength reporting owns live frontline assignments only; reserves and legacy aliases must not pad `assigned_brigades` (2026-04-15):** `computeArmyStrengthsSummary(...)` derives `front_assignment_counts_by_faction` from `buildFrontlineAssignedFormationSet(state)`, which in turn follows live corps-sector frontline truth. Reserve brigades and stale `brigade_front_assignment` aliases are not part of that owner surface. The durable rule is: when a report reads current frontline assignment truth, correct the test to the live owner rather than preserving older prose that counted sector reserves as if they were frontline-committed. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate scenario reporting contracts onto vitest`.

**Sector and movement residue files should keep live helper truth and retire stale integration theater once stronger sector-owner suites exist (2026-04-15):** `corps_front_sectors_multi`, `sector_coverage_defense`, and `osid_column_movement` had drifted into mixed files where a few real helper/pathing contracts sat beside broad integration assertions built on dead top-level state shapes and pre-hardening sector semantics. The durable rule is: keep the still-unique helper truth, repair fixture ownership to canonical nested state, and retire the stale integration block when stronger sector-truth suites already own that behavior. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): refresh sector and movement residue contracts onto vitest`.

**Refresh stale mega-files to their still-unique contracts instead of preserving dead launch/lifecycle lore (2026-04-15):** `sector_offensive.test.ts` looked like one more runner migration, but most of its failing assertions were not bugs - they were fossils from before planning-duration caps, preparation readiness, idle-recovery tightening, and dedicated operation-proof suites landed. The durable rule is: when an old mega-file overlaps newer targeted owners, keep only the clauses it still uniquely proves and retire the duplicate stale expectations instead of cosmetically dragging them forward. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): refresh sector offensive contract tests onto vitest`.

**Operation injection and anomaly contracts belong on vitest when they only prove in-process lifecycle truth (2026-04-15):** `pre_planned_operations`, `triggered_operations`, `multi_axis_operations`, `operation_birth_anomaly_contract`, and `orphan_operation_brigades` all exercise the operation substrate in-process: catalog shape, queue injection, axis helpers, and anomaly classification. The durable rule is: move that adjacent band together to vitest when it does not own scenario hashes or run-artifact comparison, and keep the stale lifecycle mega-file (`sector_offensive.test.ts`) out of the packet until its expectations are refreshed to HEAD. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate operation injection and anomaly contracts onto vitest`.

**Identity and migration ownership tests belong in the fast vitest lane when they only prove deserialization and canonicalization truth (2026-04-15):** `identity_migration` and `migration_nested_ownership` looked historical because they lived in `node:test`, but both files only exercise in-process deserialization, nested-owner rescue, faction canonicalization, and default materialization. The durable rule is: when a migration suite is pure JSON hydration and schema-owner truth, move the adjacent pair together to vitest and pin them in fast discovery instead of treating old runner choice as architecture. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate identity and nested migration contracts onto vitest`.

**Scenario-startup contracts can migrate to vitest, but long runners need explicit timeouts once they leave `node:test` (2026-04-15):** The startup/init cluster (`scenario_init_control_*`, `scenario_init_formations`, `scenario_displacement_census_seeding`, `scenario_must_hold_contract`, `scenario_no_initial_brigades`) proved to be canonical vitest material, but the longer scenario-run members immediately hit Vitest's default 5s ceiling even though the contracts themselves were still correct. The durable rule is: if a scenario-derived suite is still just startup/reporting/shape truth and not a black-box calibration gate, move it to vitest and set an explicit per-test timeout instead of treating the old runner as architecture. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate scenario startup contracts onto vitest`.

**Combat-support fixture helpers must write canonical nested owners, not dead top-level aliases (2026-04-15):** The morale and lifecycle residue files looked like simple runner migrations until Vitest exposed that their fabricated `GameState` was still writing formations, encirclement, and political control into top-level or override-stomped locations the live engine no longer reads. The durable rule is: when a helper fabricates combat/support `GameState`, merge nested `state.military.*` and `state.political.*` partitions explicitly, or the suite will start proving its own fake defaults instead of the real owner path. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate combat-support residue contracts onto vitest`.

**Pure institutional-state families should move together once they only prove deterministic tables and formulae (2026-04-15):** Competence valuation tables, competence allocation treaty rules, organizational-penetration seeding, and the derived organizational-penetration formula all belong to one political/institutional truth band. The durable rule is: when a domain family is just deterministic data tables, validation, and formula logic, move the adjacent files together to vitest rather than leaving a tiny `node:test` island behind. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate institutional competence and organizational-penetration contracts onto vitest`.

**Recruitment test helpers must merge nested `military` and `political` overrides instead of discarding them (2026-04-15):** A test helper that spreads `...overrides` before rebuilding default nested objects can quietly drop the very `military.militia_pools` and `political.political_controllers` fixtures the recruitment tests think they are exercising. The durable rule is: when a helper fabricates canonical `GameState`, merge nested partitions explicitly, or the test will start proving its own defaults instead of the intended seam. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate recruitment and militia contracts onto vitest`.

**Militia-pool validation must read `state.military.militia_pools`, not a dead top-level alias (2026-04-15):** `validateMilitiaPools(...)` is called against canonical `GameState`, so reading `(state as any).militia_pools` silently empties the validator for real callers. The durable rule is: militia pool validation owns the nested `state.military.militia_pools` substrate, with any top-level alias treated only as legacy fallback. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate recruitment and militia contracts onto vitest`.

**Formation validation must read `state.military.formations`, not a dead top-level alias (2026-04-15):** `validateFormations(...)` is called from the live formation CLI and validation paths with a canonical `GameState`, so reading `(state as any).formations` silently empties the validator and turns every downstream call into theater. The durable rule is: formation validation owns the nested `state.military.formations` substrate, with any top-level alias treated only as a legacy fallback, never as the primary read path. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate formation contracts onto vitest and repair canonical validator ownership`.

**Retire stale doctrine expectations when the function owner is narrower than the old test prose (2026-04-15):** `runFormationHqRelocation(...)` owns one bounded behavior: if an HQ sits in enemy-controlled territory, relocate it to a friendly same-municipality or adjacent-municipality settlement. It does **not** own AoR depth selection or “move to depth-2 behind the front” doctrine. The durable rule is: when a migrated test asserts behavior outside the current function’s owned contract, delete or demote the stale expectation instead of preserving a ceremonial red-green lie. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate formation contracts onto vitest and repair canonical validator ownership`.

**Treaty dev-UI helper tests are still pure domain proof when they only exercise deterministic helper logic (2026-04-15):** The `dev_ui_phase16/17/18` and deterministic-export suites do not mount React or talk to desktop bridges; they just prove treaty-authoring helper logic, validation display ordering, and deterministic preset/export behavior. The durable rule is: when a “UI” file is really a pure helper/reporting contract, treat it as part of the domain’s fast vitest lane rather than leaving it in `node:test` because of the filename. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate treaty dev-ui helper contracts onto vitest`.

**Small pure supply families should move together once the reserve-heavy lanes already live on vitest (2026-04-15):** `supply_gating`, `supply_reachability`, and `supply_reachability_osid` are deterministic reachability/gating contracts, not scenario runners or packaging tests. The durable rule is: once the more complex supply-reserve suites already live in vitest, pull the remaining pure supply helpers across as one small family instead of leaving a tiny `node:test` island behind. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate supply reachability and gating contracts onto vitest`.

**Delete placeholder tests once their owner surface is gone; do not migrate theater into the new harness (2026-04-15):** `bot_brigade_attack_dedup.test.ts` had already admitted that its SID-based owner (`bot_brigade_ai.ts`) was deleted and that the file no longer proved current OSID behavior. The durable rule is: when a file only says “legacy tests removed” and points at another live owner, delete it instead of carrying the placeholder into vitest. Migrating theater just creates a cleaner-looking lie. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate bot management contracts onto vitest and retire dead dedup placeholder`.

**Treaty and victory-condition contracts should share the vitest universe even when one member is scenario-heavy (2026-04-15):** The treaty lane contains both pure in-process clause/application suites and one adjacent scenario-backed proof (`victory_conditions_a2`). The durable rule is: do not keep a second runner alive just because one domain member is slower. Keep the whole lane in vitest, then let discovery classify the scenario-backed file into the scenario slice while the pure treaty contracts stay in the fast slice. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate treaty and victory-condition contracts onto vitest`.

**When an emergence harness migration fails, suspect stale phase/report names before suspecting the engine (2026-04-15):** The emergence family moved cleanly to vitest, but the first run failed because several tests were still naming a retired Phase E pipeline shape (`phase-ii-consolidation`, `phase-ii-front-emergence`, `phase_e_aor_derivation`, `phase_e_rear_zone_derivation`) and an old peace-phase error string. The durable rule is: when a pure pipeline-proof suite starts failing during harness unification, first grep the live phase owner names and report keys before inferring behavioral regression. Test drift around renamed steps is common after long hardening campaigns. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate emergence substrate contracts onto vitest and retarget stale phase-proof labels`.

**Pure emergence substrate suites belong on vitest when they only exercise Phase E derivation and pipeline order (2026-04-15):** AoR instantiation, front emergence, pressure diffusion/gating/schema, rear-zone detection, determinism, and the Phase E validation suite all prove one in-process emergence substrate band. They do not need scenario orchestration or packaging semantics, so leaving them in `node:test` only created another fake runner split. The durable rule is: when an adjacent emergence family is deterministic and in-process, migrate the whole band together to vitest and pin representative pipeline/schema members in the fast discovery slice. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate emergence substrate contracts onto vitest and retarget stale phase-proof labels`.

**Displacement validation must police nested `state.displacement.*` owners, not dead top-level ghosts (2026-04-15):** The displacement family migration exposed the same smell already seen in war-phase and early-war validation: `validateGameStateShape(...)` was still checking `displacement_camp_state` through a stale top-level gate even though canonical state, serialization, and sim code store every displacement field under `state.displacement`. The durable rule is: if a displacement contract lives under the nested displacement partition, the validator must reject malformed values at that nested owner path rather than silently accepting them because a dead top-level alias no longer exists. Applied in `[2026-04-15] fix(test+state): migrate displacement contracts onto vitest and repair nested displacement validation`.

**Large deterministic displacement families should migrate together to vitest once they stop needing special runner semantics (2026-04-15):** Routing, takeover, accumulation, triggers, municipality aggregation, capacity hooks, pipeline integration, state schema, pipeline validation, and morale/displacement schema tests all prove the same in-process displacement substrate. Keeping them in `node:test` only creates fake harness multiplicity around one of the main civilian-consequence owners. The durable rule is: when an adjacent displacement family is deterministic and in-process, move the whole band together to vitest and pin representative schema/takeover files in the fast discovery slice. Applied in `[2026-04-15] fix(test+state): migrate displacement contracts onto vitest and repair nested displacement validation`.

**Front posture and segment substrate contracts belong on vitest when they only exercise in-process front-state logic (2026-04-15):** `assignable_front_segments`, `front_assignment`, `front_breaches`, `front_posture_commitment`, `front_posture_normalize`, `front_posture_regions_expand`, `front_pressure_accumulate`, `front_pressure_supply_modulation`, `front_regions`, `front_segments`, and `front_segments_validate` all prove the same deterministic front substrate band. They do not need scenario orchestration or artifact management, so keeping them in `node:test` only creates fake harness multiplicity around one of the combat layer's core state owners. The durable rule is: when a front-domain suite is pure assignment, posture, pressure, region, or segment-state logic, migrate the adjacent family together to vitest and pin representative members in the fast discovery contract. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate front posture and segment substrate contracts onto vitest`.

**Early-war validation must police nested canonical owners, not dead top-level ghosts (2026-04-15):** The early-war schema migration exposed the same smell the war-phase packet had already found in another band: `validateGameStateShape(...)` was still checking `war_jna` and `war_alliance_rbih_hrhb` on the root object even though canonical state, serialization, and sim code store them under `state.military` and `state.political`. The durable rule is: if an early-war contract lives under a nested partition in `GameState`, the validator must reject malformed values at that nested owner path rather than silently accepting them because a stale top-level alias no longer exists. Applied in `[2026-04-15] fix(test+state): migrate early-war contract family onto vitest and repair nested early-war validation`.

**Large deterministic early-war families should migrate as one band, not one stray file at a time (2026-04-15):** Alliance lifecycle, authority degradation, control flip, control strain, displacement hooks, entry gating, injected-graph parity, JNA transition, militia emergence, pool population, early-war schema, turn structure, and OOB early-war entry are all pure in-process contracts around the same Peace/early-war substrate. Leaving them scattered in `node:test` only creates the illusion that early-war logic needs a special runner. The durable rule is: when an adjacent early-war family is deterministic and in-process, migrate the whole band together to vitest, then pin a representative early-war schema/structure slice in the fast discovery contract. Applied in `[2026-04-15] fix(test+state): migrate early-war contract family onto vitest and repair nested early-war validation`.

**Pure Phase D combat substrate contracts belong on vitest when they only exercise in-process state mutation and validation (2026-04-15):** `combat_command_friction`, `combat_command_friction_effect`, `combat_exhaustion`, `combat_supply_pressure`, `combat_front_emergence`, and `phase_d_validation` all prove the same combat substrate band and do not need scenario orchestration, packaging semantics, or process isolation. Keeping them in `node:test` only manufactures a second harness owner around deterministic logic. The durable rule is: when a combat-domain suite is just Phase D state mutation, stabilization, pressure, or validation truth, move the adjacent family together onto vitest and pin a representative member in the fast discovery slice. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate Phase D combat substrate contracts onto vitest`.

**When a harness migration exposes a dead validation path, fix the validator instead of teaching tests to lie (2026-04-15):** `combat_state_schema.test.ts` was mutating `state.political.war_supply_pressure` and `state.political.war_exhaustion`, which is where the schema and sim already store those fields. The migration surfaced that `validateGameStateShape(...)` was only checking dead top-level `war_supply_pressure` / `war_exhaustion` / `war_exhaustion_local` paths, so malformed nested values were silently accepted. The durable rule is: if a test move reveals the validator is policing the wrong owner path, repair the validator to the canonical state location rather than watering down the test. Applied in `[2026-04-15] fix(test+state): migrate core pipeline/state contracts onto vitest and repair nested war-field validation`.

**Canonical serializer and GameState guardrail suites belong on the fast vitest lane when they are pure (2026-04-15):** The deciding question for save-shape tests is not whether they touch important state, but whether they need special runner semantics. `serialize_gamestate_stability`, `serialize_gamestate_rejects_wrappers`, `serialize_gamestate_no_derived_fields`, `game_state_shape`, `game_state_no_derived_fields`, and `game_state_turn_week_invariant` are all pure validation/serialization contracts with no process orchestration. Keeping them in `node:test` only creates fake harness multiplicity around one of the repo's most central invariants. The durable rule is: when a serializer/GameState contract is pure, migrate it to vitest and pin a representative member of the cluster in the fast discovery slice. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate serializer and GameState guardrails onto vitest`.

**Weak serializer fixtures and roadmap-prose sentinels should retire once stronger owners exist (2026-04-15):** A tiny fixture round-trip can be useful when the serializer is first born, but once the repo has production-scale real-save idempotency plus dedicated serializer contract suites, the minimal file stops adding meaningful protection and starts implying a false owner surface. The same is true for old roadmap-alignment sentinels whose whole job is to assert that a copied directive file still contains an exact title string and prohibition sentence. The durable rule is: when a stronger runtime or real-artifact suite exists, retire the tiny fixture/prose sentinel rather than keeping a comforting but weak second proof. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): retire superseded save-load fixture and roadmap sentinel`.

**Pure in-process engine contract suites should not stay in `node:test` just because they are old (2026-04-15):** A cluster of small engine-state suites can end up stranded in the chunked runner long after the repo has a canonical vitest lane. The deciding question is not age or domain, but whether the test needs process isolation, scenario execution, or special runner semantics. Files like `turn_pipeline.test.ts`, `turn_pipeline_determinism_smoke.test.ts`, `patron_pressure.test.ts`, `negotiation_pressure.test.ts`, `combat_summary.test.ts`, and `scenario_activity_truth.test.ts` are all pure in-process logic/source contracts, so keeping them in `node:test` only creates fake harness multiplicity. The durable rule is: if a suite is pure state mutation, aggregation, determinism, or source-selection logic, move it to vitest and then lock its discovery into the fast slice. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): migrate pure engine contract suites onto vitest`.

**Necessary proof should move to the canonical harness; residual packaging should merge into the owner suite (2026-04-15):** Two small test smells look similar from far away but need opposite treatment. A test like `desktop_sim_bundle_smoke` still proves a unique contract — that the bundled desktop sim can actually be built and `require(...)`d cleanly in CommonJS mode — so it should stay, but live in vitest with the rest of the desktop contract lane instead of lingering in `node:test` by inertia. A file like `ui_threat_precision_player_visibility`, by contrast, is not a distinct owner surface; it is one clause of the broader player-visibility contract and belongs inside `ui_player_visibility.test.ts`. The durable rule is: keep unique proof, move it to the canonical harness; delete only the extra file boundary when the real owner suite already exists. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): move bundle smoke to vitest and fold threat-precision residue into player visibility`.

**Tiny real-save invariant locks should consolidate by artifact family once the owner is stable (2026-04-15):** A cluster of one-test files around `data/derived/latest_run_final_save.json` can all be valid and still be maintenance residue if they re-open the same artifact from five different files to prove adjacent clauses of the same ownership surface. The durable rule is: once the latest-save sector/front truth owner is stable, keep those checks together in one canonical suite named for the artifact family (`real_save_sector_truth_contracts`) rather than one file per clause (`edge_cap`, `geometry_invariant`, `live_owner`, `single_owner`, `faction_side_ownership`). That keeps the proof readable without weakening it. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): consolidate real-save sector truth contracts`, which replaced five tiny latest-save files with one five-test suite.

**Oversized test suites should be split by behavior families, not by arbitrary line counts (2026-04-15):** The safe way to break up a god-test file is to preserve existing proof groups and their local helpers, then move those groups into coherent domain files without inventing a new shared abstraction layer first. For `command_authority.test.ts`, the stable seams were: lifecycle/provenance, strain signals, friction/stabilization, interpretation/review, assessment/constraints, and explanation/delegation. The durable rule is: split on truth domains and wrapper boundaries (`describe(...)` families), keep pure helper setup duplicated when that avoids hidden coupling, and only extract shared helpers later if the new files show real duplication pressure. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): split command-authority proof into domain suites`, which preserved all 290 assertions while deleting the 2.8k-line junk-drawer file.

**Vitest discovery should classify slow scenario runners by actual runner calls, not by filename folklore (2026-04-15):** Once pure contract suites were moved onto vitest, the remaining harness debt was no longer "which runner owns this file?" but "which vitest files are cheap enough for the fast lane?" The durable rule is: keep a single discovery owner (`tools/test/discover_test_files.mjs`), but let it split vitest into `fastVitestFiles` and `scenarioVitestFiles` based on real scenario-runner calls (`runScenario`, `runScenarioDeterministic`, `runProbeCompare`, `compareAgainstBaselines`) rather than on filename prefixes like `scenario_` or `integration_`. That keeps lightweight registry/repair/preseeding suites in the fast lane while moving true run-heavy suites into an explicit scenario slice. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): split vitest discovery into fast and scenario slices`, which added `test:vitest:fast`, `test:vitest:scenario`, and a discovery contract proving every vitest file belongs to exactly one slice.

**Pure source/package contract suites should live in vitest unless they truly need the node:test lane (2026-04-15):** The test-discovery owner in this repo is import-driven: `tools/test/discover_test_files.mjs` classifies files by whether they import `vitest`. That means a contract suite stranded in `node:test` by inertia becomes a second harness owner even when it is only doing source/package/workflow assertions. The durable rule is: keep `node:test` for files that genuinely need that runner's semantics or chunked execution model, but move pure contract suites into vitest so the daily gate, discovery contract, and roadmap unification lane all point the same way. Applied in `[2026-04-15] test(repo): move desktop contract guardrails onto the canonical vitest lane`, which migrated `desktop_packaging_contract`, `desktop_packaged_runtime_probe`, `desktop_startup_snapshot_guardrails`, and `desktop_release_ci_guardrails`, then locked discovery with `tests/test_discovery_contract.test.ts`.

**Save serialization must emit the same canonical defaults that load-time migration injects (2026-04-15):** AWWV's continue-from-save proof failed even though the turn loop was deterministic, because uninterrupted runs were saving formations with implicit optional defaults while resumed runs reloaded them with explicit defaults (`activation_gated`, `force_label`, `ops`, and related lifecycle/read-model fields). The durable rule is: the canonical save boundary must serialize through the same defaulting/migration surface that deserialize uses, or save-load-continue will drift on hidden shape differences instead of on real sim behavior. Applied in `[2026-04-15] fix(harness): prove continue-from-save equivalence on canonical save output`.

**Warroom-local overlays must not live in the shared shell handoff union (2026-04-15):** `strategic-overview` and `event-log` are owned by the React Warroom shell, not by the cross-shell tactical handoff bridge. The durable pattern is to keep `ShellHandoffCommand` reserved for cross-shell navigation (`army-hq`, `codex`, `chronicle`, `advance-turn`) and model Warroom-only overlays with a separate local command type plus an explicit local command guard. That keeps the shared parser honest, prevents URL/query or message-boundary consumers from treating overlays as full navigations, and makes the `App.tsx` handoff handler branch locally before any transition logic runs. Applied in `[2026-04-15] fix(ui): split Warroom-local overlays from shared shell handoff`.

**Roadmap status must distinguish formal release labeling from transition-band work (2026-04-15):** AWWV can legitimately have package semver still at `v0.8.1` while the repo is already executing the `v0.8-to-v0.9` hardening band and pulling forward bounded `v0.9.x` slices. The durable rule is: do **not** paper over that by prematurely renaming the whole project `v0.9`, but also do **not** leave the roadmap status table claiming "Not started" once real consequence, comparison, or accessibility slices are already on `main`. Mark those milestones as **partial**, name exactly what is live, and keep the remaining closure gates explicit. Applied in `[2026-04-15] docs(plan): realign roadmap status with shipped v0.8-to-v0.9 work`.

**Tactical-map performance packets should start by cutting duplicate render work before chasing bigger rewrites (2026-04-15):** `MapContainer` owns multiple deck-layer update paths and a pulse RAF loop, so the first honest performance seam is render churn, not speculative algorithm surgery. The safe pattern is: route every Deck overlay update through one guard that compares the actual render inputs before calling `deckOverlay.setProps(...)`, and gate animation loops so they sleep when no staged arrows, ghost paths, or visible recent battle markers exist. This is a real reduction in needless renderer work, but it is **not** a benchmark artifact and should never be reported as a measured FPS win unless a before/after capture exists. Applied in `[2026-04-15] perf(ui): reduce tactical map render churn`.

**Warroom shell hotspots must be real buttons at the React owner layer, not hover-only rectangles (2026-04-15):** `src/ui/map/components/warroom/WarroomShellLayer.tsx` is the canonical accessibility seam for the Warroom overlay. If a hotspot is meant to navigate, it must expose native button semantics, a stable accessible label, and keyboard activation (`Enter` / `Space`) at that owner layer. `title` text on an absolutely positioned `div` is not enough: it leaves the shell mouse-only and unnamed for assistive tech. The safe pattern is to centralize the label (`getWarroomRegionLabel`) from tooltip-or-id, keep the region mapping (`regionToShellHandoff`) separate from accessibility naming, and announce unavailable shell state through a polite `role="status"` region. Applied in `[2026-04-15] feat(ui): make Warroom hotspots keyboard-accessible`.

**Codex should consume endgame comparison truth through a narrow resolver before anyone tries to build a giant essay engine (2026-04-15):** `CodexPanel` is a reading surface, not an owner of simulation truth.
