The safe pattern is to keep long-form essays canonical in `essay_index.json`, then add a small UI-side resolver that can evaluate a narrow condition language and insert downstream notes into selected essays. In practice, the first useful slice is: `ghost_when` for historically real events that never occurred in the player's war, plus `dynamic_sections` for endgame comparison notes. The resolver should stay deterministic and data-authored (`GAME_OVER`, `COMPARISON_NOTES`, `RUPTURE:*`, `EVENT:*`, `FLAG:*`, `AND/OR/NOT`) instead of inventing a freeform scripting surface. Applied in `[2026-04-15] feat(ui): make Codex endgame-aware with ghost essays`, where Srebrenica became a ghost essay when the rupture was absent and the Dayton essay gained live divergence-note insertions from `historicalComparison.divergence_notes`.

**Chronicle and Wrapped should consume endgame comparison truth before Codex grows a full dynamic-essay engine (2026-04-15):** `Chronicle` is the campaign-memory owner and `Wrapped` is the retrospective recap surface. If `historicalComparison.divergence_notes` already exists, those shells should consume it directly rather than waiting for a full Codex essay engine or inventing a second comparison packet. A restrained `ghost` narrative card is acceptable here when it reflects a historically real rupture that never occurred in the player's war (for example Srebrenica surviving), but Codex remains a static essay shell until a dedicated dynamic-essay owner lands. Applied in `[2026-04-15] feat(ui): thread endgame comparison into Chronicle and Wrapped`.

**Consequence substrate owner split must stay explicit across state, negotiation, and political layers (2026-04-15):** `src/state/patron_pressure.ts` owns IVP and `patron_state` evolution (`patron_commitment`, `diplomatic_isolation`, `constraint_severity`). `src/sim/negotiation/patron_pressure.ts` separately owns negotiation-side `support_level`, `override_authority`, sanctions, and other patron pressure used by peace-plan / Dayton decisions. `src/sim/political/political_peace_plan.ts` owns pure bot peace-plan response scoring, while `src/sim/negotiation/peace_plans.ts` is only the trigger / pending-packet / resolution wrapper that calls into that scorer. `src/sim/events/strategic_dimensions.ts` must read canonical `state.political.war_exhaustion`, not any stale `state.military.war_exhaustion` shadow, because faction exhaustion is the political-layer accumulator shared with termination and commander drag. Applied in `[2026-04-15] fix(sim): restore political war_exhaustion as strategic dimension owner`.

**`sector_offensive.ts` should keep lifecycle ownership while leaf helper families move out (2026-04-15):** The safe decomposition pattern for `src/sim/combat/sector_offensive.ts` is not to split the lifecycle across many pseudo-owners. Keep operation lifecycle and mutation entrypoints (`advanceSectorOffensives`, `updateSectorOffensiveResults`, `evaluateCorpsOffensiveLaunch`, `evaluateSectorOffensiveLaunch`, `reevaluateWeakenedOperations`, `evaluateOperationProgress`) in `sector_offensive.ts`, and extract only leaf helper families that are coherent and independently reusable. In practice, two families were safe: (1) axis / operation-shape helpers (`isMultiAxis`, axis objective and brigade collection, role assignment, execution reset, contiguity validation), and (2) launch / readiness helpers (feasibility estimation, approach-OSID derivation, readiness checks, supply readiness, momentum helpers). Downstream consumers should import those helper modules directly rather than through the god-file facade where possible. Proof should come from targeted offensive vitest suites plus a canonical 40w scenario hash rerun; do not overclaim the legacy `node:test` files as green if they are already stale against HEAD. Applied in `2026-04-15` sector-offensive helper extraction, which preserved final hash `2dc5a98fd4f2ec2e`.

**war_phases.ts should be decomposed by late-turn step families while keeping the canonical array in one place (2026-04-15):** The safe maintainability pattern for `src/sim/turn_phases/war_phases.ts` is not to fragment the whole pipeline into dozens of tiny helpers. Instead, extract *coherent ordered families* (for example: final reconciliation/assertion, late negotiation/rupture/Dayton, command briefing/summary) into leaf modules that each export `NamedPhase[]`, then compose them back into the single canonical `warPhases` array with ordered spreads. The durable invariant is: `war_phases.ts` keeps ownership of the top-level ordering, while helper modules own contiguous late-turn bands. Proof should come from two places: (1) a structure assertion that each helper family appears as a contiguous ordered slice inside `warPhases`, and (2) a scenario hash rerun proving no behavior drift. Applied in `9da5522a`, which extracted three late-turn families and preserved the 40w canonical hash `2dc5a98fd4f2ec2e`. Linked lane: `[2026-04-15] refactor(sim): extract late war-phase step families`.

**Desktop IPC-side strain computation has a single CJS-side owner at `src/desktop/command_strain.cjs` (2026-04-15):** When electron-main.cjs IPC handlers need to read the same derived metric at multiple call sites — especially a numeric formula like command strain that decides a gate *and* a cost tier in different handlers — the formula must live in one CJS helper, not inline in each handler body. The durable pattern is: extract the formula to `src/desktop/<metric>.cjs` as a pure function, `require(...)` it at the top of `electron-main.cjs`, and add it to both `package.json` build.files AND the static expected array in `tests/desktop_packaging_contract.test.ts` (the dynamic `require()`-scan assertion picks it up automatically). If a TS-side canonical already exists for the same metric in the renderer (e.g. `src/ui/map/data/command_strain.ts`), the CJS helper may intentionally implement a narrower subset — but that divergence must be flagged in the helper header with a pointer to the TS owner and an explicit note on which sources it omits, so future maintainers extend in one place rather than resurrect duplication. Applied: Wave 4 stance gate + Level 2 stabilize-command-relationship handlers in `electron-main.cjs` now both call `computeCorpsCommandStrain(state, corpsId, currentTurn) → { totalStrain, isCompromised }`; exhaustion (Source 3) remains TS-only per prior behavior. Linked lane: `[2026-04-15] refactor(desktop): extract command strain computation from electron-main.cjs`.

**`shellNavigation.ts` is canonical Tactical → child-surface navigation dispatch (2026-04-15):** Any Tactical-shell entry point that opens Army HQ, Codex, or Chronicle as a navigation dispatch (keyboard shortcut, toolbar button, inbox action, hotspot callback) must route through a helper in `src/ui/map/utils/shellNavigation.ts` (`openArmyHQTab`, `openArmyHQRecordsSubTab`, `openArmyHQBriefingForCorps`, `openCodex`, `openChronicle`, `applyShellHandoffCommand`) instead of calling `setArmyHQOpen`/`setArmyHQTab`/`setCodexOpen`/`setChronicleOpen` directly. Exceptions are explicit and narrow: (1) internal state dismissal (e.g. escape-to-close a modal calling `setArmyHQOpen(false)` is fine), (2) reopen-with-last-tab dispatch where forcing a specific tab would stomp the user's last-viewed tab (`PresidentialToolbar.handleOpenHQ` is the canonical idiom — keep the `setSelectedArmyId` + `setArmyHQOpen(true)` pair with a comment), (3) Warroom hotspots already route through `tacticalShellHandoffHandler` → `applyShellHandoffCommand`, which is itself canonical. Raw `useGameStore.setState({ armyHQTab: ... })` bypasses are always wrong — they skip the gameStore setter contract. Proof: behavioural source-scan test in `tests/ui_shell_navigation.test.ts` asserts App.tsx/PresidentialToolbar/TopToolbar do not reintroduce direct setter sequences for navigation dispatch. Linked lane: `[2026-04-15] fix(ui): unify Tactical navigation on shellNavigation helpers`.

**All UI selection/modal/order state must reset on save load, not just inbox flags (2026-04-14):** `gameStore.loadSave` previously only reset `openingBriefDismissed`. Selection state (`selectedOsid`, `selectedFormationId`, `selectedCorpsId`, etc.), modal state (`armyHQOpen`, `opsPlanningModalOpen`, `commanderSelectionContext`, etc.), order state (`stagedOrders`, `orderModeForFormation`), and visual state (`operationTargetOsids`, `ghostLinePoint`, `flashOsid`) all survived save loads. These hold entity references from the old save that may not exist in the new save — causing ghost panels, broken detail views, and confusing player state. The durable pattern: `loadSave` must reset ALL entity-referencing UI state to initial values as part of the atomic `set()` call that applies the new `loadedGameState`. Any new store field that references save-specific entities (formation IDs, corps IDs, sector IDs, operation keys) must be added to this reset block. Proof: 11 tests in `tests/ui/gamestore_load_reset.test.ts`. Linked lane: `[2026-04-14] fix(ui+desktop): harden post-load UI state reset and load error classification`.

**Desktop load errors should be classified into player-facing messages, not raw exceptions (2026-04-14):** When `loadStateFromPath` or `loadScenarioFromPath` throws, the raw `e.message` contains internal details (validation paths, stack-trace fragments, JSON parser output). The player sees this in the UI error banner. The durable pattern: `classifyLoadError(e)` in `electron-main.cjs` maps known error signatures to human-readable prefixes ("Save file is damaged or not valid JSON", "Save file structure is incompatible with this version", "Save file was created by a newer version and cannot be loaded") while preserving the raw message for debugging. Wire it into all `load-*-dialog` and `start-new-campaign` catch blocks. Proof: 10 tests in `tests/ui/desktop_load_error_classification.test.ts`. Linked lane: `[2026-04-14] fix(ui+desktop): harden post-load UI state reset and load error classification`.

**Endgame must separate termination, judgment, and comparison (2026-04-14):** AWWV already has scenario victory termination (`evaluateVictoryConditions`, `checkWarTermination`) and a separate verdict/scoring engine (`computeFullVerdict`). The durable rule is: ending the war is not the same as judging the war, and neither is the same as historical comparison. Termination decides *when the game stops*; the verdict packet decides *what kind of outcome the player produced*; endgame comparison later decides *how that war differed from history*. Do not let one function pretend to own all three. Linked lane: `[2026-04-14] docs(plan): resolve design gates and ungate blocked roadmap lanes`.

**Sensitive-history content belongs in locked consequence and reckoning surfaces, not optimization loops (2026-04-14):** The engine may model precursors such as enclave integrity, displacement, atrocity visibility, patron pressure, and international standing collapse. But historically named atrocity/genocide content must enter as a locked rupture consequence plus post-hoc reckoning, not as player-issued commands, score-positive levers, or tactical efficiency puzzles. For Srebrenica specifically: enclave fall can remain mechanical war truth; genocide aftermath belongs to locked consequence flags, patron/standing shock, and endgame/cost-ledger comparison. Linked lane: `[2026-04-14] docs(plan): resolve design gates and ungate blocked roadmap lanes`.

**Adapter reads must never initiate negotiations or mutate latent state (2026-04-14):** `GameStateAdapter.derivePendingDayton` exposed a design smell: the renderer read path was calling Dayton initiation helpers, which makes UI observation itself an owner of game progression. The durable rule is: adapters may shape persisted state for display, but if a negotiation should begin, that must be written by a canonical pipeline/state owner first. Read paths may read `pending_*` packets; they must not create them. Linked lane: `[2026-04-14] docs(plan): resolve design gates and ungate blocked roadmap lanes`.

**Roadmap work should be packetized from existing milestone plans before new plan docs are written (2026-04-14):** AWWV already has strong milestone-grade planning for most `v0.8-to-v0.9` work (`save/load`, `UI surface ownership`, `product architecture simplification`, `shell cohesion`, `explanation surfaces`). The durable pattern is: audit `MASTER_ROADMAP.md`, the existing `docs/plans/*.md`, the current scorecard, and recent implemented reports first; then derive bounded execution packets from those sources instead of drafting new roadmap docs for already-planned lanes. Packet prompts should start by inspecting current repo truth and should no-op cleanly if another lane already landed the seam, rather than assuming the pasted report is current. Only write new planning docs when the existing plan is stale, too broad to packetize, or blocked by missing design contracts. Applied in this session by creating `2026-04-14-roadmap-execution-packet-backlog.md`, `2026-04-14-roadmap-execution-packet-prompts.md`, and refreshed `v0.9.0` consequence/endgame planning docs instead of duplicating the `v0.8-to-v0.9` plans. Linked lane: `[2026-04-14] docs(plan): packetize roadmap execution backlog and refresh v0.9 consequence/endgame planning`.

**Inbox dismissal state must reset on save load (2026-04-14):** Session-local dismissal flags (`peacePlanDismissed`, `acknowledgedEventIds`) that live in React `useState` survive save loads — a new save mid-session silently hides real pending items behind stale flags. The durable pattern: any UI-side dismiss/acknowledge state that gates inbox visibility must reset when the loaded state fingerprint changes. Watch `lastLoadedStateFingerprint` from Zustand, not `loadedGameState` identity (which can be referentially unstable). This applies to any future inbox dismiss mechanism. Linked lanes: `[2026-04-14] fix(ui): reset inbox dismissal state on save load`.

**Autonomy proposals are inbox-worthy when adapter truth exists (2026-04-14):** `state.meta.pending_proposal_reviews` is canonical upstream truth for AI-generated command proposals at autonomy level 1 (Assisted). The adapter must filter to player faction + unresolved only (`accepted == null`). Inbox item type `autonomy_proposal` with action `autonomy_panel`, priority 35. The AutonomyPanel already renders the review UI — the inbox entry is the *discovery* path, not the *decision* surface. Pattern: when upstream truth exists and a panel already handles the decision, the inbox item's job is notification + routing, not duplicating the decision UI. Linked lane: `[2026-04-14] fix(ui): plumb autonomy proposals into presidential inbox`.

**Inbox Routing: Action-Family vs Item-Identity (2026-04-14):** When an inbox action opens a *queue-based modal* (like EventModal which cycles through multiple pending items), the inbox click MUST pass item identity so the modal opens at the correct queue index. When an inbox action opens a *singleton modal or panel* (like PeacePlanModal or ArmyReservePanel), family-based routing (action name only) is sufficient. The EventModal accepts a queue index. Without item identity, the handler defaults to index 0 — silently discarding the player's click intent. This creates a hidden "always open first" behavior that is invisible from the inbox but breaks the player's mental model. Any future inbox item type that routes to a queue-based modal must pass `itemId` through the callback. Test by creating 3+ pending items of that type and verifying click-to-open identity for each. Linked lane: `[2026-04-14] fix(ui): close inbox identity routing seam`.

**Commander systems that only read operational counters (corps_exhaustion) are blind to faction-level war exhaustion (2026-04-14):** The entire `src/sim/combat/commander/` directory — briefing, assess, plan, decide, emit — read only `corps_exhaustion` (0-100, decaying per turn). It never read `state.political.war_exhaustion` (monotonic, unbounded). This meant corps COs could launch offensives with full enthusiasm even when the nation was bleeding out. The durable pattern is: when a subsystem needs to reflect accumulated national cost, it must read the monotonic faction-level accumulator (`war_exhaustion`), not just the operational per-unit counter. The fix wires `faction_war_exhaustion` into the briefing and applies a `factionExhaustionDrag` multiplier (1.0 at 0, floor 0.3 at 600+) to offensive plan scoring. Linked lane: `[2026-04-14] feat(commander): wire faction war_exhaustion into commander plan scoring`.

**Save/load round-trip tests must use real production-scale saves, not minimal hand-built fixtures (2026-04-14):** Existing `save_load_roundtrip.test.ts` used a fixture with one brigade and two OSIDs. Real state has 200+ formations, 700+ political controllers, corps command entries, autonomy fields, war exhaustion accumulators, and deeply nested structures. Field-level regressions (e.g. migration defaults overwriting legitimate undefined, adapter path drift after field relocation) only appear at production scale. The durable pattern is: keep a real `latest_run_final_save.json` in `data/derived/` and run idempotency tests against it. `serialize(deserialize(serialize(deserialize(file))))` must be byte-identical to `serialize(deserialize(file))`. Linked lane: `[2026-04-14] test(save-load): add real-save round-trip proof tests`.

**Resolved autonomy proposals must be GC'd at turn boundaries, not accumulated indefinitely (2026-04-14):** `apply-autonomy-transition` previously filtered `pending_proposal_reviews` to remove only unresolved prior-turn proposals (`p.turn < meta.turn && p.accepted === undefined`). Resolved proposals (accepted/rejected) accumulated forever, growing the serialized state each turn. The durable pattern is: at each turn boundary, clear ALL prior-turn proposals with `p.turn >= meta.turn`. Current-turn proposals haven't been generated yet at this pipeline point, so the filter is safe. Linked lane: `[2026-04-14] fix(autonomy): GC resolved proposals from prior turns`.

**Packaged Electron entrypoints must ship every local main-process helper they `require(...)`, and the contract should be proven from source instead of by a hand-kept list (2026-04-10):** AWWV’s packaged desktop crashed in production-like probe mode because `electron-main.cjs` started requiring `./autonomy_ipc_contract.cjs`, but `package.json` still shipped only `electron-main.cjs` and `preload.cjs` inside the packaged app. The durable pattern is: treat `electron-main.cjs` as the source of truth for local main-process `.cjs` helper dependencies, include those helpers explicitly in `build.files`, and lock the relationship with a contract test that scans the source for `require('./*.cjs')` calls. This keeps packaged runtime truth aligned with the real entrypoint instead of trusting a stale static list. Linked lane: `[2026-04-10] fix(desktop): harden packaged startup contract`.

**A baked startup artifact is part of shipped runtime truth, not just build-cache convenience (2026-04-10):** Once desktop `apr_1992` startup consumes `data/derived/startup/apr_1992_initial_save.json`, any engine/runtime hardening that changes initial brigade/sector truth also changes the shipped product unless the baked artifact is refreshed. The durable pattern is: keep the startup builder in `src/scenario/startup_snapshot.ts` as canonical truth, treat the JSON artifact as a one-way derived product artifact, and require `desktop:startup-snapshot:check` plus a real packaged probe before claiming the desktop startup path is hardened. Linked lane: `[2026-04-10] fix(desktop): harden packaged startup contract`.

**When a renderer needs faction-scoped review work, enforce the scope at the desktop/read-model boundary first and let the panel be downstream only (2026-04-10):** A renderer-side filter is useful as belt-and-suspenders, but it is not enough if the desktop IPC contract still returns mixed-faction packets and accepts arbitrary ids on writeback. In AWWV, the authoritative boundary for autonomy review is `electron-main.cjs`: it already knows `state.meta.player_faction` and owns `pending_proposal_reviews`. The durable pattern is: scope `get-*` readback to the active player faction at the desktop boundary, reject cross-faction ids on `accept-*` / `reject-*`, and keep the panel a consumer of already-owned truth. Applied: `get-autonomy-state`, `accept-proposal`, and `reject-proposal` now all route through `autonomy_ipc_contract.cjs`. Linked lane: `[2026-04-10] fix(desktop): scope autonomy IPC to the active player faction`.

**Player-facing text must humanize internal zone ids through one shared formatter, not many local string hacks (2026-04-10):** In AWWV, ids like `zone:arbih_1st_corps:gorazde_2` are canonical routing/state truth, not language meant for the player. When reserve or autonomy surfaces print them directly, the UI starts leaking internal topology syntax and each surface is tempted to invent its own string cleanup rule. The durable pattern is: keep the sim id authoritative, then route every player-facing rendering through one downstream formatter that strips `zone:<corps>:` and humanizes the anchor token deterministically. Applied: `formatPlayerFacingZoneLabel(...)` now owns that translation for both Army Reserve provenance copy and autonomy op-proposal descriptions. Linked lane: `[2026-04-10] fix(ui): humanize commander zone labels in reserve and autonomy text`.

**Faction-scoped UI review surfaces must consume the live player faction, never a remembered default (2026-04-10):** In AWWV, all three factions are playable and review/proposal packets already carry their owning `faction`. If a UI surface hardcodes a faction literal while filtering live review work, it silently creates a second truth owner and makes other campaigns look empty even when the packets are present. The right pattern is: pass the active `player_faction` from the shell/root owner and filter against the packet's own `faction` field in one pure helper. Keep the packet authoritative; keep the panel a consumer. Applied: `AutonomyPanel` now filters `pending_proposal_reviews` by `playerFaction` passed from `App.tsx` instead of `p.faction === 'RBiH'`. Linked lane: `[2026-04-10] fix(ui): align autonomy proposal review with active player faction`.

**Territorial anomaly detectors must treat sector coverage as defense truth before calling a tile undefended (2026-04-09):** In AWWV, the sim already owns defense at the sector level: `corps_front_sectors` plus active assigned/reserve brigades define whether an empty OSID is still defended by the line. `checkUndefendedPaintedMismatch(...)` and `checkAdjacentUncontestedTerritory(...)` were still equating "no brigade standing exactly on the tile" with "no defense exists," which inflated `n1409` to `56` inertia mismatches and `111` walk-in warnings. The hardened rule is: physical presence is one defense signal, not the only one. If the controlling faction still has an active sector whose `territory_osids` / `sub_segments[*].friendly_osids` cover the OSID, the anomaly layer must consume that canonical owner before declaring the tile undefended. Linked lane: `[2026-04-09] fix(harness): align territorial warnings with sector coverage truth (n1410)`.

**Anomaly surfaces must consume the sim's canonical unresolved-owner list, not rebuild their own doctrine (2026-04-09):** `collectUnresolvedSectorBrigades(...)` and final sector reconciliation already decide which brigades truly fell through the sector pipeline, publishing that truth as `military.unresolved_sector_brigades`. If a downstream detector re-derives â€œunassigned frontline brigadesâ€ from local heuristics like `corpsWithSectors`, assigned sets, or op participation, it can contradict the canonical owner and mix false positives with real seams. In AWWV, `n1401` had `unresolved_sector_brigades = []` while the anomaly detector still emitted 8 critical unresolved brigades. The fix was to make `detectUnassignedFrontlineBrigades(...)` read the canonical list directly and assert equality in integration coverage. Rule: when a final reconciliation pass already publishes an unresolved-owner list, anomaly/reporting layers must consume it verbatim rather than re-deriving eligibility. Linked lane: `[2026-04-09] fix(harness): align unassigned_frontline_brigades with canonical unresolved sector truth (n1402)`.

**Descriptive formation tags must never become live anomaly owners or exemptions (2026-04-09):** `FormationState.tags` already marks tags as descriptive-only metadata, but `placement:fixed_home_osid` had quietly become a runtime exemption in `detectUnassignedFrontlineBrigades(...)`. That suppressed real deployment failures and conflated two truths inside `brigade_far_from_home`: truthful redeployments with live sector/loan ownership and genuinely ownerless drift. The hardened rule is to classify deployment anomalies from live owner state (`assignment`, `elite_loan_state`, real sectorless-reserve exemptions), not from broad OOB/recruitment tags. If a tag is stamped at formation creation and not maintained as a canonical state machine, it is documentation or flavor, not an authority source. Linked lane: `[2026-04-09] fix(harness): classify far-from-home brigades by live owner truth (n1401)`.

**If final field-brigade cross-corps sector ownership is forbidden, leave the brigade unresolved instead of inventing a foreign owner (2026-04-09):** A displaced brigade sitting inside another same-faction corps's territory can look temptingly "rescuable," especially when an old enclave helper already exists. If the canonical contract says non-elite field brigades may not finish in foreign-corps sectors, do not preserve that helper as a silent fallback. In AWWV, the honest move was to demote `assignCrossCorpsEnclaveDefenders(...)` to a no-op and restrict `rehomeUnassignedBrigadesToPhysicalSectorOwners(...)` to same-corps candidates only. That removed a false `cross_corps_sector_assignment` owner for `arbih_717th_slavna_mountain` and exposed the real residual seam as `brigade_far_from_home`. Rule for future work: unresolved drift is more truthful than an invented foreign-corps assignment unless an explicit attachment/loan contract exists.

**Lesson memory extraction must happen at the close-boundary, not on the lesson-consumer side (2026-04-05):** Phase 4 wires `CommanderLesson` extraction in `emit.ts:buildUpdatedLessons()`, not in `plan.ts:selectWinningIntent()`. The lesson consumer (plan.ts) receives an already-assembled, already-filtered list; it applies deltas without extracting. This separation is load-bearing: `emit.ts` owns the `cappedHistory` with closed-turn entries and the `planDecision.plan` with target OSIDs â€” both required for extraction. `plan.ts` owns intent scoring â€” it should not need to know where lessons came from or how they decay. Pattern: place derived-memory extraction in the layer that owns the closed-turn record (emit/state assembly), not in the layer that consumes the signal (scoring/decision). The consumer receives a clean readonly list; the assembler applies expiry, extraction, and cap. Linked: `[2026-04-05] v0.8.1 Phase 4`.

**Personality and lesson modifiers are additive post-score, never override hard-blocks (2026-04-05):** Phase 4 adds `personality_delta` and `lesson_delta` to intent scoring in `selectWinningIntent()`. Both are applied AFTER the switch-case base score and BEFORE the hard-block rules. This ordering is canonical: soft signals can shift the winning intent among eligible candidates, but they cannot rescue a blocked candidate. A lesson saying "offensive good here" does not unblock an exhausted corps. A personality bias toward aggression does not override a campaign-role prohibition. Pattern: when adding soft behavioral modifiers to any scoring loop, always place them after base scoring and before hard-block evaluation. Hard-blocks are invariants; soft modifiers are preferences. The distinction must be encoded in the code structure, not just in documentation. Linked: `[2026-04-05] v0.8.1 Phase 4`.

**When a guard is added to one assignment-pipeline path, audit all paths for the same gap (2026-04-05):** The brigade assignment pipeline has multiple functions that can assign brigades to sectors: `assignCrossCorpsEnclaveDefenders` (Step 6b), `ensureMinimumSectorCoverage` (Step 7), `commanderReviewAssignment` (Step 8a), and `rehomeUnassignedBrigadesToPhysicalSectorOwners` (Step 8d). Lane A added a drifted-brigade home_osid gate to the enclave function (Step 6b) but not the rehoming function (Step 8d). Brigades stripped by `enforcePhysicalSectorOwnership` (Step 8c) then fell through to Step 8d and got cross-corps assigned anyway. Pattern: when adding a guard to one function in a multi-step pipeline, grep for every other function that can produce the same outcome (in this case, cross-corps assignment) and apply the same guard. The pipeline is a chain of fallbacks â€” a guard in one link is useless if the next link re-introduces the problem.

**Select-then-filter is a deadlock pattern â€” filter before select when the filter can reject the entire plan (2026-04-05):** `createOpportunityPlan` selected the top N brigades by fitness_offense from ALL surplus, then checked if those specific brigades could reach the objective. When the best-fitness brigades were deep in the rear (8+ BFS hops), the reachability check rejected every objective and the plan returned null â€” even though 25+ other surplus brigades WERE reachable. The planner had a structural deadlock: it always picked the best-but-unreachable and always failed. Fix: pre-filter the surplus pool by reachability BEFORE selecting. The plan forms with the best REACHABLE brigades instead of the best overall. Pattern: whenever a selection is followed by a hard filter that can reject the entire result (not just trim it), apply the filter first to the candidate pool. Select-from-filtered is always correct; select-then-filter-all-or-nothing is a deadlock waiting for the right data shape.

**UI components must never cast LoadedGameState to raw GameState â€” the adapter boundary is the contract (2026-04-04):** `ChiefOfStaffBriefing.buildStrainParagraphs` cast `LoadedGameState` to `GameState` via `as unknown as GameState` to call `computeCorpsCommandStrain`, which read `state.military.corps_command` â€” a path that exists on raw `GameState` but not on `LoadedGameState`. Crashed on every Army HQ open. The adapter (`GameStateAdapter`) transforms nested `GameState` into flat `LoadedGameState` with `FormationView[]`. `FormationView` already had `commandStrain`/`commandStrainLabel` populated at adapter time. Fix: read the adapter-derived field directly. Pattern: when a UI component needs a derived value that the adapter already computes, NEVER reach past the adapter into the raw state. The `as unknown as` double-cast is a type-safety red flag â€” if you see it crossing the adapter boundary, it is almost certainly wrong. Grep for `as unknown as GameState` periodically to catch future violations.

**On Windows, a missing `vite` shim is not the same thing as Vite being absent (2026-04-04):** This repo can end up in a state where `vite` is installed and `node .\\node_modules\\vite\\bin\\vite.js` works, but bare `vite` in npm scripts fails with `'vite' is not recognized...'`. That means the package exists but the npm/cmd shim resolution is broken or missing. For deterministic repo scripts, prefer calling the local Vite entrypoint directly in `package.json` instead of relying on the shell to resolve `vite`.

**Operation AAR success is not combat provenance unless the export tracks why control changed (2026-04-04):** `finalizeOperationAAR()` currently marks an operation `success` when all objectives are held at finalization, and `recordOperationWeeklyEntries()` logs captures whenever controller changes to the operation faction. That means `success + total_attacks = 0` does not automatically mean "rear-pocket consolidation, not a bug" and does not prove combat malfunction either. It means the AAR/export layer is provenance-blind: it cannot distinguish capture by operation combat from capture by consolidation or another control event that happened while the operation was active. Pattern: when reading scenario anomalies, separate **runtime truth** from **export truth** before classifying a symptom as gameplay-bug, non-bug, or reporting defect.

**Delegation visibility is a reading of existing state, not a new system â€” the three-tier outcome classification already existed (2026-04-04):** `deriveOperationOutcomeCategory()` (ordinary_compliance / reluctant_compliance / direct_intervention) was already called by CommandRecord and OutcomeCategoryBadge for post-launch operations. The delegation gap was purely pre-decision: the player could see delegation truth AFTER launching but not BEFORE. The fix was a new derivation (`deriveDelegationContext`) reading the same two inputs (commander_assessment + strain) to classify the pre-decision path. Pattern: before building a "new visibility system," audit whether the truth is already computed and displayed somewhere â€” the gap may be temporal (pre vs post decision) rather than structural. Adding a pre-decision mirror of an existing post-decision classification is cheaper and more consistent than inventing a parallel classification.

**Standing delegation summary extends an existing section rather than creating a new one (2026-04-04):** The corps-level delegation summary (how many active ops are under normal delegation vs presidential direction) was added to CommandRelationshipSection, not as a new section. Rationale: CommandRelationshipSection already owns the institutional relationship truth (strain, friction, stabilization). Delegation health is institutional truth. Adding it to CorpsSituationSection (which owns military/strategic constraints from CommanderState) would mix derivation paths. Pattern: when adding a new standing truth display, check which existing section already owns the derivation domain. Extend that section rather than creating a new one. The silence=healthy check must expand to include the new truth (delegation notice can make the section visible even when strain = 0 and no friction).

**Condition-based strain sources don't decay â€” they persist while the condition persists (2026-04-04):** Corps exhaustion strain (+1 at â‰¥50, +2 at â‰¥75) is the first condition-based strain source. Unlike event-based sources (force-launch, friction) that decay -1/turn, exhaustion strain has no decay because it reads current state, not historical events. Stabilization can't resolve it because there's no friction event to clear â€” the corps is physically exhausted. This creates a meaningful distinction: event-based strain recovers with time, condition-based strain recovers only by addressing the condition (reducing operational tempo). Pattern: when adding strain sources, distinguish events (decaying, resolvable) from conditions (persistent, must address root cause). Don't apply decay formulas to conditions or stabilization to non-friction sources.

**Three renders for one theme = one section scan for the player (2026-04-04):** Army HQ corps card had command-relationship information split across an inline friction panel, a "Command Management" section, and a "Command Standing" section. The player had to scan 3 visual groups to answer one question. Consolidation into a single "Command Relationship" section with internal reading order (status â†’ forecast â†’ constraint â†’ friction â†’ action) reduced section count while increasing comprehension speed. Pattern: when N adjacent sections serve the same player question, merge into one section with internal hierarchy. Keep sections separate only when they draw from genuinely different derivation paths (e.g., command strain vs CommanderState situation assessment).

**Postponement count IS the truthful trend record â€” no previous_assessment field needed (2026-04-04):** Wave 6 needed to show whether an operation is improving, stagnating, or deteriorating. Considered adding `previous_commander_assessment` to CorpsOperation. Rejected: `postponement_count` already captures the exact information because only 'postpone' increments it and resets to intel_gathering, 'launch' ends the cycle at 'ready' (assessment never re-runs), and 'abort' ends the operation. So postponement_count=0 means first assessment (no prior), â‰¥1+launch means improved, â‰¥1+postpone means stagnating, â‰¥1+abort means deteriorated. Zero new persisted fields, zero save/load risk. Pattern: before adding a "previous X" field, check whether the existing lifecycle transitions already encode the history.

**When the engine writes enough snapshots, derive explanation on-read instead of adding engine fields (2026-04-04):** Wave 5 needed to explain why the commander recommends postpone/abort. The engine's assessment formula uses 3 weighted factors (intel 40%, force ratio 30%, supply 30%) compared against personality-derived thresholds. All inputs were already snapshotted on the operation (intel_confidence_at_assessment, supply_readiness_at_assessment, force_ratio_estimate) and the commander's aggressiveness+competence were on NamedOfficerView. Instead of adding new engine-side fields (assessment_score, go_threshold, factor_scores), the UI reconstructs the exact formula on-read. This avoids engine scope creep and keeps the explanation derivation testable as a pure function. Pattern: if the engine already persists the raw inputs and the formula is deterministic, derive the display signal in the adapter/derivation layer. Only add engine fields when the formula needs state that doesn't survive to the next turn.

**When adding a guard filter to an existing function, update ALL test fixtures that call it (2026-04-04):** Adding `isBrigadeAssignedToFront()` to `applyPostureOrders` and `applyPostureCosts` was correct â€” posture mechanics should only apply to front-assigned brigades. But 12 tests broke silently: the functions returned early/skipped without error, and the tests saw zero changes without knowing why. Pattern: when adding a new guard (like checking `corps_front_sectors`), grep for ALL callers including test files, and update their fixtures to satisfy the new precondition. Silent skip on missing data is the hardest failure mode to diagnose â€” the function "works" but processes nothing.

**Test fixtures must satisfy the full precondition chain, not just the function's direct parameters (2026-04-04):** `commanderReviewAssignment` has 9 parameters, but internally it calls `findFrontAnchoredSectorId` (checks sub_segments), `getSectorComponent` (checks componentOf + territory_osids), `getSectorFrontOsids` (needs sub_segments for BFS), and `friendlyDistanceToAny` (needs adjacency + friendlyOsids). Tests that pass empty maps for "unused" parameters break when the function's internal helpers consume them. Pattern: when a function takes infrastructure maps (componentOf, adjacency, friendlyOsids), populate them with test-relevant entries even if you think they won't matter â€” the function's callees may use them as filters.

**Operation-level and corps-level assessment read disjoint state â€” propagate, don't unify (2026-04-04):** Wave 4 audit confirmed that `commander_assessment` (operation go/no-go) reads operation-level readiness (intel confidence, force ratio, supply) while `classifyPrimaryConstraint` (corps constraint) reads corps-level strategic state (zone assessments, threat, exhaustion, command strain). They share almost no inputs and CAN diverge â€” e.g., operation ready to launch while corps is under siege. This divergence is a feature: the operation readiness gauges tell one truth (tactical fitness), the corps constraint tells another (strategic context). The correct propagation model is to show BOTH at decision time, not to merge them. The corps constraint context section in the operation modal uses the SAME derivation as the Army HQ corps card, just in compact form. No new derivation, no reconciliation logic. Two honest truths from two different vantage points.

**Standing vs decision-time explanation surfaces have different density requirements (2026-04-04):** The Army HQ corps card (`CorpsSituationSection`) is the standing/ambient explanation â€” it shows the full hierarchy: badge + dominant reason + relief path + detail factors (military, institutional, threat, plan). The operation briefing modal (`OperationConstraintContext`) is the decision-time surface â€” it shows only badge + dominant reason + relief path. The detail factors are intentionally omitted because: (1) the modal is already dense with readiness gauges, force ratio, and action buttons; (2) the detail factors are one click away on the corps card; (3) the constraint badge + reason + relief path is the minimum needed for a presidential go/no-go decision. Pattern: when propagating explanation to a secondary surface, identify the minimum truthful slice â€” don't clone the full surface.

**Relief paths are the constraint's inverse stated with real numbers, not advisory theater (2026-04-04):** Wave 3 adds `reliefPath` to each constraint classification. The pattern: take the exact condition that triggered the constraint and state its inverse using the same real values. Garrison deficit of 4 â†’ "Requires 4 additional brigades". Exhaustion at 65% â†’ "Exhaustion at 65% â€” requires reduced tempo". Command compromised â†’ "Stabilize or allow natural decay". For threat_pressure, where the player cannot control enemy behavior, the honest framing names what the player CAN do (reinforce, hold defensive positions) without promising outcomes. Never add "win battles to restore X" or "enemy will retreat when Y" â€” those are outcome predictions the engine cannot guarantee. The relief path is a derived string from the same `classifyPrimaryConstraint()` function, not a new data source.

**Descriptive explanation becomes decision-useful through priority-ordered constraint classification (2026-04-04):** Wave 1 showed a flat list of factors (military, institutional, threat, plan). Wave 2 adds `classifyPrimaryConstraint()` which selects THE single dominant factor using a fixed priority: siege > threat_pressure > defensive_duty > force_condition > institutional_strain > plan_lifecycle > none. Pattern: when a derivation produces multiple concurrent factors, add a classification pass that picks the dominant one by urgency priority. The classification is a pure function consuming the same real inputs â€” not a new data source. The UI shows the dominant reason prominently with a type badge, and the detail factors in secondary text below. This makes explanation decision-useful (the player knows what KIND of constraint is primary and what the main one IS) without adding any data the engine doesn't already compute.

**Commander plan reasons are transient unless explicitly persisted â€” persist the reason, not just the plan status (2026-04-04):** `PlanDecision.reason` in `plan.ts` computes explicit reason strings every turn ("corps in defensive stance â€” no new plans", "viability dropped to 0.15", "adopted pre-planned op: Op Corridor"). But the `CommanderState` only persisted the plan object and its `suspension_reason` â€” not the creation, advancement, or abandonment reasons. This meant the richest explanation data was computed and discarded. Pattern: when an engine loop produces a decision with a reason string, persist the reason alongside the decision in the state that survives to the next turn. `last_plan_action` + `last_plan_reason` on `CommanderState` is the minimal addition. Derive player-facing explanation on-read from these persisted fields â€” never in GameState, always in the adapter/derivation layer.

**Corps situation assessment is derived on-read from CommanderState, not stored on GameState (2026-04-04):** `deriveCorpsSituationAssessment()` in `command_strain.ts` consumes zone_assessments, threat_assessment, force_assessment, current_plan, last_plan_action/reason, plus corps stance/exhaustion/strain from FormationView. It produces postureSummary, militaryFactors, institutionalFactors, planExplanation, and threatContext. All null/empty = silence (healthy). This follows the established pattern: GameState holds canonical facts; adapter derives display signals. The derivation distinguishes military conditions (exhaustion, deficit, siege), institutional constraints (stance, strain), plan lifecycle (with persisted reasons), and threat context (pressure, concentration). One canonical surface in Army HQ corps card â€” not scattered across multiple panels.

**Sim-layer consequences must not import from UI code â€” use inline approximation (2026-04-04):** The CA recovery rate reduction (Wave 10) needs to know whether any corps is under strain from force-launched ops or unresolved friction. The full `computeCorpsCommandStrain` function lives in `src/ui/map/data/command_strain.ts` â€” UI code that sim code must not import. Rather than moving the function or creating a shared module (scope creep), the recovery step uses an inline approximation: count recent force-launched ops (within 3 turns) and unresolved friction events (within 2 turns), apply 0.5 penalty per source, cap at 2. This is intentionally a rougher calculation than the UI-side strain score â€” it captures the same directional signal (more interventions = slower recovery) without creating a simâ†’UI dependency. Pattern: when sim code needs a derived value that lives in UI, write a simpler inline version that reads the same GameState fields. Accept the duplication cost â€” it's smaller than the dependency cost. If the two calculations diverge significantly in the future, extract to a shared sim-level utility.

**Reluctant compliance is a distinct presidential outcome category, not a variant of ordinary approval (2026-04-04):** When a president launches an operation against a commander's explicit recommendation to postpone or abort â€” without spending CA and without triggering the Direct Intervention flag â€” the institutional record is meaningfully different from an ordinary approved launch. The command chain complied under presidential direction rather than in agreement with it. Collapsing both into "Approved" hides a pattern that matters for accountability and future institutional damage. Pattern: distinguish three outcome tiers from the launch snapshot: `ordinary_compliance` (commander said launch, president agreed â€” clean channel), `reluctant_compliance` (commander said postpone/abort, president overrode without CA â€” compliance under direction), `direct_intervention` (CA spent, flag set â€” override with cost). The `was_force_launched` field distinguishes the last from the first two; `commander_assessment_at_launch` distinguishes the first two from each other. Priority: direct_intervention overrides reluctant even if assessmentAtLaunch is postpone. Apply this three-tier pattern to any future decision surface where the cost paid (CA, resources) is separate from the institutional weight of the decision.

**Institutional interpretation surfaces should fire on clean approvals when context exists, not only on overrides (2026-04-04):** `DirectInterventionSection` only fires when the player overrides a reluctant commander. But command strain is a pre-decision context that matters even on clean approvals â€” a player launching an operation with a compromised corps should see the institutional friction before they click. Pattern: separate the "override cost display" (fires only on override path) from the "institutional context preview" (fires whenever strain > 0, regardless of approval path). The former is `DirectInterventionSection`; the latter is `OrderInterpretationSection`. Silence = healthy applies to both â€” neither fires at strain=0. Apply this separation to any future decision surface where context and cost are distinct: show context pre-decision, show cost at override.

**Lanes are not closed until docs match code truth â€” plan checkboxes and roadmap wording both need updating (2026-04-04):** A lane is not done when the last test passes. It is done when three things are true: (1) the implementation report exists, (2) the plan doc has all checkboxes checked and the status line updated, and (3) the roadmap wording reflects completion. Skipping the doc propagation wave leaves the plan in a zombie state â€” showing `[ ]` unchecked items for work that is actually complete, which misleads future sessions and audit passes. Pattern: always treat doc propagation as a mandatory final wave, not optional polish. The plan doc and roadmap are contracts with future sessions, not just historical records.

**Dual-path forks must always emit a diagnostic when the fallback fires (2026-04-04):** Any engine function that has a canonical path and a legacy fallback path must make the fallback visible. When `hasLiveSectorFrontlineTruth()` is false, activity counts come from `political_controllers` contact graph rather than hardened sector edges â€” activity summary data in those turns is approximate. The `console.warn` in `evaluateDisplacementTriggers` enforces this: silence means the canonical path fired; a warn means a developer or QA run should investigate why sectors were absent. Pattern: for every fork of the form `canonical ? canonicalPath() : legacyFallback()`, emit a warning on the fallback branch with the condition that triggered it and the quality implication. Never let a fallback fire silently â€” silent fallbacks make activity/reporting drift invisible.

**Vocabulary collisions between engine enums and UI severity strings cause insidious bugs (2026-04-04):** The string `'active'` was used both as a brigade `FormationState.status` value (engine-wide) and as a `ThreatItem.severity` level (UI). Any grep, type guard, or future `switch` on `'active'` could conflate the two. Fixed by renaming the threat severity to `'offensive'` â€” a term unambiguous in context. Pattern: when defining severity/state enums for UI display objects, always check that the string values are distinct from engine-level status enums (`'active'`, `'inactive'`, `'reserve'`, `'destroyed'`). Prefer domain-specific terms (`'offensive'`, `'hardened'`, `'gap'`) over generic lifecycle terms that the engine already owns.

**When demoting a brigade from assigned to reserve, always clear derived cache fields on the formation object (2026-04-04):** The sector pipeline sets `formation.assigned_sub_segment_id` as a derived cache field via `syncSectorAssignmentsToFormations` + `assignBrigadesToSubSegments`. When `assertBrigadeReachability` detects an unreachable brigade and the demotion loop moves it from `assigned_brigade_ids` â†’ `reserve_brigade_ids`, it must also clear `formation.assigned_sub_segment_id = undefined`. Failing to do so leaves the formation carrying stale sub-segment truth that survives into the UI adapter, which reads the field directly. General pattern: any pipeline step that demotes or reassigns a formation must clear all derived cache fields that were set under the old assignment. The UI adapter is a consumer of cached formation fields â€” it should always see post-demotion truth, not pre-demotion remnants.

**Adapter canonical derivation: build reverse maps from authoritative sources before the main loop (2026-04-04):** When the `GameStateAdapter` derives a field from two possible sources â€” a canonical engine structure and a cached formation field â€” always build the reverse map from the canonical source once before the main formation loop, then do O(1) lookup per formation. In this case: `corps_front_sectors[*].sub_segments[*].primary_brigade_ids` â†’ reverse map `brigadeId â†’ sub_segment_id`, built before the formation loop, used as the primary source; formation field is fallback only. This pattern avoids O(brigades Ã— sectors Ã— sub_segments) per-brigade search and ensures the canonical source always wins over a stale cached field. Apply the same pattern to any adapter field that has both a live canonical structure and a cached formation copy.

**Sector assignment lessons â€” territory presence is not frontline truth (2026-04-04):** Phase 1.5 of `classifyBrigadesByTerritory` previously assigned brigades to sectors based on territory membership (OSID in `sector.territory_osids`) without verifying that the brigade could physically reach the sector front. This allowed deep-rear brigades â€” sometimes in topologically ambiguous positions adjacent to a different-component sector â€” to appear as sector-assigned. Fix: require BFS front-adjacency within `TRUTHFUL_SECTOR_REACHABILITY_MAX_HOPS` (30) friendly hops before writing to `assigned_brigade_ids`. Brigades that fail the check go to `remaining` for Phase 2 + downstream repair, NOT to reserve â€” premature reserve insertion in Phase 1.5 causes false state in disconnected topologies. Pattern: every sector assignment step must check physical reachability, not just administrative/territorial membership. The component check is necessary but not sufficient â€” a brigade can be in the same connected component as a sector but physically separated from its front by a narrow gap.

**assertBrigadeReachability must return actionable results, not just log (2026-04-04):** A diagnostic assertion that calls `console.error` and returns `void` is theater. The violations are logged but the false state survives into runtime. Changed `assertBrigadeReachability` to return `string[]` of unreachable brigade IDs; the caller in `corps_front_sectors.ts` demotes them from `assigned_brigade_ids` to `reserve_brigade_ids`. Does NOT throw â€” hard-crashing on a corrupted save is worse than a logged demotion. Pattern: any invariant check that detects broken state must either fix it or prevent it â€” logging alone is insufficient when the engine subsequently reads the broken state as truth.

**Compatibility-only fields need dual comments: canonical block + test-contract text (2026-04-04):** `brigade_front_assignment` at `game_state.ts:1697` needed both an expanded `// COMPATIBILITY-ONLY:` block (explaining no live writers, what owns truth instead, and the relevant report) AND the original `/** Legacy compatibility fallback only... */` JSDoc text. The reason for both: `engine_honesty_legacy_contracts.test.ts` asserts the exact JSDoc substring as a contract check. Replacing the JSDoc with comment-style text broke the test. Pattern: when adding expanded documentation to a compatibility field, preserve the existing JSDoc text that tests assert, and add the expanded block above it. Never replace tested documentation strings without updating the tests.

**Interpretation categories classify institutional drag type, not commander personality (2026-04-04):** `OrderInterpretationCategory` (strain_shaped / caution_driven / feasibility_constrained / tempo_resistant) names what the SYSTEM is doing to the order, not who the commander is as a person. `strain_shaped` means institutional friction is the shaping force (strain > 0). `caution_driven` means professional judgment is operating cleanly (strain = 0, assessment = postpone/abort). `feasibility_constrained` means a hard physical limit exists (siege, force deficit, defensive duty). `tempo_resistant` means the operation window is closing (deteriorating/not_viable trend). This distinction is load-bearing: "caution-driven" is not "stubborn commander" â€” it is the system correctly signaling that conditions are not met without any friction event. Pattern: when designing command explanation categories, classify the INSTITUTIONAL PHENOMENON (what is blocking the order), not an attributed personality trait. The category should be falsifiable from GameState fields, not inferred from a vague "personality" flag.

**Interpretation section firing scope: ALL reluctant assessments, not just strain > 0 (2026-04-04):** `OrderInterpretationSection` previously fired only when strain > 0. A commander could recommend abort at strain = 0 â€” no friction, no override, just honest infeasibility â€” and the section stayed silent, leaving the player with no label for the resistance. The expanded firing condition (postpone OR abort, regardless of strain) closes this gap. The `caution_driven` category handles the strain=0 reluctance case. Pattern: when an explanation section guards on a proxy (strain > 0) rather than the underlying question (is there resistance to explain?), audit whether the proxy can be false while the phenomenon is present. The correct guard is: "does the player have resistance to interpret?" not "is strain elevated?"

**Windows 11 notification method audit (2026-04-04, host: 10.0.26200):** Six methods tested in isolation. Results: BurntToast -- not installed (skipped silently). `msg *` -- exits 0 but no visible popup appears; Windows 11 without terminal services configured silently discards it. `Windows.UI.Notifications` via `Add-Type -AssemblyName Windows.UI.Notifications` -- fails with "assembly could not be found"; the WinRT assembly is not accessible via the `-AssemblyName` shorthand in PowerShell 5 on this machine. `Add-Type -TypeDefinition` with `ContentType=WindowsRuntime` inline C# -- fails; the C# compiler rejects `[void]` as a keyword in that position. Both WinRT paths are dead on this configuration without BurntToast installed. `WScript.Shell Popup` -- **CONFIRMED WORKING**; creates a real modal dialog that is always visible regardless of Focus Assist state; auto-dismisses after configurable timeout (8s used); returns 1 (user pressed OK) or -1 (auto-dismissed). This is the canonical method. Rule: on Windows 11 without BurntToast, WScript.Shell Popup is the only reliable visual notification. Do not assume `msg *` or WinRT paths work -- test explicitly on the target machine.

**Stop hooks fire before the parent script finishes post-processing â€” never use them for completion notifications (2026-04-04):** Claude's Stop hook fires as soon as the `claude` subprocess exits, not when the calling script finishes. If `run_handoff.ps1` invokes `claude` and then writes `response.md`, updates `meta.json`, generates review artifacts, and sends Slack *after* the subprocess exits, the Stop hook races all of that post-processing and always loses. Any notification fired from the Stop hook will announce completion before the artifacts exist. Pattern: use the Stop hook only for lightweight, artifact-free side-effects (e.g. writing a `completion_signal.json` diagnostic marker). Fire the user-facing completion notification at the end of the parent script, after all artifacts are committed. Apply the same reasoning to any hook that fires on subprocess exit but wraps a script with post-processing: the hook is not "done", the parent script is "done".

**Two resolution speeds create a real player cost curve (2026-04-04):** When a mechanic has a resolution loop (friction accumulates â†’ player resolves), giving the player two speeds â€” slow/free (acknowledge one event) and fast/expensive (stabilize all at once, pay CA) â€” creates a genuine cost-benefit decision without making either path "correct". The slow path (Wave 3 one-by-one acknowledgement) is free but takes player attention per-event. The fast path (Wave 4 Stabilize, pay CA) costs a limited resource but clears the board in one click. Neither path changes how strain decays â€” both set `resolved: true` on friction events, which `computeCorpsCommandStrain()` excludes next read. The CA cost differentiates by crisis severity: 10 for strained (minor), 15 for compromised (major). Pattern: when a mechanic has accumulation + resolution, consider whether a "bulk resolution with cost" action would meaningfully engage the player versus a single "acknowledge all" button. The cost makes the decision real; without cost, bulk resolution is just a UX convenience.

**Constraint gates on player actions must live in the IPC handler, not just the UI (2026-04-04):** When a constraint prevents a player action (e.g. stance gate: can't set offensive when compromised), the enforcement must live in the IPC handler (`electron-main.cjs`) â€” not only in UI disable logic. The UI disable is a UX convenience that prevents confusion; the IPC handler is the security layer that prevents bypass (e.g. via direct API calls, dev tools, or future UI changes). Pattern: UI sends the request â†’ IPC handler validates the constraint â†’ rejects with `{ ok: false, reason: 'compromised', error: ... }` â†’ UI surfaces the error. The `reason` field in the rejection allows the UI to show a contextual message without parsing error strings. Applied: `stage-corps-stance-order` now validates strain before accepting 'offensive'; `ArmyHQCorpsCard` disables the option in the dropdown. Both layers agree on the constraint. Generalises: any state-dependent action gate (supply gate on ops, exhaustion gate on stance) needs this two-layer pattern.

**Inline CJS strain computation is correct when TypeScript cannot be imported (2026-04-04):** `electron-main.cjs` is CommonJS and cannot import TypeScript modules (e.g. `command_strain.ts`). When the IPC handler needs a derived computation from the TypeScript layer, inline a faithful copy of the relevant logic. The inline must be: (1) mechanically identical to the canonical function (same constants, same decay formula, same sort order); (2) documented with "mirrors command_strain.ts" comment; (3) kept in sync if the canonical function changes. Do NOT: call the TypeScript module via `require()` in CJS, introduce a JSON config file for constants, or duplicate constants in a separate CJS helper without the canonical comment. The inline copy is a known limitation of the CJS/ESM boundary, not a design preference. If it becomes a maintenance burden, the right fix is migrating electron-main.cjs to ESM â€” not adding an indirect import layer.

**Friction resolution loops need composite keys, not stored IDs, when events have no natural identity (2026-04-04):** When wiring a player acknowledgement flow against engine-generated events that lack a stored `id` field, prefer a deterministic composite key over adding a new stored field. Pattern: `officerId:turn:type` is deterministic within a run, unique enough for the UI routing case, and requires no schema migration. The IPC handler matches by `officer_id + turn + type + resolved===false` â€” three fields that already existed. Adding a `uuid` or auto-increment id would require touching the engine type, the serializer, and every friction event creation site for no practical gain. Tradeoffs: composite key breaks if the same officer generates two events of the same type on the same turn (prevented by `deterministicRandom` producing one outcome per turn per officer). Generalises to: any acknowledgement flow where the event's uniqueness is guaranteed by domain constraints on the generating system.

**Closing a resolution loop requires only three pieces: existing filter, write path, and review surface (2026-04-04):** The Wave 3 friction resolution loop was viable because three pieces already existed independently: (1) `computeCorpsCommandStrain()` already filtered `!e.resolved` â€” the gate was there; (2) `FrictionEvent.resolved: boolean` already existed in the stored type â€” the write target was there; (3) `friction_events[]` was already the canonical engine array. The only thing missing was an IPC path to set `resolved: true` and a UI surface to show the unresolved items. Lesson: before designing a new mechanic, audit whether the residue of existing mechanics already has the required pieces. The resolution gate (`!e.resolved`) had been filtering events for turns without any player ever being able to exercise it. This is the "dormant gate" pattern â€” an engine filter designed for a player action that was never wired up.

**Silence = healthy is the correct default for institutional signals (2026-04-04):** When surfacing a derived institutional signal (command strain, supply pressure, officer friction) across multiple UI surfaces, the correct default is silence at the healthy threshold â€” not a zero-state badge or placeholder text. Three surfaces, three different manifestations of the same signal: CoS briefing paragraph (appears only when strain > 0, none of the document structure changes otherwise), OperationsSection notice (appears only when strain > 0 AND ops exist â€” two conditions must both be true), OperationBriefingModal compound warning (appears only when strain > 0 inside the pre-launch panel). In all three cases the zero-strain read path executes identically to pre-feature code. This is the correct pattern for information surfaces: they add signal, not noise. A "healthy" badge is noise; absence is signal. Generalises to any advisory indicator: supply sufficiency notice, commander confidence warning, exhaustion alert â€” only show when actionable, absent when nominal.

**Multi-surface signal propagation via existing prop channels (2026-04-04):** When propagating a derived signal to multiple UI surfaces, always audit what data is already available at each surface before adding new props or new GameState fields. Pattern: (1) check if the surface already has gameState or a relevant view-model; (2) if yes, import the utility and compute locally (B1 pattern â€” ChiefOfStaffBriefing); (3) if a parent already computed it, pass it down as optional props with healthy defaults (B2 pattern â€” OperationsSection via ArmyHQCorpsCard); (4) if the modal already reads from store, pass computed values into the sub-component directly (B3 pattern â€” DirectInterventionSection). Never add a GameState field just to make a derived value passable â€” derive it at the nearest convenient point. The three Wave 2 surfaces used all three patterns without any new GameState fields.

**Derive gameplay indicators on-read rather than storing derived state on GameState (2026-04-04):** When a gameplay metric can be computed from existing fields (e.g. was_force_launched + friction_events + current_turn), do not add a new GameState field for it. Instead, implement a pure utility function in the UI data layer (e.g. `src/ui/map/data/command_strain.ts`) that derives the value at read time. Benefits: no serialization burden, no GameState schema migration, no save-file compatibility risk, no divergence between stored and computed truth. The utility must be deterministic (sorted inputs, no Math.random(), no Date.now()), pure (no side effects), and owned by a single file with a clear canonical doc comment. The adapter layer calls the utility and populates view-model fields (e.g. FormationView.commandStrain) from it. Pattern generalizes to: effective supply status, threat level, diplomatic pressure score, operational momentum â€” any quantity that is a function of existing state rather than new independent state. Contrast: do NOT derive things that have real causal history that cannot be reconstructed from current state (e.g. "was this op postponed before it launched" must be snapshotted at decision time, not re-derived).

**WebP is canonical for all live UI/runtime assets (2026-04-04):** `src/ui/` imports zero `.png` files at runtime. Canonical live format is `.webp` for scene plates, flags, crests, textures, and UI art. PNG remains valid only for: raw/source art (`raw_sora/`), archived legacy (`_old/`), generated reports/docs outputs, and officer source photos. When adding new live runtime art, convert to `.webp` before importing. Tool: `warroom_resize_assets.ts` produces `.png` only â€” convert separately with sharp before committing as live asset. Vite MIME map in `src/ui/warroom/vite.config.ts` now includes `.webp â†’ image/webp`. Any future `.png` found alongside a `.webp` twin in a live asset folder is a deletion candidate unless explicitly justified.

**Exact numbers in staff estimates are always lies â€” bucket them (2026-04-03):** When a UI surface renders engine-computed values as precise numbers (force ratio to 2 decimal places, exact casualty integers, raw defense strength) it is lying to the player. The president receives a staff briefing, not a simulation trace. Pattern: replace exact numbers with bucketed uncertainty ranges and qualify with "estimated"/"assessed". Force ratio â†’ "Favorable (>1.5:1)" / "Contested" / "Unfavorable". Casualties â†’ "Light / Moderate / Heavy / Severe". Strength category â†’ "limited enemy presence (assessed)" not "THIN". Confidence â†’ "High / Moderate / Low confidence" not "73%". The tab name "RawIntelTab" was the tell â€” it was always debug content masquerading as a player feature. When in doubt: if the label sounds like an engine variable, it should not be in normal play. Applied: RawIntelTab demoted from G2Phase ops clipboard; generateThreatAssessment.ts STRENGTH_DISPLAY map + describeConfidence() bucketing. Canonical reference: PLAYER_VISIBLE_STATE.md, DEBUG_SURFACE_POLICY.md.

**Live fields are not provenance â€” always snapshot at decision time (2026-04-03):** Any engine field that is recomputed each turn cannot serve as a historical record. `commander_assessment` is recomputed by the preparation pipeline every turn, so by the time an operation is in execution its value is stale relative to what the commander actually recommended when the president decided. Pattern: when the player makes a decision against a live field, snapshot it immediately into a dedicated `_at_launch` / `_at_decision` field set in the IPC handler â€” not deferred to pipeline re-reads. The live field stays authoritative for current state; the snapshot field is authoritative for provenance/history. Applied: `commander_assessment_at_launch` set in both `stage-operation-force-launch` and `stage-operation-decision` handlers in electron-main.cjs, carried into `OperationAAR`, mapped through GameStateAdapter, rendered in `CommandRecord` (modal) and provenance sentence (history panel). The same pattern generalises to any decision made against a live signal: force ratio, supply, readiness â€” snapshot at the moment of commitment.

**Command authority is the first real cost on presidential overrides (2026-04-03):** `CommandAuthority` on `MilitaryState` (current/max/spent_this_turn/lifetime_spent) is the player's resource for Level 3 Direct Intervention. Force-launch costs 15; recovery is +2/turn (base rate). The `recover-command-authority` war pipeline step runs after `apply-sector-stance-orders`. The IPC handler blocks the action when authority is insufficient â€” the cost is enforced server-side, not just UI-side. Future Level 3 actions (manual move = 5, override stance = 10, override recommendation = 20) should follow the same pattern: check in electron-main.cjs, deduct, serialize. `command_authority` is distinct from `political_capital` â€” the former is the player's, the latter is the non-player political bot's.

**Transient flags need permanent shadows for provenance (2026-04-03):** `force_launch` on CorpsOperation is cleared by `sector_offensive.ts` when entering recovery â€” before AAR compilation reads it. Pattern: when a flag is consumed/cleared during the lifecycle but must survive into history, add a permanent `was_X` shadow field set at the moment of the action, never cleared. Applied: `was_force_launched` set in electron-main.cjs alongside `force_launch`, read by `finalizeOperationAAR` to populate `OperationAAR.force_launched` + `ca_cost_at_launch`. The shadow field lives on the operation object, not in a side-channel log, keeping provenance collocated with the entity.

**Delegation/override substrate must precede all downstream command milestones (2026-04-03):** The embryonic audit found 18+ player IPC actions, none tagged by command level, none carrying cost. 8 embryonic mechanisms exist (stance_source, warlord_friction, army_hq_overrides, political_leader_types) but none surface to the player. The 6-phase execution plan (`docs/plans/2026-04-03-delegation-override-command-friction-plan.md`) must complete Phase 1 (command level tags) and Phase 2 (command_authority field) before v0.8.2 Political Leader Bot, v0.8.3 Order Interpretation, or v0.8.4 Autonomy can build on real foundations. Canonical terminology: command_authority = player resource for military overrides; political_capital = non-player political bot resource. Do not conflate them.

**Events system has 29-turn decision gaps despite being more capable than used (2026-04-03):** The event system already has 22 condition types, 11 effect types, pressure/recurrence/flags, and 95 events â€” but decision events cluster in 1992 and late 1995, leaving mid-1993 (29 turns) and 1994 (20 turns) with no player choice. Three presidential event types (Strategic Posture Review, Visit to the Front, Humanitarian Crisis Response) can ship as v0.8.0.x pure JSON content with zero engine changes, filling these gaps. Two more (Patron Pressure Response, Commander Confidence Crisis) gain depth from v0.8.2. Design: `docs/40_reports/implemented/20260403_PRESIDENTIAL_DECISIONS_BETWEEN_OPS.md`.

**Duplicate toolbar affordances silently confuse shell ownership (2026-04-03):** The date label and CHRONICLE button both opened Chronicle â€” two adjacent elements doing the same thing violates the one-canonical-entrypoint principle. When auditing shell ownership, check for duplicate click handlers on adjacent elements. The canonical path for Chronicle from the toolbar is the CHRONICLE button only; the date label is now a non-interactive display.

**Master roadmap must say the player is the president, not just the doctrine docs (2026-04-03):** It is not enough for `PRESIDENTIAL_COMMAND_DOCTRINE.md`, `UI_OWNERSHIP_MATRIX.md`, and `.claude/napkin.md` to remember the role. `MASTER_ROADMAP.md` also needs an explicit player-role reminder in the active command-chain section or planners will drift back toward brigade-commander assumptions. Keep the roadmap loud about the delegated presidential loop.

**"Visit to the front" belongs in the event/political layer, not a new subsystem (2026-04-03):** If AWWV adds presidential presence moments between operations, implement them as lightweight event-driven choices attached to a corps/sector that temporarily alter morale, urgency, commander compliance, and visibility. Do not turn this into a travel simulator, separate map mode, or unit-control mechanic.

**Presidential command doctrine is now a single referenceable contract (2026-04-03):** `docs/20_engineering/PRESIDENTIAL_COMMAND_DOCTRINE.md` defines the player as faction president with three command levels: Level 1 Strategic Guidance (default loop â€” posture, events, priorities, reserves, plan approval), Level 2 Army/Corps Directives (active but delegated â€” stance, ops, commanders, OPSEC, loans), Level 3 Direct Intervention (exceptional override with visible cost â€” force-launch, override, manual repositioning). All shells, event text, briefings, and future mechanics must conform. Shell ownership: Warroom = LIVES, Army HQ = VISITS, Tactical Map = OBSERVES. Any new player-facing feature must name which command level it belongs to before implementation.

**Adapter defense-in-depth: filter faction-keyed families at adapter output (2026-04-03):** When the adapter ships faction-keyed records (`casualtyLedger`, `factionReserves`, `warPhaseSupplyPressure`, `warPhaseExhaustion`) to the renderer, scope them to the player's faction at the adapter return block via `scopeToPlayerFaction()`. Components already gate by `isPlayerFaction`, but adapter-level filtering prevents future component mistakes from leaking enemy truth. Observer mode (no `player_faction`) passes all factions through unchanged. This is the canonical defense-in-depth pattern for any new faction-keyed family added to `LoadedGameState`.

**Map-first principle for toolbar and shortcut affordances (2026-04-03):** High-frequency player interactions (ops snapshot, war summary) belong on the tactical map as lightweight summaries. Army HQ is for deep review (records, AAR, personnel, briefing drill-down). When a map-local surface already owns a concept (OperationsPanel, WarSummaryModal), toolbar buttons and keyboard shortcuts should open that surface first, with an explicit "HQ Review" handoff for deeper investigation. Do not route quick-glance affordances through the Army HQ modal.

**DragFactor is a structural truth pattern, not a display convenience (2026-04-04):** `DragFactor` (source + intensity + isPrimary + label) names a SYSTEM phenomenon at the right level of abstraction. `source` says what institutional force is acting (command_strain / professional_caution / hard_constraint / timing_gap). `isPrimary` says whether it is the dominant driver or a contributing factor. `intensity` and `label` are human-readable derivations from the same inputs that produced the category. This structure is load-bearing: the bullet list UI reads isPrimary to pick â— vs â—‹, and the label to render without format decisions in the component. Pattern: when a derivation produces multiple concurrent signals, model each as a typed factor object rather than concatenating prose. The component owns rendering; the derivation owns classification.

**cautionNotice is a fallback safety net, not the primary rendering path (2026-04-04):** Wave 1 introduced `cautionNotice` as the prose explanation for reluctant assessments. Wave 2 demotes it: when `dragFactors` is non-empty, the bullet list is the primary rendering path and `cautionNotice` is not shown. `cautionNotice` fires only when `dragFactors` is empty â€” covering callers that do not supply the full parameter set or derivation paths that predate the DragFactor model. Pattern: when adding a structured replacement for a prose fallback, never delete the fallback â€” keep it as a backward-compatibility net and document clearly which is primary. Silence is not the same as deletion: the cautionNotice path still exists and still fires for any path that needs it.

**Unresolved-sector warnings must come from the final canonical unresolved set (2026-04-03):** If a sector pipeline has `same-corps assignment -> enclave rescue -> coverage -> review -> final sync`, then classification-stage warnings are not authoritative. In AWWV, `PENDING_ENCLAVE_REVIEW` from `classifyBrigadesByTerritory(...)` was an example of intermediate diagnostics impersonating final truth. The real unresolved warning owner is the final unresolved set after sector sync.

**Stale `assigned_sub_segment_id` is false frontline residue (2026-04-03):** `assigned_sub_segment_id` is not harmless metadata. `commander_march_correction.ts` treats it as live frontage authority. If a brigade leaves sector ownership and keeps that field, the repo now has two contradictory truths about where the brigade belongs. Clear `assigned_sub_segment_id` whenever sector sync rebuilds assignments; let sub-segment assignment repopulate it only for brigades that are truly back on a sector line.

**Sector truth needs a final physical-ownership pass after late writers (2026-04-03):** Contiguity fixes and enclave-rescue fixes are not enough if later passes can still re-roster brigades on paper. In AWWV, sector output is only honest after a final strip pass that removes any brigade whose current `location_osid` is not on the sector front, in the sector territory, or one hop behind as reserve. Emit unresolved warnings only after that final pass, otherwise the warning stream and the sector roster disagree about what the engine really believes.

**Claude quality must be enforced by repo rails, not repeated reminders (2026-04-03):** If you want future Claude agents to behave at studio level, do not rely on each session restating taste or standards. Put the behavior into a short execution rail and wire the command surfaces to read it. `docs/20_engineering/CLAUDE_EXECUTION_STANDARD.md` now serves that role.
