**Legacy SID combat is reporting fallback, not co-equal combat truth (2026-04-03):** `attack_resolution_osid` is the canonical war-phase combat summary. `resolve_attack_orders` survives only as a compatibility fallback when operational combat data is unavailable. Rule for future work: any scenario rollup, diagnostic, or report that can see both summaries must prefer OSID output first and only fall back to SID data when the canonical OSID report is absent.

**Officer growth must read sector frontline truth, not brigade posture (2026-04-03):** `updateBrigadeOfficerQuality(...)` used to treat any non-`defend` posture as "frontline" service. That is false authority because posture is mutable shell-era residue while sectors are the live frontline owner. Rule for future work: if live `corps_front_sectors` exist, progression and drift systems must use sector/frontline assignment, not posture heuristics. A posture fallback is only acceptable when no live sector truth exists at all.

**Deep shell concepts need one owner even when multiple shells can open them (2026-04-03):** War Summary now lives in `army_hq/WarSummaryContent`, and `WarSummaryModal` is only a presentation wrapper. Rule for future work: if Tactical Map, Army HQ, Warroom, or Codex can all open the same concept, only one component may own the actual model/logic; every other path must wrap or hand off to it instead of recreating a second truth standard.

**Operation selection should open the tactical ops shell, not create a second tactical detail owner (2026-04-03):** `selectedOperationKey` now opens `OperationsPanel`, and the old rail-mounted `OperationDetail` surface is gone. Rule for future work: Tactical Map gets one map-facing operations surface; deeper review belongs to Army HQ/briefing flows, not a second tactical panel family.

**Summary shells must not reconstruct front truth from compatibility fields (2026-04-03):** `SituationTab` used to fall back to `assignableFrontSegments.length` when canonical `frontPressureByEdge` data was absent. That kept a second front-summary authority alive in the UI. Rule for future work: if canonical front/pressure summary data is absent, show zero or unavailable state and fix the producer instead of re-deriving from compatibility leftovers.

**Codex is a shell handoff, not a Records subtab (2026-04-03):** Army HQ Records may link into Codex, but it must present that as a handoff to a separate shell. Rule for future work: if clicking a control opens a global shell, do not visually model it as one more inline subtab of the current shell.

**Front tooltips must read sector truth, not compatibility front segments (2026-04-03):** The live front tooltip no longer derives persistence/context from `assignableFrontSegments`; it uses `corpsFrontSectors` instead. Rule for future work: once a player-facing front feature has a canonical sector owner, do not let tooltip or hover helpers sneak compatibility front DTOs back into the shell.

**Compatibility front snapshots should not ride in live player DTOs (2026-04-03):** Once summaries, tooltips, and shell actions stop consuming `assignable_front_segments`, remove the matching `LoadedGameState` field too. Leaving compatibility snapshots in the live DTO teaches future implementers that the old concept still owns player truth.

**Dead helpers inside authoritative engine files are future bugs (2026-04-03):** An unused helper like `deriveCorpsFrontMapping(...)` is more dangerous inside `bot_corps_directives.ts` than in an obvious archive folder. If it reads compatibility state from a live AI file, future work will eventually treat that compatibility lane as canonical again. Delete it once no live caller remains.

**Deleting a rebuild path is not enough if the schema still blesses the old concept (2026-04-03):** `local_fronts` had already stopped rebuilding every turn, but as long as `GameState` still declared the field and the live AI hub still re-exported front-compat helpers, the repo kept teaching future work that the concept was still real. Rule for future cleanup: when a compatibility lane loses runtime authority, remove the schema residue and public export surface in the same wave or it will grow back through inference rather than code.

**Campaign memory is its own shell, not a flavor variant of records (2026-04-03):** In AWWV, `Chronicle` is the campaign-memory owner, `Army HQ Records` owns operational review, and `Codex` owns static reference. Rule for future work: if a Warroom prop like the newspaper wants to show â€œwhat happened,â€ hand it off to Chronicle once the tactical shell exists instead of building another local archive packet.

**Zero-battle deployment warnings must gate on live battle ownership before they accuse a brigade of inactivity (2026-04-09):** `brigade_never_fights` was still sweeping every active brigade with `battles_fought = 0`, which mixed together live front/reserve brigades, canonical Graz cold-front brigades, and assignment-null formations with no current battle owner. Pattern: if an anomaly is implicitly asking "why hasn't this brigade fought yet?", it must first prove the brigade still has a live sector/loan owner on a non-frozen front. Otherwise the detector is accusing protected silence and ownerless drift with the same vocabulary as truthful active deployment.

**Density outlier heuristics must exclude canonical no-combat fronts before computing faction medians (2026-04-09):** `frontline_density_imbalance` was still counting `hvo_tomislavgrad` as an HRHB low-density failure even though the sim already owned that RS-HRHB frontage as a Graz cold front through `isSectorColdFront(...)`. Pattern: if an anomaly computes pressure, density, or deployment balance from active sectors, first remove sectors covered by explicit no-combat contracts (cold fronts, truces, freezes) before calculating medians or outliers. Otherwise the anomaly layer reinterprets protected silence as misdeployment and poisons the faction baseline for the sectors that really are active.

**A dead field with a live writer is still a live lie (2026-04-03):** `assignable_front_segments` had already lost its real shell/AI readers, but the turn pipeline kept rebuilding it every turn. Rule for future cleanup: once a compatibility field loses its canonical readers, remove the runtime writer immediately even if the field itself has to survive a little longer for old saves or tests.

**Parallel reserve-loan prototype rails are a future-bug magnet even when runtime no longer imports them (2026-04-03):** `army_reserve_system.ts` is the live elite-loan owner; the old `elite_loan.ts` prototype and its dedicated tests were retired because they encoded a different lifecycle while no longer driving runtime. Rule for future work: when a subsystem gains a new canonical owner, delete the old prototype rail once it stops serving live runtime, or the next cleanup will drift onto the wrong implementation just because it still looks neat and test-backed.

**Missing roadmap lanes should become execution-grade plans before the work starts (2026-04-03):** Sector/frontline truth, UI density + shell cohesion, and product architecture simplification are now covered by `docs/plans/2026-04-03-v080x-sector-frontline-truth-plan.md`, `docs/plans/2026-04-03-v08to09-ui-density-and-shell-cohesion-plan.md`, and `docs/plans/2026-04-03-v08to09-product-architecture-simplification-plan.md`. Rule for future work: if a priority becomes Ã¢â‚¬Å“obviousÃ¢â‚¬Â in chat but has no execution-grade Pyrrhic plan, write the plan before implementation so the repo does not drift back to implicit ownership.
- 2026-04-03: Tactical shell authority rule â€” `PresidentialToolbar` is the mounted tactical-map shell. `TopToolbar` is legacy reference only. Global shell shortcuts must route through canonical actions/IPC, never through DOM text/button scans.
- 2026-04-03: Scenario harness rule â€” `src/scenario/scenario_runner.ts` may report or apply real breach outcomes, but it may not seed synthetic `front_posture`, brigade edge assignments, `front_segments`, or `front_pressure` to force them.
- 2026-04-03: Supply pressure and war exhaustion rule â€” when `corps_front_sectors` exists, it is the frontline authority for frontage/exposure mechanics. Legacy front-edge/front-descriptor inputs survive only as compatibility fallback.

- 2026-04-03: Summary-generator naming rule - helper builders like `generateThreatAssessment(...)` are part of the player shell and must route visible names through `playerSafeText` helpers. Do not trust raw formation names in report/title generators just because they are not mounted React panels.
- 2026-04-03: Desktop bridge rule - preload owns fanout, shells only subscribe. If Warroom or tactical map ever needs to "re-register" for state updates, the bridge contract is wrong. Embedded shells must mirror the same event model, including turn reports, instead of inventing a weaker parallel callback path.
- 2026-04-03: Operations-review naming rule - Army HQ operations review, operation briefing, tactical operations detail, and officer replacement prompts must derive corps/brigade/place labels from player-safe helpers (`getPlayerSafeCorpsName`, `getPlayerSafeBrigadeName`, `getOsidDisplayName`). These are command-trust surfaces, not places for raw `corps_name` or fallback ids.
- 2026-04-03: Stale architecture docs are an active false-authority risk - if an older polished engineering doc still presents a retired shell like `TopToolbar` as live, future agents will route new work into the wrong surface. When a shell owner changes, update the code and the glossy architecture docs in the same wave.
- 2026-04-03: Shared detail panels must not become hidden shell routers - `SettlementDetailContent` is now renderer-only and takes explicit callbacks for sector/operation drilldown. Rule for future work: if a component is reused by tooltip and selection shells, it should render player-safe content and accept navigation callbacks from its owner instead of reading/writing global store state directly.
- 2026-04-03: Army HQ geography rule - ORBAT, sector detail, and operation-history review are primary command surfaces, so location names there must prefer canonical OSID display maps (`getOsidDisplayName`) over rough `formatOsidLabel(...)`/`humanizeOsid(...)` fallbacks.
- 2026-04-03: Warroom control language rule - settlement control status shown to the player must be humanized before render. Do not surface raw simulation enum casing/underscore vocabulary in Warroom administrative shells.
- 2026-04-03: AAR is a command-trust surface, not a debug dump - after-action reports must use player-safe faction labels, brigade names, and canonical place names. Do not let battle review panels fall back to raw brigade ids or `humanizeOsid(...)` just because they are "reporting" rather than issuing orders.
- 2026-04-03: Reserve shells should share the same geography contract as command shells - even narrow management panels like `ArmyReservePanel` should resolve base and staging locations through the canonical OSID display map instead of rough string humanization.
- 2026-04-03: Tactical-shell density rule - if one decorative toolbar element forces every rail and panel lower, that is not local styling debt; it is shell-envelope debt. Reduce the global clearance budget before micro-tuning inner panel padding.
- 2026-04-03: Chronicle and ops-planning hover are still player-facing report surfaces - they must use player-safe place and faction naming just like command panels, even if they are narrative or planning aids rather than the main shell.
- 2026-04-03: Warroom should hand off deep staff review instead of owning a parallel packet - faction overview, command briefing, and report-review desk props now route into canonical tactical-shell destinations through `src/ui/shared/shellHandoff.ts`, and the same handoff survives browser/dev new-tab launches through a one-shot query-param round trip. Rule for future work: when a Warroom interaction needs detailed staff depth, add a shell handoff to Army HQ/Codex rather than another Warroom-local modal owner.
- 2026-04-03: Dead shell packets should be deleted once the anchor routes directly to the owning shell - `OperationalSituationModal` survived after the desk map already routed straight to tactical play, which made Warroom look like it still owned a second operations packet. Rule for future work: if an anchor now hands off directly, delete the old intermediate modal instead of leaving it around as fallback theater.
- **`node:test` nested subtests are not a drop-in harness contract for Vitest (2026-04-15):** `tests/ui_map_game_state_adapter.test.ts` looked like a simple runner migration until Vitest exposed that two adapter assertions were never top-level tests at all - they were nested under prior `test(...)` bodies and only legal under `node:test` semantics. Rule for future work: when migrating a file from `node:test`, audit for nested `test(...)` declarations and flatten them into real top-level Vitest tests before declaring the migration complete.

- **Political/truce helper residue can hide both stale fixtures and stale behavioral claims at once (2026-04-15):** the vitest migration for `local_truces` and adjacent political helpers showed two distinct failure modes that look identical from a distance: shallow `GameState` fixtures that silently drop the `political` shell, and old assertions that still describe superseded live constants or blocking rules. Rule for future work: when a pure helper band fails after runner migration, audit both the fixture shell and the claimed runtime behavior before deciding whether the code or the test is wrong.

- **Nested-state harness drift can masquerade as officer-system regressions (2026-04-15):** `tests/officer_system.test.ts` initially looked like a live named-officer lookup failure after the vitest migration, but the actual break was the same stale pattern seen in earlier recruitment/militia residue: a shallow `...overrides` helper silently replaced canonical nested `military` / `political` partitions. Rule for future work: any "minimal state" fixture that accepts partial `GameState` overrides must merge namespaced owners explicitly, or test migrations will invent fake subsystem failures.

- 2026-04-03: Dead compatibility helpers are worse than noisy comments if they still look reusable - `src/state/theatres.ts` had no live imports, but its existence kept suggesting theatre tagging was still a plausible runtime layer. Rule for future work: once a compatibility helper loses every live importer, remove the file and replace it with explicit tests/docs rather than preserving a polished ghost.
- 2026-04-03: Dead shell APIs are still dangerous even when no UI calls them - the desktop bridge was still exporting `stageBrigadeAoROrder`, `stageCorpsFrontOrder`, and `stageOgSubfrontOrder` long after the live tactical shell stopped using them. Rule for future work: if a player-facing command path loses its live UI owner, retire it from preload/main-process/DesktopSim in the same wave instead of leaving a fake capability at the shell boundary.
- 2026-04-03: Dead runtime steps are more dangerous than dead files - `corps_front_assign.ts` looked legacy, but the real danger was that `war_phases.ts` still scheduled `ensure-derived-corps-front-edges` and `apply-corps-front-orders` every war turn while the functions themselves derived and assigned nothing. Rule for future work: if a subsystem becomes compatibility residue, remove its live pipeline steps and reporting hooks in the same wave, or the repo will keep treating a no-op as meaningful authority.
- 2026-04-03: Retired compatibility fields should not keep a ceremonial pipeline step - once `brigade_reposition_orders` stopped being a live player command and the adapter stopped exposing it, keeping `apply-brigade-reposition` in `war_phases.ts` was just ritualized false authority. Rule for future work: if a compatibility field survives only for old saves/tools, runtime should either ignore it completely or consume it in a narrowly owned migration path, never in the main turn pipeline.
- 2026-04-03: Polished canon docs can become false-authority bugs - when sectors become the frontline owner and shell ownership shifts, high-trust docs like `Systems_Manual_v0_7_0.md`, `context.md`, and `TACTICAL_MAP_SYSTEM.md` must be updated in the same wave or future agents will route new work back into retired front/theatre/toolbar lanes.
- 2026-04-03: Sector frontline truth can still vanish in reporting if two valid edge universes are compared without a bridge - `CorpsFrontSector.edge_ids` are OSID frontier edges, while some harness/report paths still walk canonical settlement edges. Rule for future work: when a subsystem accepts both canonical and operational geometry, explicitly bridge between them at the boundary instead of letting â€œno matchesâ€ collapse into fake zero activity.
- 2026-04-03: Local package CLIs are part of repo truth on Windows - if the repo can run only when `node_modules/.bin` shims happen to exist, the build/run contract is brittle. Rule for future work: canonical pipeline wrappers should invoke local package entrypoints directly or provide repo-local wrappers, not rely on PATH luck inside nested shell hops.
- 2026-04-04: Consolidation passes should happen every 5-8 micro-reports per lane to prevent doc drift â€” after the 2-day sprint produced 20+ micro-reports across 3 lanes, orientation cost for new agents became high. Consolidated lane summaries with canonical owner tables and end-to-end flow descriptions restore navigability. Micro-reports are preserved as traceability artifacts, not replaced.

- 2026-04-04: Interpretation category must reach the consequence surface, not just the explanation surface â€” Wave 1/2 gave players a category label and drag-factor list, but the override button copy was still category-blind. Rule for future work: when a derivation system classifies institutional drag by type, the classification must propagate to every downstream consequence surface (not just the explanation panel) or the category is only cosmetic.
- 2026-04-04: Pre-compute at modal level, pass as prop â€” when a display component needs a derived value that depends on other modal-level state, compute it in a useMemo at the parent level and pass as a pre-computed prop. Do not import derivation functions into dumb display components. This matches the existing ReadinessTrendIndicator / RecommendationDriverSection pattern and keeps display components dependency-free.

- 2026-04-04: Dispatch from the real bench, not a generic team block. Use `.claude/AGENT_TEAM_ROSTER.md` plus the repo taskforce briefs in `.claude/agents/` to fit specialists to the actual task shape. Do not name fictional or unavailable roles like `gap-finder`; use real challenge roles such as `architect`, `technical-architect`, `product-manager`, `quality-assurance-process`, `authority-auditor`, `ui-truth-keeper`, or `operations-reality-checker` as appropriate.

**`assignCrossCorpsEnclaveDefenders` fires for drifted brigades as well as genuine enclave defenders â€” discriminate by `home_osid` coverage (2026-04-04):** The enclave-defender repair pass in `brigade_assignment.ts` is designed for brigades physically isolated inside foreign-controlled territory. It also fires for drifted brigades â€” brigades whose `location_osid` was captured by foreign-corps operations or marches but whose home municipality is still in own-corps hands. The discriminator: a genuine enclave defender has no own-corps sector whose `territory_osids` includes the brigade's `home_osid`. A drifted brigade does. Gate on this check after the `ownCorpsHasSectorInComponent` pass; skip drifted brigades and leave them for normal repositioning/recall. Without this gate, drifted brigades are permanently re-homed into foreign corps sectors, corrupting density metrics and AI force evaluation for both sides.

**Sector split products can be zero-brigade â€” the pipeline has no post-split equalization within own corps (2026-04-04):** `splitNonContiguousSectors` partitions a parent sector's `territory_osids` by geographic connectivity. Brigades are assigned to child sectors only if their current location falls in the child's component. When parent brigades are concentrated in one component, the other child sector gets correct front edges but zero brigades. The assignment pipeline has no mechanism to recruit from adjacent own-corps sectors to fill these orphan children. Diagnostic: sector IDs with `split0`/`splitN` suffixes in the end report, cross-referenced with `assigned_brigade_count: 0`. Fix options: (a) splitter refuses to emit a zero-brigade child, or (b) post-split equalization pass borrows from sibling/adjacent own-corps sectors with surplus. Investigate in `sector_splitting.ts` + `brigade_assignment.ts`.

**Non-contiguous split children require a territory-membership pre-pass, not BFS equalization (2026-04-04):** The fill contract for split child sectors fails through two simultaneous blocks: (1) Phase 1 of `classifyBrigadesByTerritory` exhausts all brigades into the largest child via `location_osid` â†’ `frontOsidToSectorIndices`, leaving nothing for Phase 1.5; (2) `ensureMinimumSectorCoverage` Step 2 is double-blocked â€” the `> 1` donor guard and the cross-component BFS filter both reject split siblings, which are disconnected by definition. Correct repair: a territory-membership pre-pass at the start of `ensureMinimumSectorCoverage`, before Steps 1/2. For each zero-brigade sector with front edges, find a sibling brigade whose `location_osid` is in the zero-sector's `territory_osids`, is not on the sibling's frontline, and leaves the donor with â‰¥ 1 remaining. Move (not copy) one brigade. No BFS needed â€” territory membership is a stronger predicate than component reachability in this context. Rejected alternatives: (a) splitter refusal breaks the geographic split contract; (b) relaxing the cross-component filter still blocks on `> 1` guard. Pattern: when a repair pass is blocked by a connectivity check that is correct for its primary use case but wrong for disconnected split products, add a pre-pass that uses a more direct authority (territory membership) rather than weakening the BFS filter for all callers.
- 2026-04-04: Validation gates must anchor on the latest relevant complete runs, not older anomaly artifacts. If a gate compares against a run hundreds of sessions older than the current ATH baseline, first prove that run is still the intended comparator. Otherwise demote it as stale-run noise and do not let it block roadmap work.
**Derived front-edge refresh needs a matching final truth rebuild for every dependent authority (2026-04-08):** Re-deriving `war_front_edges_osid` near the end of the pipeline is not enough if dependent structures like `corps_front_sectors`, `unresolved_sector_brigades`, and `sector_combat_ratings` still reflect pre-recruitment or pre-recall state. That creates a split-brain save: newer front edges over older sector truth. Pattern: when a late pipeline step refreshes an authority that other derived structures depend on, either rebuild every dependent structure in the same late phase or explicitly mark the older structures non-authoritative and do not serialize them as final truth. Linked: `[2026-04-08] fix(engine): Seal sector builder and rebuild final sector truth after late writers`.
**Sibling front de-overlap must move edge truth, not just label truth (2026-04-08):** If two same-corps sectors share a friendly front OSID, deleting that OSID from the weaker siblingâ€™s `friendly_osids` without moving the corresponding `edge_ids` creates a silent contradiction: the sector still owns front edges whose friendly endpoint it no longer claims. Pattern: whenever front ownership changes, transfer the incident edge ownership first, then rebuild `sub_segments` from edge truth. Linked: `[2026-04-08] fix(engine): Make sector overlap cleanup edge-truth-safe and block late truth-violating transfers`.
**Late balancing passes need the same physical-truth gate as emergency coverage passes (2026-04-08):** It is not enough to protect `ensureMinimumSectorCoverage()`â€™s early rescue logic. Any later equalization or reinforcement pass that can move brigades between sibling sectors must also refuse to transfer brigades that donor sectors already truthfully own by frontline, territory, or one-hop reserve claim. Otherwise a system can appear repaired earlier in the builder and still serialize false sector ownership after a later â€œbalanceâ€ step. Linked: `[2026-04-08] fix(engine): Make sector overlap cleanup edge-truth-safe and block late truth-violating transfers`.
**Triggered operations need completed-history gates, not idle-corps gates (2026-04-08):** `Operation Herzegovina Consolidation` used to trigger on `corpsOpFinished(vrs_herzegovina)` alone. In noop or reduced harness states, that condition was accidentally true before `Operation Visegrad` or `Operation Foca` had ever happened, so the op validated against missing brigades and emitted false noise. Pattern: if a triggered op is supposed to be a follow-on to named earlier operations, encode those prerequisites explicitly through operation-history checks (`corpsCompletedOp(...)`) instead of inferring them from present-time idleness.
**Compatibility warnings need a â€œshould this authority exist?â€ gate (2026-04-08):** `evaluateDisplacementTriggers()` was warning whenever `hasLiveSectorFrontlineTruth()` was false, even in empty-roster noop harnesses where no sector truth could or should exist. Pattern: fallback warnings should require both the structural context (for example an operational SIDâ†’OSID mapping) and the live-force context (an active brigade roster) before declaring a missing-authority fault. Otherwise harnesses drown in expected compatibility noise and teams stop trusting the warnings.
**Completed-operation proofs must read archival authority, not runtime slots (2026-04-08):** A final save is allowed to have an empty `active_operations` list after an op completes. Proof tests that still expect completed progress to survive there are reading the wrong owner. Pattern: when verifying a finished operation, use `operation_aars.json` / completed AAR history for progress and completion evidence; reserve `active_operations` for live runtime only.
**Recovery gates must run each test under its native harness (2026-04-08):** A Vitest-authored test can appear â€œbrokenâ€ when it is actually being launched through `tsx --test`. Pattern: if a repo-level recovery/check script mixes Node tests and Vitest tests, keep each file in the runner that owns its assertion/runtime contract instead of flattening them into one generic command.
**Operation truth needs a final authority pass after late brigade writers (2026-04-08):** Active operations can drift after creation when elite loans attach or recall, brigades are replaced, or late lifecycle changes occur after the op was first anchored. Pattern: before serialization, rebuild operation truth from active formations plus final sector truth: filter participants to active brigades only, filter every axis roster to that surviving set, re-derive the sector anchor from live brigade locations, and demote empty execution ops into explicit recovery instead of saving them as fake live execution. Linked: `[2026-04-08] fix(engine): Final operation truth reconciliation and axis-integrity hardening`.
**Axis rosters are equal citizens of operation truth, not derived decoration (2026-04-08):** A fix that updates `participating_brigades` but leaves `axis.assigned_brigades` stale only moves the drift one layer down. Pattern: every path that adds, removes, recalls, or replaces an operation participant must update both the flat participant list and the axis-level roster, or diagnostics and execution will disagree about who is really fighting. Linked: `[2026-04-08] fix(engine): Final operation truth reconciliation and axis-integrity hardening`.
**Operation anchors should prefer physical brigade truth over stale sector overlap (2026-04-08):** When deriving `sector_id` for a live or queued operation, a sector that physically owns the participating brigades through `territory_osids` or frontline `friendly_osids` is more truthful than a sector that merely still lists those brigades in an old assigned/reserve roster. Pattern: anchor selection should score physical ownership first, then fall back to roster overlap only when live location truth is unavailable. Linked: `[2026-04-08] fix(engine): Final operation truth reconciliation and axis-integrity hardening`.
**Launch-time feasibility checks must use the same truth surface as brigade-side order issuance (2026-04-08):** A probe that looks plausible from coarse geometry or objective lists can still be dead on arrival if the actual brigade cannot produce a legal, above-threshold adjacent attack this turn. Pattern: operation birth logic must validate candidate objectives through the same concrete attackability and political-block filters that the brigade AI will face one phase later. If launch and execution read different truth surfaces, the engine manufactures fake work. Linked: `[2026-04-08] fix(engine): Close dead-on-arrival probe birth and align combat-causality diagnostics with final attack truth`.
**Recovery invalidation must key off recovery reason, not just the absence of logged attempts (2026-04-08):** An operation that exits because every objective became politically blocked is not the same failure as an operation that promised action and then never attacked. Pattern: downstream diagnostics should classify recoveries from the explicit lifecycle reason (`no_logged_attempt`, `political_blocked`, `brigade_attrition`, etc.), not infer failure solely from missing attack logs. Otherwise honest clean exits pollute failure metrics. Linked: `[2026-04-08] fix(engine): Close dead-on-arrival probe birth and align combat-causality diagnostics with final attack truth`.
**Eligible-attacker diagnostics must read post-trim, post-block order truth (2026-04-08):** Counting "eligible attackers" before final corps-level trimming, share limits, or political blocking creates a stale metric that can disagree with the orders that actually survive into combat. Pattern: any execution-quality metric that claims to describe attacking forces must be derived from the final emitted order set, not a pre-filter approximation. Linked: `[2026-04-08] fix(engine): Close dead-on-arrival probe birth and align combat-causality diagnostics with final attack truth`.
**Packaged runtime proof should extend one canonical probe manifest instead of spawning sibling probe commands (2026-04-08):** Desktop runtime coverage stayed coherent when every new packaged route and contract was added to `desktop:package:probe` as another manifest assertion rather than as `desktop:sandbox:probe`, `desktop:operational:probe`, or other parallel scripts. Pattern: when expanding packaged-runtime proof, strengthen the single canonical probe path and its manifest first; only add a separate probe command if the launch environment is fundamentally different. Linked: `[2026-04-08] fix(desktop): Prove packaged operational tactical-map renderer reaction through the real session/store path`.
**Packaged-mode truth needs a real packaged probe in CI; unit tests and builds are not enough (2026-04-08):** Vitest plus `npm run build` can stay green while asar path resolution, packaged window routing, or IPC/session lifecycle is broken. Pattern: any desktop packaging change that matters to shipped behavior must be verified through a real packaged executable launched by a packaged-runtime probe, and that same probe should be the CI gate. Linked: `[2026-04-08] fix(desktop): Prove packaged operational tactical-map renderer reaction through the real session/store path`.
**Renderer freshness proof should validate the exact pushed payload, not merely eventual plausible state (2026-04-08):** A packaged renderer reacting to bridge traffic is necessary but still too weak if the proof only checks that state "looks right" after settling. Pattern: when verifying renderer-side bridge consumption, compare against the exact pushed payload identity already owned by the real store path (for example store fingerprint and probe marker), rather than inventing a synthetic UI assertion. Linked: `[2026-04-08] fix(desktop): Require packaged tactical-map renderer freshness and exact pushed-payload identity`.
**Historical-op triggers need both chronology and live relevance (2026-04-08):** A triggered operation should not exist just because the calendar says so. `Operation Kotor Varos` still hit the validation gate even after the map had already made every objective RS-controlled, producing startup warning noise instead of useful planning truth. The fix was not to suppress validation output globally; it was to make the trigger contract honest by requiring at least one remaining enemy-held objective before the operation is considered live. Pattern: historical or scripted operation offers need two gates - "is it the right time?" and "is there still anything left to do?" A pure time gate is a stale-content trap.

**`no_logged_attempt` is execution failure, not a catch-all for bad operation birth (2026-04-08):** Dead-on-arrival probes were being born in planning, failing to become execution-ready, then entering recovery as `no_logged_attempt`. That polluted combat-causality because the engine was classifying a planning/orchestration failure as a combat/execution failure. The correct contract is: if a probe misses its immediate launch window without ever becoming usefully executable, it should enter `planning_invalidated` recovery. Reserve `no_logged_attempt` for operations that actually reached execution truth and still failed to attack. Pattern: recovery reasons are part of causality provenance, not just cleanup labels. Choose them by lifecycle ownership.

**Generic corps trimming must never silently override named operation ownership (2026-04-08):** `bot_brigade_ai_osid.ts` had a corps-level attack-share cap and RBiH warlord-friction trimming that could remove attack orders from brigades already participating in active operations. The result was a planner/executor civil war: an operation could be born honestly, then starved by a generic cap that did not know it was touching named-op participants. Fix: split attacks into pinned active-operation attackers and trimmable generic attackers, then trim only the latter. Pattern: when a global balancing pass acts on the same objects as a higher-priority ownership system, add a "pinned" exemption for objects already claimed by the higher-priority system.

**Combat diagnostics must read operation-local final truth, not corps-wide approximations (2026-04-08):** `eligible_attacker_count` was originally derived from `eligible_attackers_by_corps`, which is a coarse corps snapshot taken before final order trimming. That made operation diagnostics disagree with the operation's real surviving attack orders. Fix: keep the legacy field name for compatibility, but derive it from the operation's own surviving participant attack orders after final order truth is known, and only invalidate execution lulls when the operation has never logged prior attempts or captures. Pattern: when diagnosing a per-entity failure (operation, sector, brigade), use the entity's final local truth owner. Corps- or faction-level aggregates are good for dashboards, not for blame.

**Split-child overlap rescue needs an edge-floor guard, not a blanket frontline prohibition (2026-04-09):** The original territory-membership pre-pass in `ensureMinimumSectorCoverage(...)` correctly refused to steal frontline-essential brigades from donor sectors, but that rule was too strict for sibling sectors created by contiguity splitting when both sectors truthfully share the same front OSID. In that shape, a brigade can be "frontline-essential" to the donor and still be the honest brigade for the zero-covered sibling. The right repair is narrow: prefer ordinary territory rescues first, then allow a shared-front overlap rescue only when the donor remains at or above its own hostile-edge floor after the transfer (`assigned_brigade_ids.length > max(1, length_edges)` before donation). Pattern: when a frontline guard blocks a split-child repair, do not weaken the guard globally; add an overlap-specific exception tied to the donor's retained frontage floor and deterministic candidate ordering.
**Post-run validators must consume serialized unresolved truth, not reconstruct retired assignment doctrine (2026-04-09):** `tools/validate_run_consistency.cjs` was still enforcing "every brigade in a corps with sectors must be assigned" even though the live engine had already narrowed the owner to `military.unresolved_sector_brigades`. That produced false failures on honest final saves. Pattern: when a validator is checking the result of a canonical builder or reconciliation pass, read the builder's serialized unresolved/error set directly instead of re-deriving a broader doctrine from nearby state. If the sim already decided what counts as unresolved, downstream harnesses should validate that owner, not compete with it.
**Operation readiness must use the same objective-approach truth as execution (2026-04-09):** `cmd_arbih_1st_corps_t18` proved that coarse sector-subsegment membership is too broad to decide whether a named attack is launch-ready. Planning was treating any friendly OSID in the target subsegment as a valid approach, while brigade-side execution only permits graph-adjacent attacks through `war_front_edges_osid`. That mismatch manufactured fake execution turns and polluted combat-causality with a zero-eligible operation. Pattern: if launch readiness and execution both depend on concrete objective approaches, both stages must read the same graph-valid approach set. Use coarse subsegment membership only as a narrow compatibility fallback when adjacency truth is absent, never as a co-equal runtime authority.
**Shell summaries and shell actions must read the same queue owner (2026-04-09):** the military review queue was already canonically summarized as `presidentialReviewQueue`, but `App.tsx` still answered `pendingEventDecisions` directly and `WarroomStatusBar.tsx` still summarized the same work from a different field. That left two live owners for one review flow. Pattern: once a queue has a canonical derived owner for player-visible summary and routing, every shell surface should either summarize that queue or hand off to its action surface. Do not let a root shell component keep a direct action path on a lower-level source field after the summary owner exists.
**Operation rosters must yield to live foreign-sector ownership (2026-04-09):** `Operation Podrinje Sweep` kept `rs_5th_podrinje` in the Drina participant roster even after final sector truth had attached the brigade to `sector:vrs_1st_krajina:1`. Active/axis rosters are not independent authority; they are downstream views of live brigade ownership. Pattern: when a brigade has live sector claims and none belong to the operation corps, both `participating_brigades` and `axis.assigned_brigades` must drop it immediately. Preserve roster identity only when same-corps live sector truth is absent, not when foreign-sector truth is already known.
**Late repair must be allowed to reclaim stale movement packets for ownerless drift (2026-04-09):** The Podrinje pair proved that removing false sector/operation owners is not enough if `brigade_movement_orders` still belongs to an earlier generic movement phase. Pattern: when a late repair pass has the stronger truth surface for a stranded formation, existing orders are not sacred. Allow the repair owner to overwrite stale movement only for the narrow shape that proves generic ownership is already wrong: `assignment = null`, outside same-corps sector space, not in an active operation, and not already marching home. This keeps the override deterministic and prevents broad movement-authority fights.
**Repairs must not preserve orders that the graph itself says are impossible (2026-04-09):** `n1403` showed that an ownerless brigade can keep a home-recall packet even after sector and operation ownership are gone, but a reachability probe proved the brigade had no friendly path home at all. Pattern: once a repair pass becomes the final owner of a movement packet, it must prove the route is reachable before preserving or rewriting that packet. If no friendly path exists, clear the order and let the unresolved/stranded owner stay visible rather than serializing a fake instruction.
**Late sector mutators can recreate ghost frontline shells after an earlier prune (2026-04-09):** `buildFactionSectors(...)` already pruned zero-owner ghost sectors, but `canonicalizeSiblingFrontOwnership(...)`, `sealMergedSectorTruth(...)`, and `relocateMisassignedBrigadesToTruthfulOwners(...)` still ran afterward inside `buildCorpsFrontSectors(...)`. That let `sector:arbih_1st_corps:8` survive with front edges but no territory or brigades. Pattern: if authoritative builders run late ownership/canonicalization passes after an earlier cleanup, rerun a narrow final prune immediately before sync/serialization or ghost artifacts will leak back into downstream truth.
**Operation execution gates must use the same concrete attackability truth as brigade execution (2026-04-09):** `cmd_vrs_east_bosnian_t29` proved that coarse positional readiness is still too weak to authorize execution. The participants had reached the coarse approach shape accepted by `areParticipantsReadyForExecution(...)`, but the real adjacent-target predictor still rated every opening attack into `op:brcko:brka_2` as below threshold, so the op entered execution and then completed with `0` attacks. Pattern: when a lifecycle gate decides whether an operation is executable now, it must reuse the same graph-valid target prediction and attack threshold that brigade-side order issuance will face one phase later. If planning and execution read different truth surfaces, the engine manufactures fake live assaults.
**Sparse compatibility states need an explicit "adjacency authority absent" fallback, not fake predictor certainty (2026-04-09):** tightening the execution gate around `war_front_edges_osid` broke lightweight tests that intentionally omit front-edge adjacency. Pattern: when a stricter live-runtime truth surface depends on graph data that some compatibility tests do not serialize, add a narrow, documented fallback that recognizes the authority is absent and defers to the older coarse rule for those sparse states only. Do not silently treat missing adjacency as a negative predictor result.
**Battle-to-operation ownership must be captured at combat time, not re-derived from surviving participants (2026-04-09):** `cmd_vrs_east_bosnian_t24` in `n1407` logged a real battle, but causality still emitted `attack_orders_without_battles` because the brigade that fought had been trimmed out of `participating_brigades` by the time diagnostics ran. Pattern: when a downstream metric needs to know which operation owned a battle, stamp `operation_id` / `operation_name` onto the battle record at resolution time and let reports/diagnostics consume that owner directly. Survivor-list inference is a compatibility fallback, not canonical truth.
**Anomaly dead-front detectors must consume canonical cold-front truth before declaring failure (2026-04-09):** `zero_combat_corps` was still classifying `hvo_tomislavgrad` as a broken dead front in `n1408` even though the sim already owned that RS-HRHB frontage as a Graz cold front through `isSectorColdFront(...)`. The detector had enough evidence to know the silence was protected by truce, but it was only reading `front sectors + brigade battle history`. Rule: when the engine already owns an explicit no-combat contract (cold front, truce, freeze, political block), anomaly/reporting layers must consult that owner before inferring that zero activity means a broken front. Linked lane: `[2026-04-09] fix(harness): align zero-combat corps with Graz cold-front truth (n1409)`.
**Brigade stacking warnings must distinguish same-sector line concentration from ownerless co-location (2026-04-09):** A frontline sector can truthfully concentrate multiple brigades on one OSID when the sector itself canonically covers that tile. Treating every 2+ brigade co-location as suspicious turned `n1410` into `26` stacking warnings even though most of those stacks were simply same-sector front concentrations at covered OSIDs like `op:bihac:bihac_2` and `op:capljina:tasovcici_2`. The hardened rule is narrow: only suppress the warning when every brigade shares the same `assignment.sector_id`, every brigade is `role === 'front'`, and that sector canonically covers the OSID through `territory_osids` / `sub_segments[*].friendly_osids`. If ownership is mixed or missing, keep the warning. Linked lane: `[2026-04-09] fix(harness): align brigade stacking with sector truth (n1412)`.
**External whole-repo reviews must be normalized against current merged-main truth before they drive the roadmap (2026-04-10):** A strong review can correctly identify structural risks while still containing stale queue items if the repo moved after the review snapshot. Pattern: before promoting any external review into the roadmap, sort its claims into three buckets: still-live, historically true but already closed, and still-unproven until current runs re-benchmark them. Use the review to strengthen the scorecard, not to overwrite current repo truth with an older board snapshot.
**God-file cleanup only becomes roadmap-worthy when it is framed as a bounded non-drift program (2026-04-10):** Oversized files are not automatically the next thing to refactor just because they are ugly. They become legitimate priority when they are shown to be merge magnets that slow safe hardening across multiple lanes. Pattern: once a file-structure concern crosses that threshold, promote it into an explicit decomposition program with named target files, extraction order, and proof rules: no hidden behavior changes, same-hash scenario proof where no runtime change is intended, and bug fixes split into separate lanes instead of buried inside refactors.
**Once the substrate is strong enough, maintainability can legitimately outrank the next product-truth audit (2026-04-13):** There is a point where the engine is no longer mainly blocked by missing truth lanes, but by the cost and risk of changing oversized entrypoint files. Pattern: when multiple recent hardening lanes all succeed yet continue to pile unrelated changes into the same god files, promote decomposition to the active next program even if an important audit lane still exists. This is not aesthetic cleanup; it is safety and velocity infrastructure. Keep the displaced audit lane explicitly second so the product question does not vanish.
**Once a hardening campaign spans many subsystems, freeze it into a promotion scorecard before choosing more lanes (2026-04-10):** Freeform "what still feels weak?" discussion becomes unreliable once engine, UI, harness, reserve, and packaging work have all improved in parallel. Pattern: write a durable system scorecard that lists the current grade, the next target grade, the exact bounded work required for that promotion, and the proof needed to justify it. Also separate hardening, realism, content/runtime audit, and redesign-gated seams so the team can keep rotating to real bounded work instead of stopping just because one local board has dried up.
**Startup snapshot proof must never mutate the repo-owned artifact, and baked snapshot comparisons must normalize checkout formatting first (2026-04-10):** The packaged startup contract was already sound, but the proof harness was still brittle: destructive tests rewrote the committed snapshot in place, and raw byte comparisons treated Windows CRLF checkout as semantic drift. Pattern: when a baked artifact is both a product dependency and a proof target, route failure-path tests through a canonical override boundary to a throwaway copy and normalize non-semantic formatting at the contract read layer before comparing against builder truth.
**Threat and force-balance belong to player-safe staff abstractions, not raw decimal readouts (2026-04-10):** The renderer may still carry exact `threat_ratio` for engine-adjacent logic, but normal player-facing shells should not print values like `1.37:1` or `2.04×` just because the number is available. Pattern: keep exact threat truth in sim/adapter ownership, then route player-facing tactical and Army HQ surfaces through one shared qualitative formatter so pressure appears as a designed staff assessment rather than a leaked engine scalar.
**Player-mode front-edge payloads must not carry enemy shell-routing ids, and sector shells must revalidate selection at the player-visibility boundary (2026-04-10):** In AWWV, `sector_id` is not harmless display data; it is a routing key that opens CorpsFrontPanel and highlights a whole sector family on the map. If front-edge hover/click GeoJSON ships both factions' `sector_id` values in normal player mode, the tactical shell can select enemy sectors even when every higher-level list view is properly filtered. The durable pattern is two-layered: strip enemy `sector_id` / `corps_id` from front-edge payloads before they reach the renderer interaction path, and make the receiving shell revalidate any selected id against `filterPlayerFacingSectors(...)` / `findPlayerFacingSectorById(...)` before rendering. Applied: player-mode front-edge features now omit enemy sector metadata, and both `MapContainer.tsx` and `CorpsFrontPanel.tsx` gate sector selection through the shared player-visibility helper. Linked lane: `[2026-04-10] fix(ui): harden front sector player visibility`.
**Shell/bootstrap surfaces must stay null-safe at the player-faction boundary instead of inventing a default side (2026-04-10):** In AWWV, `state.meta.player_faction` is canonical shell identity. If a UI/bootstrap surface falls back to `'RBiH'`, `'RS'`, or `factions[0]?.id`, it silently creates a second owner and turns missing identity into false campaign truth. The durable pattern is: resolve player faction once through a shared helper, let downstream surfaces render neutral/unavailable states when identity is absent, and never mint a faction locally just to keep a panel alive. Applied across App/Warroom/BottomStatusStrip/Dayton/Wrapped/keyboard shortcuts/ticker shells in the lane `[2026-04-10] fix(ui): align shell player-faction ownership with canonical resolver`.
**Exact operation readiness numerics should survive in debug-only surfaces, while normal play consumes one shared staff-balance abstraction (2026-04-10):** If the engine/read-model already owns exact force-ratio truth, normal player-facing shells should not each print their own `toFixed(...)` copy or commander-threshold math. That creates multiple presentation owners and leaks staff-internal precision. The durable pattern is: keep exact numerics canonical in the engine/read-model, route ordinary presidential/Army HQ surfaces through one downstream abstraction helper, and reserve exact values for explicit debug-only surfaces like raw intel. Applied in AWWV via `getPlayerSafeOperationBalancePresentation(...)`, which now owns the player-facing force-balance vocabulary across operations, corps-front, briefing, OPORD, narrative, and recommendation-explanation surfaces. Linked lane: `[2026-04-10] fix(ui): demote player-facing operation force-ratio precision to staff abstractions`.
**Action boundaries should consume the packet's canonical id, not a containing owner field (2026-04-10):** If a request packet already has a stable `request_id`, approval/decline/history must all key off that packet id. A containing field like `corps_id` can describe who asked, but it is not the action identity. In AWWV, reserve decline/history were already request-keyed while approval was still corps-keyed, which made the desktop boundary collapse multiple same-corps requests into one implicit action. The durable pattern is: thread the packet id through UI, preload, main-process, and sim mutation paths, and treat owner/context fields (`corps_id`, `faction`) as payload only. Linked lane: `[2026-04-10] fix(desktop): align reserve approval with canonical request identity`.
**Front-edge display repair must live at the renderer ownership boundary unless scenario proof says the sim packet can safely absorb it (2026-04-11):** The East Bosnia/Donje Žešće seam proved that raw `corps_front_sectors` can still omit some authoritative `war_front_edges_osid` faction-sides without the simulation itself being wrong. A sim-side backfill inside `corps_front_sectors.ts` looked attractive but changed operation behavior and broke scenario proof. The durable pattern is: if the player-visible failure is missing hover/click/glow ownership on front edges, add one deterministic display/read-model owner that completes sector/corps ownership from raw sector claims plus same-faction adjacency, and only promote that logic into sim truth after scenario proof stays clean. Linked lane: `[2026-04-11] fix(map): complete display front-edge ownership for hover, click, and sector glow`.
**Sector packets must be re-split after every late merge before they reach the save/UI boundary (2026-04-11):** Early sector builders can correctly split front lines and still lose the invariant if later merge/canonicalization passes union edges back together. In AWWV, `mergeLateSiblingFrontFragments(...)` could produce final sectors over `MAX_SECTOR_EDGES`, which the map then rendered as disconnected glow chunks. The durable rule is: after the last merge that can change edge ownership, run one final geometry invariant pass that normalizes subsegments, splits non-contiguous line pieces, re-applies the edge cap, then repartitions territory through the canonical Voronoi owner path. Do not make the renderer hide a bad sector packet. Linked lane: `[2026-04-11] fix(sim): enforce final sector-line geometry and tolerant shared-border fronts`.

**Shared-border detection must tolerate coordinate drift, not rely on exact coordinate-string equality (2026-04-11):** Operational polygons can share a real boundary while their vertices differ by tiny floating-point drift. Donje Zesce and Mazlina exposed this: exact segment-key matching wrote `shared_segments: 0`, causing `computeFrontEdgesOsid(...)` to skip a real RBiH-RS front edge. The durable rule is to compare segment endpoints within a small epsilon in both directions when deriving `shared_segments`, and to keep the enrichment script and both operational derivation scripts on the same rule. Exact string equality is a data-tool convenience, not geometry truth. Linked lane: `[2026-04-11] fix(sim): enforce final sector-line geometry and tolerant shared-border fronts`.
**If a sector-line layer is visible, it must belong to the interaction family too (2026-04-11):** The map can still feel broken even when sector geometry is correct if hover/click only listen to a subset of the rendered line layers. In AWWV the visible `sector-edge-glow-*` layers were not first-class interaction layers, so the player could be directly over the white sector line and still miss selection unless the older hover hitbox happened to overlap. The durable rule is: keep one explicit list of interactive front/sector line layers and use it consistently for line hover suppression, direct layer listeners, and map-wide click fallback. Do not let the visible stroke and the clickable stroke diverge.
**Shared-front brigade anchors must reinforce the neediest sibling sector before pressure-based tie-breaks (2026-04-11):** The Foca/Kalinovik rim regression showed that a brigade standing on a front OSID shared by multiple same-corps sibling sectors cannot be assigned by threat bias alone. That rule repeatedly fed already-covered siblings and starved the small child sector that actually needed the anchor brigade to survive final serialization. The durable rule is: when same-corps sectors share a front OSID, prefer the sibling with zero assigned brigades first, then the sibling with the largest unmet edge need, and only then use pressure/id tie-breaks. Shared-front anchors are coverage truth before they are pressure optimization.
**Final sector reconciliation must recompute end-of-turn spatial truth instead of reusing cached mid-turn context (2026-04-11):** A final sector packet can look internally coherent and still be wrong if reconciliation answers to an earlier cached spatial context after control/front ownership has shifted later in the turn. The durable rule is: any final-save ownership pass that claims to serialize end-of-turn front truth must rebuild spatial context from current controllers plus current `war_front_edges_osid`, then cache that refreshed result for downstream consumers. Cached mid-turn spatial is a performance input, not final truth authority.
**Story-breaker investigations must not be collapsed into one false root cause when they cross subsystem boundaries (2026-04-11):** The Podrinje / Sarajevo / Posavina lane looked like one grand strategic failure from the player's point of view, but the real owners were different: Podrinje required recruitment identity preservation and per-axis readiness truth, Sarajevo required the political state object to follow the besieged split-city pocket instead of whole-city majority supply, and Posavina required both the same multi-axis readiness fix and a live scenario must-hold contract. Durable rule: when several strategic outcomes look wrong at once, prove each owner chain separately in fresh runs before designing a shared fix. A single "story" symptom can hide engine, scenario-contract, and state-boundary bugs at the same time. Linked lane: `[2026-04-11] fix(sim): harden Podrinje identity, Sarajevo siege pocket truth, and Posavina corridor continuity`.

**Browser-bound map/state code must not depend on `node:crypto` when all it needs is deterministic identity (2026-04-11):** `src/state/enclave_integrity.ts` only needed stable enclave ids, but it used `createHash('sha256')`, which broke `desktop:map:build` because the map bundle reaches state code in the browser. Durable rule: if a state/helper module is shared by sim and map/UI, use a browser-safe deterministic hash for internal ids unless cryptographic strength is actually required. Save/report determinism is the invariant; `node:crypto` is not. Linked lane: `[2026-04-11] fix(sim): harden Podrinje identity, Sarajevo siege pocket truth, and Posavina corridor continuity`.
**Consequence substrate owner map (2026-04-14, corrected 2026-04-15):** Ten canonical owners for negotiation/consequence substrate: (1) `compute_capital.ts` → `computeNegotiationBreakdown()` — raw war metrics per faction, (2) `compute_combat_effective.ts` → `computeCombatEffectiveBrigades()` — per-faction active brigade count, (3) `strategic_dimensions.ts` → `computeDimensionBaseValues()` — 6 scored dimensions per faction, with `internal_cohesion` reading canonical `state.political.war_exhaustion`, (4) `src/state/patron_pressure.ts` — IVP and `patron_state` evolution, (5) `src/sim/negotiation/patron_pressure.ts` → `updatePatronPressure()` — support_level, override_authority, sanctions, (6) `patron_events.ts` → `evaluatePatronEvents()` — scripted modifiers at historical trigger weeks, (7) `src/sim/political/political_peace_plan.ts` → `computePoliticalPeacePlanResponse()` — bot peace-plan scoring truth, (8) `src/sim/negotiation/peace_plans.ts` → `evaluatePeacePlans()` — trigger / pending packet / rejection-consequence wrapper, (9) `dayton_negotiation.ts` — territorial/institutional packages and bot response, (10) `scoring.ts` → `computeFullVerdict()` — pyrrhic score, grades, dimension grades. Dead wires closed 2026-04-14 and corrected 2026-04-15: `civilian_casualties_caused` wired from displacement events, `political_cohesion` → `internal_cohesion` migration completed in grade anchors, dead `war_phase_exhaustion` removed from internal_cohesion, and stale `state.military.war_exhaustion` shadow replaced with canonical `state.political.war_exhaustion`.

**Shared-front paper-empty sectors are not automatically uncovered fronts (2026-04-12):** A sector can serialize with zero own assigned/reserve brigades at a same-corps shared-front knot while the exact friendly front OSID is physically occupied by a sibling sector's active front brigade. In AWWV, `sector:vrs_1st_krajina:4` at `op:maglaj:jablanica` looked empty by `sector.density`, but `rs_1st_ozren_light_infantry` physically covered that OSID as a front brigade in sibling `sector:vrs_1st_krajina:0`. The durable reporting rule is: before `frontline_density_imbalance` accuses a low-density paper-empty sector, check whether every front OSID is physically covered by active same-corps sibling-sector frontage. If yes, suppress the false gap. If the front OSID is unique and uncovered, still report it. Cold-front unstaffed sectors remain a separate truce-owned truth.
**Residual boards must be retired once current repo proof closes them (2026-04-16):** A roadmap sentence can stay scary long after the underlying seams are gone. In AWWV, southeast-Herzegovina unresolved-frontline, `op:brcko:brka_2`, and Foča/Kalinovik ownership all still appeared as "remaining live reds" even though current proof already showed `unresolved_sector_brigades = 0`, anomaly alignment held, anchor suites passed, and dedicated real-save ownership/display tests were green. Pattern: when a residual board is older than the latest direct proof, update the board to name the surviving follow-up honestly instead of preserving a dramatic-but-false red list. Linked: `[2026-04-16] docs(roadmap): retire stale scenario-board residuals after direct proof`.
**Cross-platform CI must either hoist nested UI deps or install them where the UI package owns them (2026-04-16):** A repo can look healthy on a Windows workstation and still fail immediately on GitHub if root `tsc` and release scripts traverse `src/ui/map` while CI installs only the root package. In AWWV, `Baseline Regression` was typechecking files that import `maplibre-gl`, `pmtiles`, `@deck.gl/*`, and `@vitejs/plugin-react`, but those dependencies lived only in `src/ui/map/package.json`. Pattern: when the root workflow runs against code owned by a nested UI package, either hoist those dependencies into the root owner or explicitly install the nested package in every CI job that touches that code. Do not let local nested `node_modules` masquerade as repo-wide truth.
**Package scripts that gate CI should use cross-platform npm bin resolution, not Windows path literals (2026-04-16):** Hardcoding `node .\node_modules\vite\bin\vite.js` or similar works just long enough to fool a Windows-first repo, then explodes on Ubuntu runners before the real build contract is exercised. Pattern: canonical scripts that matter to CI should use npm-resolved bins (`vite`, `vitest`, `electron`, `electron-builder`) or forward-slash paths so the same script text survives on Windows and Linux. Once the path bug is removed, rerun the deeper gate immediately, because it can reveal the next honest failure hiding behind the portability mistake.

**Once discovery reaches zero `node:test` entrypoints, package scripts must stop pretending the old runner is still canonical (2026-04-16):** A compatibility runner can outlive the thing it was preserving and quietly become a lie. In AWWV, `tools/test/run_node_tests.mjs` survived after discovery had already reached zero `node:test` files, so `npm test`, `test:engine`, and `qa:all` were still narrating a split-runner world that no longer existed. Pattern: when discovery proves one runner family is empty, either delete the compatibility runner or demote it completely out of canonical scripts the same day. Otherwise the repo keeps teaching stale process truth.
**A transition band should be retired as soon as its remaining items are all either closed or promoted, not preserved for mood (2026-04-16):** Roadmap labels have inertia. In AWWV, `v0.8-to-v0.9` was still being spoken of as the current state even after save/load truth, shell ownership, event execution ownership, test-suite unification, CI guardrails, and architecture simplification had all landed. Pattern: once a transition band's remaining work is either complete or clearly reclassified into milestone-owned future lanes, rename the current state to the next active development band. Otherwise the roadmap keeps manufacturing fake limbo long after the code moved on.

**Lifting a thesis out of a code-comment header and into authoritative canon is a real deliverable (2026-04-16):** `src/sim/negotiation/scoring.ts` carried the negative-sum thesis as a file header — *"The least bad version of a tragedy"* — and `src/sim/negotiation/rupture_consequences.ts` described itself as *"Ring 2 of the sensitive-history boundary"* in its first comment. The substrate was there; the authority was hiding in source code where the game designer, historian, and product manager can't reliably read it. Pattern: when a file comment is already stating a design thesis that governs downstream surfaces, lift the thesis into a canon document with the same words, cross-referenced both directions. A thesis that lives only in code is durable for engineers but invisible to every other role; a thesis that lives in canon is citable. Applied in `[2026-04-16] docs(canon): close v0.9.0 victory-conditions and sensitive-history gates`.

**Sensitive-history features belong in exactly one of three rings or they do not get built (2026-04-16):** The `v0.9.0` Sensitive History Design Gate settled AWWV's position on depicting genocide, ethnic cleansing, siege-starvation, and mass atrocity in a way that takes the subject seriously without turning it into an optimization problem. Ring 1 = modeled mechanically with locked consequences; Ring 2 = represented narratively with ICTY citations; Ring 3 = refused entirely (no "commit genocide" decision tree, no concentration-camp system, no negotiable condemnation, no body-count optimization, nine other explicit prohibitions). Pattern: any proposal that touches sensitive history must identify which ring it belongs in before implementation work begins. A feature that fits no ring is not a gap in the design — it is a signal that the feature should not exist. The rupture expansion rule (four criteria — mass scale, ICTY/ICJ finding, specific trigger, non-reversible) keeps Srebrenica as the only rupture by design rather than by omission. Applied in `[2026-04-16] docs(canon): close v0.9.0 victory-conditions and sensitive-history gates`.
**Roadmap features need an owner plan, an integration dependency, and a proof surface before implementation starts (2026-04-30):** The v0.9 roadmap already had many milestone plans, but the system-level glue was implicit: presidential loop, formation-life believability, v1.0 release integration, post-1.0 child-plan triggers, and open design-question governance. The durable rule is: when auditing roadmap readiness, do not stop at "is there a plan file with a similar name?" Check whether the feature has (1) a current owner plan, (2) dependencies against adjacent systems, and (3) a proof surface. If any of those are missing, create a plan or child-plan trigger before work starts. Applied in `[2026-04-30] docs(roadmap): audit plan coverage and add missing architecture execution plans`.

**Major scenario evidence should trigger a roadmap sync, not just a run report (2026-04-30):** AWWV now uses scenario output as product-planning evidence, not only calibration evidence. The durable rule is: after a major 40w/188w/200w run, remote branch integration, or milestone close, update the Master Roadmap's current-status layer under the Studio Health lane so stale baselines do not survive beside fresher repo truth. Applied in `[2026-04-30] docs(roadmap): promote v0.9 closure loop, formation-life believability, and roadmap-sync cadence`.

**Opportunity resolutions should link to AAR ids, not infer outcomes from names (2026-05-01):** The operation opportunity layer is an authorization shell; once approved, the normal `CorpsOperation` lifecycle owns execution and `sector_offensive.ts` owns AAR finalization. The durable rule is: opportunity-resolution truth must be closed by linking the finalized `OperationAAR.operation_id` back onto `OperationOpportunityResolution.executed_op_aar_id`, with `exit_class` derived from the AAR outcome. Do not make Cost Ledger, Codex, or UI consumers infer opportunity results from operation names or active-operation state. The deterministic match key is `executed_op_name + response_turn == AAR.started_turn`, with stable sort fallback by proposal id. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(operations): link opportunity resolutions to completed operation AARs`.

**Opportunity family UI should consume the generic opportunity ledger, not create family-specific panels (2026-05-01):** Once opportunity proposals, resolutions, and AAR ids exist, Army HQ records should read the generic `operationOpportunityRecords` view from `GameStateAdapter`, which is derived from `operation_opportunities`, `operation_opportunity_resolutions`, pending proposal reviews, and linked `operation_history` rows. Durable rule: new opportunity families (5th Corps, Vlasic, Orasje, etc.) should not add bespoke UI panels or hardcoded operation-name readers. They should populate catalog truth; the records surface displays proposal status, decision, AAR link, exit class, attacks/objectives/grade, and prerequisite-axis counts generically. Player-facing consumers must stay faction-scoped and read `executed_op_aar_id + exit_class`, not infer outcomes from names. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(ui): surface operation opportunity records in Army HQ`.

**Cost Ledger should summarize opportunity outcomes from linked resolution truth, not raw live queues (2026-05-01):** Opportunity decisions become endgame-accounting truth only after the resolution row exists; approved offensive entries should then join through `executed_op_aar_id` to `operation_history`. Durable rule: Cost Ledger / War Reckoning may summarize proposal response, faction, exit class, AAR outcome, attacks, objectives, and grade stars, but it should not re-run catalog predicates, scan active operations, or infer success from operation names. The live queue is context; the resolution + AAR link is the accounting owner. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(endgame): add opportunity decisions to the Cost Ledger`.

**Opportunity content review starts with the health diagnostic, not manual save-scraping (2026-05-01):** After any opportunity-family lane, run `node tools/diagnostics/opportunity_health_audit.cjs <run_dir>` on the relevant long-run output before debating calibration. Durable rule: first classify the proposal/decision/AAR chain itself - approved rows without AAR links, broken AAR ids, T3 sentinels, duplicate resolution rows, attacks, objectives, and exit classes - then decide whether remaining issues belong to content predicates, operation execution, combat math, or historical calibration. Applied in `[2026-05-01] tool(diagnostics): add operation opportunity health audit`.
**Opportunity proposal review needs two surfaces: generic autonomy fallback and Army HQ dossier (2026-05-01):** `pending_proposal_reviews` is a generic assisted-command queue, but operation opportunities carry a distinct decision meaning through `proposed_action: "OPPORTUNITY:<proposal_id>"`. The canonical UI pattern is: preserve `proposed_action`, `current_value`, and `proposed_value` in `LoadedGameState.pendingProposalReviews`; derive a separate `operationOpportunityProposals` DTO from live opportunity state + matching review rows; route inbox clicks to Army HQ briefing (`army_hq_opportunity`), not the generic `AutonomyPanel`; keep `AutonomyPanel` as a compact fallback for non-opportunity proposals. The Army HQ dossier owns live opportunity authorization calls. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(ui): surface pending operation opportunities as Army HQ dossiers`.

**Do not read opportunity catalog files from UI to make live dossiers richer (2026-05-01):** The UI should render persisted player-safe proposal state, not import `operation_opportunity_catalog_*` content. Catalog imports in the tactical map would couple product UI to sim authoring files and risk leaking raw objectives/rosters that were never persisted through the player-visible boundary. If the dossier needs corps labels, map footprint, force traits, or variant choices, persist those as safe DTO fields at the engine/adapter seam first, then render them. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(ui): surface pending operation opportunities as Army HQ dossiers`.
**Claude/Codex work should be authored as mega-lanes by default, with parallel-agent dispatch in the prompt (2026-05-01):** Once a system has enough evidence and substrate maturity, a prompt shaped as "fix one helper, add one test, report back" causes handoff churn and keeps the user in the loop as project manager. The durable rule is: future Claude prompts should normally be autonomous mega-lanes with 4-8 internal phases, phase commits, tests, docs, ledger/knowledge propagation, and scenario/UI proof where relevant. Prompts should explicitly require parallel agents up front: architecture/technical ownership, QA or scenario harness, game design/non-railroad review, historian for historical content, determinism auditor for state/output changes, and the relevant domain expert. A small patch is appropriate only when the user explicitly asks for one or a review finding is narrowly scoped. Applied in `[2026-05-01] docs(process): promote v0.9 work to autonomous mega-lanes`.

**A presidential turn needs an aftermath owner between execution and the next briefing (2026-05-01):** `advance-turn` can be deterministic and the inbox can be well-derived, yet the product still feels like machinery if the successful turn path only reloads state. The durable loop is Brief -> Inspect -> Decide -> Execute -> Report -> Cost -> Judge -> Next. `TurnSummary` and `lastTurnReport` already contain much of the report substrate, but Command Briefing is forward-looking and Records are passive. Add a dedicated Turn Aftermath read model/surface that summarizes what happened, what it cost, which records matter, and which reviews are next, then routes into Army HQ, War Summary, Chronicle, and Inbox. Applied in `[2026-05-01] docs(architecture): promote v0.9 work to autonomous mega-lanes and product spine closure`.

**Pre-commit hooks should be staged-aware in shared Claude/Codex worktrees (2026-05-01):** A full-repo typecheck hook is valuable for code/data commits, but harmful when a docs-only architecture commit is blocked by unrelated WIP from another active agent in the same checkout. The durable rule is: keep the guardrail for staged source/data/config changes, but let docs/process-only commits bypass `npx tsc --noEmit` after `git diff --check`. Applied in `[2026-05-01] docs(architecture): promote v0.9 work to autonomous mega-lanes and product spine closure`.

**Operation AAR attack totals must read lifecycle counters; weekly_log is not the attack source of truth (2026-05-01 fix):** LANE D exposed the failure mode: Operation Sana had `op.attack_attempt_count=7` across three axes and `recovery_reason='max_failures'`, but AAR `total_attacks=0` made the opportunity resolution say `did_not_launch`. The fix is now shipped in `operation_aar.ts`: multi-axis AAR totals sum `axis.attack_attempt_count`, legacy flat ops read `op.attack_attempt_count`, and old/partial shapes fall back to weekly-log totals. Axis summaries use the same lifecycle counter rule. `weekly_log` remains the casualty/equipment ledger; it is not authoritative for whether an operation launched. Durable rule: any consumer deriving `exit_class`, cost ledgers, anomaly warnings, or UI history from AAR attack counts should trust `OperationAAR.total_attacks` after finalization, but finalization itself must consume the operation lifecycle counters, not a parallel per-turn diagnostic field. Applied in `[2026-05-01] fix(operations): make AAR attack totals read canonical lifecycle counters`.
**Opportunity-family reviews need a fused campaign proof matrix before calibration debate (2026-05-02):** Once operation opportunities span catalog predicates, proposal queues, player/bot decisions, AAR links, per-axis delivery, and cost-ledger/accounting truth, no single source is enough to explain why an operation did or did not become history. Durable rule: after any opportunity-family or combat-delivery lane, run `node tools/diagnostics/opportunity_campaign_proof.cjs <run_dir>` before hand-tuning predicates or interpreting painted-map gaps. The proof matrix should classify each opportunity as surfaced/executed, authorized T3, approved-unlinked, resolved-no-execution, blocked-in-window, or not observed, then attach decision, exit class, AAR outcome, attacks/captures, axis delivery predicates, blockers, and reachability warnings. Applied in `[2026-05-02] tool(diagnostics): add opportunity campaign proof matrix`.

**Interior objectives should be follow-on opportunities gated by live corridors, not bundled into initial breakthrough ops (2026-05-01 Sana 95 reachability):** The 5th Corps Sana catalog initially copied the legacy scripted-op three-axis shape wholesale: Krupa, Bihac-Petrovac, and Sanski Most/Kljuc. n1605 proved the third axis was structurally unreachable at launch: `op:sanski_most:lusci_palanka_2` had no live approach corridor, so the launch-readiness loop skipped it and the axis never attacked. The engine now records `unreachable_at_launch`, but catalog authors still need to avoid creating impossible axes. The durable rule is: **when an operation's historical deep objective is only reachable after a breakthrough, author it as a separate follow-on opportunity gated on live control of corridor anchors, not as an initial axis and not as a hardcoded completion chain.** The follow-on should preserve counterfactual agency: any live route that opens the corridor can surface it, and normal combat can still fail. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(operations): split Sana 95 interior push into live-corridor follow-on`.

**Turn aftermath is a composition/read-model owner, not a second AAR or sim-report writer (2026-05-01):** The engine already owns turn truth through `state.turn_summaries[*]`, and desktop already owns the raw `turn-report-updated` push after `advance-turn`. The product gap was player handoff, not missing simulation output. The durable pattern is: build a pure UI read model (`buildTurnAftermathView`) over `LoadedGameState.latestTurnSummary` + unified inbox derivation + OSID display names; open it only after a successful load of the next save; reset it on arbitrary save load; and route all advance-turn entrypoints through one dependency bundle. Do not create another AAR writer, another desktop event payload, or another stateful "turn result" owner unless the sim truth itself is missing. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(ui): add Turn Aftermath product-spine bridge`.
**Army HQ record stacks need a summary pulse above the rows (2026-05-01):** A persistent record stream is still too slow if the player must scan every card to infer trajectory. The durable pattern is: expose a pure summary helper beside the record builder (`buildTurnAftermathLedgerSummary(records)`), then render cumulative net territory, casualties, displacement, formations lost, and hard-turn count above the detailed rows.
