Keep it read-only and derived from already-built view models; do not reach back into simulation internals from the component. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(ui): summarize Turn Aftermath records as a campaign pulse`.

**In-campaign cost should project from `TurnSummary`, not re-run endgame Cost Ledger logic (2026-05-01):** Endgame `CostLedger` remains the downstream war reckoning owner, but the player needs turn-level cost feedback during play. The durable pattern is: add a `TurnAftermathView.cost` packet derived only from persisted `TurnSummary` facts (casualties, displacement, formation destructions, supply deltas), with scan-friendly severity bands and reasons. Do not recompute verdicts, historical comparisons, rupture consequences, or negotiation capital in the turn-after-action surface; those belong to `buildCostLedger(...)` and `WarCostSummary`. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(ui): add turn-cost packets to Turn Aftermath`.

**Turn aftermath is a record stream, not just a modal notification (2026-05-01):** The product-spine report loop must survive player dismissal. The durable pattern is: build the immediate post-advance modal and the Army HQ historical surface from the same pure read model (`turnAftermath.ts`), with `latestTurnSummary` powering the modal and `turnSummaries` powering persistent records. Only the latest record should carry live inbox obligations; older records are archived turn packets unless the engine later persists per-turn obligation snapshots. Navigation should route to Army HQ RECORDS via a typed subtab (`recordsSubTab: 'aftermath'`), not by reopening generic AAR history or creating a second event log. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(ui): persist Turn Aftermath in Army HQ records`.
**Army HQ archives need review filters once record cards become rich enough to scroll (2026-05-01):** A record stack that includes cost, events, signals, actions, and territory eventually becomes a pile, even when every card is useful. The durable pattern is to keep the data owner pure (`filterTurnAftermathRecords(records, mode)`) and let the UI expose commander review modes (`all`, `hard`, `signals`, `actions`, `territory`) with counts. The campaign pulse should recompute over the visible filtered set so the player understands the selected review window, not a hidden all-record baseline. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(ui): filter Turn Aftermath records by review mode`.

**Predictor honesty fix can be a no-op for the original target if the gap was misdiagnosed (2026-05-02):** Phase 1 of the Drina enclave mega-lane converged on "predictor over-aggregating ARBiH 2nd Corps as facing-enemy-sector" as the binding gap for both Krivaja-95 and Stupčanica-95. Phase 4b shipped the enclave-aware defender-aggregation fix (`estimateForceRatio` filters defenders to those physically inside the enclave when ALL objectives are enclave-interior), and the synthetic test proved the bug 17×. But Phase 6 production validation showed Krivaja's `force_ratio_estimate` was BYTE-IDENTICAL to baseline (0.0838 → 0.0838) — the hypothesis was correct for Stupčanica's sub-segment (6× honest correction in production) but WRONG for Krivaja's (n1610 sector aggregation was already enclave-narrow; the filter had nothing to scope). Krivaja's actual binding gate was brigade roster (`rs_skelani_battalion` destroyed pre-t168, `rs_5th_podrinje` co-located at the wrong axis). Durable rule: when one mechanic explanation fits some symptoms but not all, dispatch `/sector-expert` (or analogous expert) to do empirical hand-trace of the ORIGINAL pool composition BEFORE committing the fix as universally applicable. A predictor-honesty fix is only as good as its claimed coverage; if the diagnostic only verifies the symptom (low force_ratio at AAR) and not the cause (which defenders the predictor counted), the fix can ship correct-but-mislabeled. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(combat): scope estimateForceRatio defender aggregation to enclave (PARTIAL)`.

**Sensitive-history partial-closes are canonical when § 8.3 doesn't trigger (2026-05-02):** The Drina enclave mega-lane shipped a structurally correct predictor-honesty fix (Stupčanica 6× honest correction; 17× synthetic test) but the headline acceptance criterion (Srebrenica + Žepa controllers flip RBiH → RS, rupture `srebrenica_genocide_1995` fires) was NOT MET — controllers BYTE-IDENTICAL to predecessor. The instinct is to escalate to user because "Srebrenica is sensitive history." But Design Gate § 8.3 ("when in doubt, no") governs DOUBT about Ring 3 surfaces, rupture trigger semantics, condemnation, or reward-for-atrocity — NOT mechanic-completion gaps in Ring 1 honesty corrections. `/game-designer` canon authority on Ring boundaries closes this: partial-close is canonical disposition for late-war P0s that are structurally hard but not morally fraught, IF (a) no Ring 3 surface created, (b) rupture trigger untouched, (c) no enclave mechanic mutation, (d) no atrocity-as-tactic, (e) clear handoffs documented. Durable rule: sensitive-history partial-closes do not require user approval when the lane's mechanic shipped Ring-1-correct, the failure mode is "mechanism still insufficient" (not "we crossed a moral line"), and successor lanes are chartered with proper sign-off chains. Reserve user escalation for actual Ring 3 / rupture / reward-for-atrocity DOUBT. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(combat): scope estimateForceRatio defender aggregation to enclave (PARTIAL)`.

**Stop gate enforcement at close-time prevents scope-creep into successor-lane territory (2026-05-02):** When Phase 6 of a mega-lane proves the predicted fix is necessary-but-insufficient, the temptation is to extend the lane to Phase 4d / Phase 4e and "finish the job." But stop gates are binding at close-time, not just at start-time. Stop gate 3 ("Drina-specific changes only, ICTY-cited") + stop gate 4 ("no combat-math edit unless Phase 1 PROVES ownership") jointly block Phase 4d in-lane: defender-stack compounding cascades to ALL combat (not Drina-only) AND Phase 6 evidence ≠ Phase 1 ownership proof (Phase 1 chartered the predictor lane, not the combat-math compounding lane). Durable rule: future lanes touching combat-math compounding need their own Phase 1 with `/sector-expert` + `/game-designer` + `/historian` + `/determinism-auditor` + `/qa-engineer` pre-engagement + own GREEN-case regression matrix. The stop gate exists exactly to prevent the close-time temptation to extend scope when the diagnostic surprises. Discovering during Phase 6 that "we need MORE work to hit the headline" does not retroactively grant Phase 1 ownership of that work; it generates a successor-lane handoff. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(combat): scope estimateForceRatio defender aggregation to enclave (PARTIAL)`.

**Turn Aftermath records should reuse `TurnSummary` as strategic memory, not create a second event log (2026-05-01):** The campaign loop needs more than per-turn totals; players need to remember why a turn mattered. The durable pattern is to project strategic signals from already persisted `TurnSummary` fields (`events_fired`, `notable_events`, decorations, arc transitions, supply transitions, movements) into `TurnAftermathView.signals`, then aggregate those views into a campaign pulse. Do not add another history writer, event table, or UI-only mutable archive. If a future record surface needs richer meaning, first extend the canonical `compile-turn-summary` payload, then consume it through the Turn Aftermath read model. Applied in `[2026-05-01] feat(ui): add Turn Aftermath strategic signals and momentum pulse`.
**Campaign memory should deep-link to evidence records, not duplicate them (2026-05-02 Chronicle turn-record deep link):** Chronicle is the campaign memory surface; Army HQ `TURN AFTERMATH` is the turn evidence packet. When the player wants to investigate a remembered event, route through `shellNavigation` into the existing Army HQ record with a focused turn, instead of adding expanded cost/combat/territory detail directly into Chronicle cards. The durable pattern is `Chronicle entry -> openArmyHQAftermathRecord(state, turn) -> Army HQ Records / TURN AFTERMATH -> focusedAftermathTurn`. If the target turn is outside the normal latest-record window, expand the read-model window for that focused route rather than silently failing the link. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(ui): link Chronicle entries to Turn Aftermath records`.
**Strategic priority boards should synthesize and route, not own source truth (2026-05-02 Presidential Decision Room):** The player needs one "what should I do next?" command surface, but that surface must not become a second inbox, opportunity ledger, cost ledger, Chronicle, or event log. The durable pattern is: build a pure UI read model over adapter-owned `presidentialReviewQueue`, `operationOpportunityProposals`, `commandBriefing`, `operationalSitrep`, Turn Aftermath records, active campaign cost, and Chronicle availability; sort deterministically; then route each card through canonical shell navigation to the source owner. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(ui): add Presidential Decision Room strategic priorities`.
**Advance-turn confirmations should consume Decision Room readiness, not rebuild a second checklist (2026-05-02 pre-advance command review):** The player needs to see what would be forgotten at the moment of advancing, but the modal must not become a second inbox, new blocker, or parallel history owner. The durable pattern is: project `PresidentialDecisionRoomView.advanceReadiness` into a compact confirmation view, keep `Advance Turn` on the canonical turn pipeline, and route `Review Priorities` back to Army HQ BRIEFING. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(ui): add pre-advance command review`.
**Warroom urgency affordances should be pulses, not duplicate command boards (2026-05-02 Warroom priority pulse):** The Warroom is allowed to summarize current urgency, but Army HQ owns command review. The durable pattern is: render compact counts from the Decision Room/pre-advance projection in Warroom chrome, then route clicks to Army HQ BRIEFING. Do not rebuild priority cards, queues, opportunity ledgers, or cost/history detail in the campaign shell. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(ui): add Warroom priority pulse`.
**Decision-room filters should be lenses over the card archive, not new queues (2026-05-02 Decision Room priority lenses):** Once a command board already synthesizes source truth, local filtering should derive from the finalized card list. A lens may carry counts, urgent counts, and the top card's existing navigation target, but it must not rebuild review, opportunity, SITREP, aftermath, cost, or Chronicle ownership. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(ui): add Decision Room priority lenses`.

**Pre-advance reminders must preserve the source navigation target, not collapse to one global review route (2026-05-02 pre-advance review item deep links):** A command-review reminder can summarize many source owners, but each actionable row must keep the original Decision Room `navigationTarget` so the click opens the exact owner: Inbox/Army HQ briefing, opportunity records, focused Turn Aftermath, corps briefing, or Chronicle. The broad `Review Priorities` button may still open Army HQ BRIEFING, but row actions should route through the same centralized shell helper the source board uses. This preserves the one-owner rule while making the "what will be buried if I advance?" surface playable. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(ui): add pre-advance review item deep links`.
**Warroom urgency affordances may show a compact docket, but each row must carry the original source target (2026-05-02 Warroom priority docket):** A Warroom `PRIORITIES` badge that only shows counts is legible but shallow; a full priority board inside Warroom would duplicate Army HQ / Decision Room ownership. The durable pattern is a middle layer: derive a small Warroom docket from the existing pre-advance/Decision Room readiness packet, show only the top rows and source owners, and route every action through the App-owned Decision Room navigation target. The Warroom may summarize "what needs attention before I leave this room," but the owning surfaces remain Army HQ BRIEFING, Turn Aftermath records, Chronicle, operational SITREP, and related shell helpers. Do not rebuild card synthesis in Warroom; do not add local inbox/cost/history state; do not execute decisions from the shell summary. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(ui): add Warroom priority docket`.
**Source handoffs are grouped routes over Decision Room cards, not another records system (2026-05-02 Decision Room source handoffs):** Once the Decision Room card archive has stable navigation targets, the product should answer "where should I inspect this?" by grouping cards by their existing owning surface, not by rebuilding source logic in each shell. The durable pattern is: derive `sourceHandoffs` from the already-sorted cards, group by navigation target family (`army-hq-tab`, `army-hq-records`, `army-hq-aftermath-record`, `army-hq-corps-briefing`, `chronicle`), preserve card ids in sorted card order, and keep the group's action as a route to the owning surface. Pre-advance and Warroom projections should call the same helper over their own item slice rather than synthesize a second source list. These handoffs are scan/navigation affordances only: no new inbox, records owner, cost ledger, Chronicle, event log, turn blocker, or combat-planning owner. Applied in `[2026-05-02] feat(ui): add Decision Room source handoffs`.

**Hot-path optimization Phase 0 must produce a NAMED dominating function before authorizing optimization (2026-05-04 Wave 5 bot-orders profile retry):** Wave 3's STOP-AND-ASK on bot-orders perf was correct: 562ms diffuse cost across commander loop + briefing + predictor + per-brigade evaluators with no single dominating function would have produced unsafe optimization tickets. Wave 5's instrumentation retry with proper Bash `VAR=value cmd` env-var prefix (where Wave 4 silently failed using PowerShell-style `:VAR=value`) measured per-callsite cost and found `analyzeFactionGraph` is **63.3%** of bot-orders cost, called twice per turn from two call sites with identical input. Durable rule: hot-path optimization Phase 0 must produce (a) a named function with measured % cost, AND (b) verification that the env var ACTUALLY took effect (sanity-check the audit JSON's per-callsite breakdown is non-empty before declaring "no signal"). A diffuse cost requires an instrumentation lane FIRST; an instrumentation lane that silently fails because of cross-shell syntax mistakes produces the same fake "no signal" verdict as a true diffuse cost. Once the named function is found, the next perf lane has a single-target subject and the G1+G2+G3 gate discipline (Mission C precedent) applies. Linked lane: `[2026-05-04] feat(audit): bot-orders hot-path profile retry`.

**Cross-shell env-var syntax is a binding pre-condition, not a footnote (2026-05-04 Wave 5):** Bash uses `VAR=value cmd`. PowerShell uses `$env:VAR='value'; cmd`. PowerShell-style `:VAR=value` prefix in Bash is silently ignored — the var doesn't set, the instrumentation doesn't fire, the run produces no output despite "completing" successfully and exiting 0. Wave 4's bot-orders agent hit this exact failure mode; Wave 5 succeeded by switching to proper Bash syntax. Durable rule: any cross-shell agent prompt that sets env vars MUST annotate the syntax explicitly (either `# Bash` / `# PowerShell` headers or platform-detection wrappers). When an instrumentation run reports "completed clean" but the audit JSON is empty or trivially small, the FIRST suspect is env-var syntax, not "the cost is genuinely diffuse." Linked lane: `[2026-05-04] feat(audit): bot-orders hot-path profile retry`.

**UI-only feature-flag flips are validated by descriptor inspection, not smoke runs (2026-05-04 Wave 5 Map That Scars):** A feature flag that gates a UI-only layer (deck.gl PolygonLayer in this case) and does NOT enter the sim path can be validated by extending the existing builder test suite — the validation question is "is the descriptor faction-neutral, empty-seed safe, and capability-gated?", not "does the calibration hash match?" Map That Scars T5–T8 (faction-neutral RGB, per-tier alpha, no faction coupling, capability gate) were sufficient to flip the flag default-ON without a smoke run. Durable rule: when a UI feature flag has been verified by builder tests AND the layer is composed via a capability gate that drops it on missing data, the flag-flip lane is "extend the existing test + flip the constant," not "rerun the calibration baseline." Reserve smoke runs for changes that touch sim state, scenario authoring, or any code path that flows into `final_save.json`. Linked lane: `[2026-05-04] feat(map): Map That Scars feature flag default-ON`.

**`makeAdjacency<T>` is a unifiable subset pattern; deeper test fixtures are not (2026-05-04 Wave 5 Phase 5):** N tests sharing a hand-rolled `Map<string, Set<string>>` adjacency literal but differing only in node typing CAN be refactored through a generic helper with parameterized dedupe/sort options (`makeAdjacency<T extends string>(pairs?, options?)`). 12 tests refactored cleanly with zero behavioral change. The deeper-fixture clusters (`makeOp`, `makeState`) where each test cares about a different subset of fields should NOT be unified — the resulting helper has to be either excessively flexible (loses safety) or excessively narrow (each test still hand-builds the parts it actually needs). Durable rule: identify "unifiable subset patterns" by asking whether the data shape across test sites differs only in T-parameterization or only in dedupe/sort policy. If yes, extract the helper. If tests differ in WHICH fields they populate, the helper is premature abstraction; leave the inline fixtures. Linked lane: `[2026-05-04] chore(tests): test usefulness Phase 5 — makeAdjacency helper extraction`.
